Parent Trap

by LoZfanchick

Summary

MK and Bai He attend the same camp and find out they were adopted by the same people -Macaque and Wukong. They then embark on an effort to get their divorced parents to at least be civil to each other, but lingering feelings make that harder than they imagined. However not all the lingering feelings are negative and love begins to rekindle. But throw in the fact that Wukong was and is already dating someone else and you've got a soap opera amount of drama.

 

This is a "The Parent Trap" AU it doesn't exactly follow the movie but the general gist is the same.

Notes

This is set in a modern day!AU where demons and humans coexist and stuff, no magic though. So Red Son’s hair does not burst into flames but has flame patterns, Mac still has 6 ears but no super sonic hearing, just a bit more sensitive hearing.

Chapter 1: Rivals or Siblings?

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

This was not what MK pictured his annual summer camp trip being like. He came here every year, it’s an elite camp for children of wealthy parents/guardians. He thought it’d be like every year; he and Mei (a dragon demon) arrive and meet up with a few other summer camp regulars, then they hike, swim, game, and just have their typical summer camp fun. But noooo, not this year! This year he had the misfortune of meeting Bai He.

A young girl who was new to camp this year. And ok, maybe her anger against MK was justified in the beginning. MK was a bit of a klutz and his excitement of being back at camp may have made him a bit oblivious to the damage (accidental damage) he may have caused/contributed to. It started small, he and Mei were tossing food at each other in the Mess Hall, as most campers did when they could get away with it. And MK had missed Mei a few times and those food pieces hit Bai He. He had called out a “watch out” and followed with a “sorry” each time. But he hadn’t noticed that almost all his missed throws landed on the girl or near her, making the throws seem pointed rather than accidental.

Which it was! MK didn’t mean to hit her! He didn’t even notice! And it was one time….but then MK accidentally destroyed Bai He’s arts and craft project. He wasn’t even sure what it was. He, Mei, and another regular camper -Red Son- were tossing a ball around to each other. MK had thrown the ball at where he thought Mei was, but turns out she had jumped on Red Son’s back and thus MK’s throw went rocketing towards the arts and crafts table. And it, again accidentally, destroyed Bai He and a few other campers projects. MK had apologized to them all and then ran to catch up with his friends.

Then MK had tripped in the Mess Hall food line, slipping on spilled food of some sort, and the food he had gathered had went flying and landed right on Bai He. Again an accident! But then MK had apparently ruined her big moment, which again was not his intention! Him and Red Son were heading into the sports area to try and practice fencing or the knock off challenge. MK was walking backwards teasing Red Son about last year and MK beating him. Red Son was embarrassed so he wasn’t looking at MK but off to the side. Which means no one was looking at what MK was about to run into, namely Bai He who had just bested the previous fencing champion in her age division. MK’s back had run into her, causing her to fall and unfortunately she fell right into a pile of mud just off the fencing turf from the rain the other day. He felt really REALLY bad about it but before he could even really get into his several part apologies, Bai He had run away from the crowd that had laughed at her misfortune.

Now piled up like that, yes it was bad, so yes Bai He being angry at MK was completely justified. And MK would have sadly accepted it and would have tried to work on getting her forgiveness. However, MK hadn’t really put it together that he had done all these things to the same girl, and Bai He only enlightened him as she was going off on him.

Bai He had followed him, Mei, and Red Son to the lake. There she confronted him about how he had made her camp experience terrible, that he was a bully and she wasn’t going to take it anymore. MK had been horrified and was already working on his apologies when she had crossed the line.

“Just because you’re the son of some low level celebrity doesn't mean you can treat people however you’d like!”

“Low level celebrity?!” MK had gawked, genuinely shocked and more than a little offended on his Dad’s behalf. Everyone knew who his father was! MK was in enough magazine’s and paparazzi shots for it to be known worldwide!

MK’s father was the top actor in all of stardom! He had an incredible range of acting, and had numerous awards to back it up! Plus his Dad was also an amazing director, with several of the movies he directed winning awards and recognition worldwide! And not only that, his father was loved by all types of people! Not just the movie goers, and people he helped with his many charities, and star chasers, but by the nerds of the world as well. For his Dad was also a famous game developer/beta tester. His Dad was not a low level celebrity he was THE celebrity, his Dad was Sun Wukong for God’s sake!

And just like that the battle between Bai He and himself truly began.

Bai He had then begun getting back at MK. One time in the showers she had switched his hair care for hair dye. Mei had to help him fix that. Then MK had come back from a day of canoeing to all his stuff being on the roof of his cabin. He had no idea how a little girl like her had gotten all that up there. MK and Bai He would trade barbs and jabs at each other, though MK never tried to do anything permanent or too public, still feeling a little bit bad about all the accidents in the beginning.

Then came the poker night. It was all the older campers such as himself, Mei, Red Son, and the other 16-17 year old campers staying up late and playing poker. Since they couldn’t really afford to gamble away their precious camp funds, everyone bet dares. Mei was usually the reigning poker champion, with Red Son winning a few nights as well. MK never did well at poker but still enjoyed playing. Then out of nowhere Bai He materialized. She challenged MK to a game, the winner getting to dare the other. Not one to turn down a challenge no matter how much his friends kept telling him to, MK accepted. And he had lost, badly! And with all his peers laughing at how easily he was beaten by this little girl, MK had already felt more than a little defeated. However that defeat turned to dread when Bai He first mentioned her dare.

She had wanted him to strip down and jump into the lake at night. Everyone reacted with approval, goading and ribbing MK. However MK’s heart had plummeted to his feet. As public as his life is, he -and more specifically his Dad- had kept private something till he was ready to tell the world, and that was that MK wasn’t always a boy. With a pale and terrified face he had tried to find his friends in the now suffocating crowd. Red Son had his lips between his teeth and also looked nervous for MK. MK wasn’t ashamed of being trans. It just wasn’t something he included in his introduction to people. He had a lot of his life publicized and he just wasn’t ready or willing for that to get out and for it to blow up and for him to become a figurehead, whether he wanted to be one or not and, oh he was on the verge of a panic attack.

“Actually I don’t want to see your ugly but!” Bai He’s voice rang out, cutting through the jeers and MK’s thoughts.

Wild eyed, MK focused on Bai He and more importantly Mei who was next to the little girl. His best friend then subtly mimed him to take deep breaths; obviously noticing his panicked state.

“What, so no skinny dip?” Someone in the crowd asked, disappointed.

“No.” Bai He replied calmly, she then turned to MK and tapped her one finger to her lips three times. “Instead…”

The next day MK spent the whole afternoon with the karaoke machine, singing embarrassing Disney songs to all who passed by. But he had made the best of it and even wrestled Bai He into what some would call a half hug, others an awkward chokehold; and sang “you’ve got a friend in me”.

He had hoped the pranks would end there but of course they hadn’t. And then it was taken too far. They had inadvertently gotten one of the meaner camp counselors caught in the crossfires of a rather intricate prank with ropes, syrup, and shaving cream. MK was impressed if not infuriated at the punishment he and Bai He were then forced into.

They were to move into the isolation cabin and their camp activities were heavily restricted if not all together revoked. And so here they were, both in a cabin, far away from all the other campers and forced in close confinement with each other. MK on one side and Bai He on the other.

“She shouldn’t have said that about you.” Bai He broke the silence.

“Huh?” MK, who had been pouting and glaring out the window, stewing in his anger at where he was now, turned to the young girl.

Bai He was sitting on the edge of her bed, her legs dangling over the edge as her fingers gripped and re-gripped her bed’s comforter.

“The camp counselor.” She continued, her voice small. “It wasn’t nice and it wasn’t true.” She then looked up at MK and gave him a pointed look. “You are a boy.” MK couldn't hold back the audible gasp that left him at that.

Mei had confessed after the disaster poker night she had told Bai He enough that she thought the girl may have figured it out, but wasn’t positive. Either way the little hellraiser never seemed to use him being trans against him, or try to exploit it in any way. So he had assumed she didn’t figure it out. But then as they were led to the one isolation cabin MK had tried to argue he couldn’t room with Bai He as she was a girl. The camp counselor, who had access to his medical files, just gave him the dirtiest look and said “well seeing as you’re also a girl it shouldn’t be an issue.”

It was part of the reason he immediately went to his bed to pout angrily. He hated people like that bitch, but starting a fight or provoking them would just draw more attention and he wasn’t ready for that yet. But it didn’t make it easier to swallow.

“Thanks.” MK literally squeaked out, his throat tight with emotion at his enemy’s' easy acceptance of his gender.

Bai He gave a nod then glanced around the room and pulled her knees up to herself, hugging them. It then crashed down on MK, she recognized him as a boy, an older boy. And he was sharing a room with her, a young girl. Panicked MK jumped out of bed and began pacing about the cabin.

“Oh don’t worry though! If you feel uncomfortable we can draw a line through the cabin! I’ll stay on my side and I’ll never cross over to yours. Or, or I can move my bed maybe even further from yours? I’m not sure if they’re bolted down or not but I can try! Or-”

“MK!” Bai He cut in, standing in front of the pacing boy, who lept back like she was on fire.

“I’m not worried, you're not that type of guy. I’ve seen how you interact with your friends enough to know you're not a bad guy.”

“PHEW!” MK literally deflated but then pointed a stern finger at the girl. “BUT! If I make you uncomfortable in any way or you want me to just give you space, just say the word! Even if it’s raining I’ll go outside or jump in the closet or something! Deal?” He held his hands out to the girl who smiled and shook it.

“Deal.”

“Good!” MK beamed and then went to go back to sitting on his bed.

“Are you going to have your Dad go after the camp counselor?” Bai He asked, as she followed MK.

“Huh?” Mk tilted his head in confusion as the girl sat on the bed next to him.

“For using your private medical information against you. My Baba would say go after them. They all have to sign an NDA and shouldn't parade that knowledge around like she did.”

“I doubt she’ll bring it up in public….it’s actually not the first time she’s said something to me about it.” MK muttered. “She’s always had a problem with me.”

“Then you really should have your Dad go after her.” Bai He said seriously.

“No…It’s just her, and if I avoid her it’s easier than being known as the kid who got a camp counselor fired.”

The little girl pouted at him and made a tsk-ing sound. “You should still say something. She’s being a bully.”

MK smiled and tilted his head at the young girl next to him. “I thought I was the bully?” He teased.

Bai He glared at him and pouted again. “You were -are! Argh shut up!” She shoved the brunette man’s arm who laughed at the young girl's frustration.

“But you said I was a good guy, right? Right?” He continued to tease as he poked the young raven haired girl's arm.

“You- argh!” She got up and stormed over to her bed. This made MK’s face fall, fearing he pushed too hard and offended the young girl.

“Hey I’m-”

“You were a bully!” Bai He said, not looking at MK but at the floor as she stood by her bed, fists clenched. “I wanted to fit in and make friends but no one wanted to be near me cause everyone thought you didn’t like me. And they’d rather be on your side than mine.”

“Bai He…” MK whispered, “I’m so sorry. Really I am! I never meant for that to happen.”

The young girl only threw him a glare then went back to focusing on the floor.

“So I thought I’ll be your enemy and maybe people who don’t like you will at least want to be my friends but…” She sighed and sagged onto her bed, looking tired and upset.

Slowly, cautiously, MK got up and went to squat on the floor next to Bai He’s bed; by her side, so if she wanted she could easily glance over at him.

“I don’t want people like that as my friends.” She confessed quietly. “...I’m not good at making friends.”

“And I didn’t help matters.” They fell into a silence till MK broke it again. “Bai He I’m so sorry for making your first camp visit terrible. But you know what?” He smiled, trying to catch the girl's eyes, and once he did he continued. “You did make some friends.” The raven haired girl looked both hopeful and doubtful. MK beamed and gestured to himself. “Me, Mei, and Red Son.”

Bai He’s face dropped into confusion and screamed doubt.

“Mei loved your pranks against me, she was really impressed with the furniture on the roof one!” MK boasted, trying to clean Bai He of doubt. “Red Son was won over by the hair dye prank. He wanted me to keep it. Said I deserved it for being a noodle brain to you in the first place.” Bai He let out a giggle at the insult and MK beamed. “And I sang you our friendship song, remember?” His eyes twinkled with mirth as he jumped up and posed in front of the young girl. He then began to sing a few lyrics from the song You Got a Friend in Me. Bai He laughed throughout the whole thing, which only encouraged MK to continue.

Once MK was done, Bai He was laughing loudly and MK was also giggling at himself. MK then joined her on her bed and the two shared a smile.

“You have a terrible singing voice.” The raven teased. MK only huffed a laugh in agreement.

“Well my Dad is great at a lot of stuff but singing isn’t one of them.”

“Opposite of my Baba.”

“Who is your Dad?” MK asked, curious as he realized he never heard of who the girl's parents are.

“His name is Macaque,” Bai He beamed, the biggest smile MK has seen on her the whole summer. “He’s a big name for live theater! We’ve traveled to London, Australia, and Greece!”

“Woah! Is your Mom in live theater as well?”

The raven girl simply shook her head. “No, I was adopted as a baby. It’s just me and Baba.” She beamed then jolted and quickly added “And our butler Tang! He’s great! A little boring at times,” She allowed. “But he tells fun stories.”

“I have a butler too!” MK piped in immediately. “Though I guess that’s not surprising.” He rubbed the back of his head sheepishly. Since this was a camp for wealthy children it made sense most of them would have butlers or nannies.

“What’s their name?” The young girl encouraged.

“Pigsy! He’s actually great at singing but doesn’t do it in public, no idea why, he’d be great at it.” MK mused then shook his head and continued. “He makes the best food!”

“Lucky!” The young girl interrupted jealousy coloring her tone. “Tang loves food but can’t cook and Baba is ok at it but not great. We usually eat out alot.”

“Pigsy is impossible to take out!” MK groaned. “He’s so picky about where we go, cause his standards are so high for food. One time I brought home some fast food I picked up while driving and he made me throw it away saying it was garbage.” MK wiped a fake tear from his eye as Bai He laughed.

“Want to know something?” Bai He whispered as if she was about to impart some grand secret.

“Absolutely!” MK whispered back.

“I think my Baba is a fan of your Dad.” She whispered.

MK only blinked. It wasn’t strange for him to hear this, most people were fans of his Dad. However Bai He was acting as if she was imparting a great secret so MK went along with it.

“Oh?”

The young raven nodded her head and then went over to her bags and began going thru them, appearing to look for something.

“Sometimes I’ll see him stare at pictures of Sun Wukong.” MK made an awkward face at the young girl's back, grateful she didn’t see it, but he couldn’t help his reaction at that piece of info.

“And he has this!” She triumphantly pulled something from her bag. Hurrying over she climbed back on her bed to show the item to MK.

It was a picture of his Dad. Though it was from when his Dad was young, also the picture was torn, frayed in the middle from the messy tear. As if in anger or sadness someone took the previously whole picture and ripped it in two. Sun Wukong was smiling and appeared to have been laughing at something. Around his one bicep, closest to the tear was a black haired arm, tenderly gripping the laughing monkey.

MK gasped as he looked the picture over, vaguely hearing Bai He’s explanation about having the photo.

“I thought if I had a rare picture of Sun Wukong people would want to talk with me. But when I heard you were here I figured it would be weird to pull that out and brag about it. And I know I called your Dad a small celebrity, but I was mad at you! Everyone knows Sun Wukong!” Finally the young girl noticed, MK seemed entranced by the photo. “....MK?”

Said man blinked then glanced at the raven only to turn back to the photo. Almost as if in a trance he began to mutter.

“I was also adopted, I mean obviously as I’m not a monkey demon. But I remember….vaguely having two dads. But Dad and everyone never mentions anything about it, even when I ask or beg for answers…But I know I remember another Dad. I don’t remember much, really all I remember is the color black….” He kept looking at the hand on Wukong’s bicep.

Gulping MK slowly got up and went into his stuff, taking the photo with him. Meanwhile the young girl said nothing, allowing MK the time to look through his belongings.

Finally MK pulled something out with a shaky breath. Yet he didn’t turn around to show Bai He, just stared down at Bai He’s picture and presumably the item he was looking for.

“Finally I got my Dad’s Manager, Tripitaka, to give me something. He literally only gave me this photo and said nothing more about it.” MK then rejoined Bai He on her bed and reverently held out a half torn photo. “…I think this was my other Dad.”

Both photo pieces matched up to make a whole. In the photo MK had, there was a black furred monkey demon that was also smiling, though more subdued than Wukong’s smile. Now it was Bai He’s turn to gasp as she looked the now complete photo over.

“That’s my Baba.” She whispered.

MK eyes immediately met hers as they both gawked at each other.

-

“Sooo your siblings…maybe?” Mei asked, skepticism wreaking from her voice as she sipped on her juice.

Mei and Red Son had sneaked into the isolation cabin at MK’s coded request that he managed to give them in the Mess Hall, and were now staring at the 17 and 11 year old who nodded in excitement. MK and Bai He were beaming at the two other campers. Red Son and Mei shared a skeptic look.

“That’s quite the leap in logic.” Red Son deadpanned.

“BUT it makes sense!” MK once again pointed to the now taped together photo of Macaque and Wukong.

“Noodle-brain one photo does not mean you two are siblings!” The redhead fired back.

“No but a copy of our medical records says we were both adopted from the same agency, and both have 2 names on the adoption papers.” Bai He said proudly and beamed at MK who chimed in with the two names.

“Sun Wukong and Six-eared Macaque!”

“NO WAY!” Mei exploded, leaping over to MK and Bai He, her face beaming at the discovery.

“Yes way!” MK cheered, and then was swept up in Mei lifting him off the ground and jumping up and down in excitement.

“Oh my God you have a little sister! And she’s so cute! And devious!”

“Wait a minute!” Red Son yelled, halting MK and Mei’s celebration. “How on earth did you figure that out?!”

“Bai He snuck into the Counselor’s office.” MK made jazz hands over to the young girl who tilted her head and gave a seemingly innocent smile.

“You have to be very silent when backstage in the theater.” She said as an explanation for her ability to sneak into the counselor’s office and access restricted files.

“Dude!” Mei punched MK’s shoulder. “Why didn’t you ever look up your adoption papers?! You were always telling me about remembering another Dad!”

“Cause I didn’t think of it!” MK whined as he rubbed his shoulder.

“Holy shit.” Red Son gapped. “So you two are really siblings, like brother and sister.”

“Yup!” MK and Bai He said at the same time, and MK reached over to wrap one arm around his younger sister.

Chapter End Notes

And it starts! Sorry it was rushed near the end but I just want to get the basic set up down so we can jump into the shadowpeach.

Chapter 2: Meet and Greet

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

For the rest of the duration of the camp MK and Bai He were inseparable. And seeing as the two had made up, they were released from the isolation cabin by the Head Counselor. MK helped Bai He socialize with the other campers and the two would often hang out with Red Son and Mei. Finally the end of camp was drawing near and Red Son had just gotten off the phone in the Office.

“Were you able to do it?” MK asked eagerly, practically bouncing in place. Next to the brunette was his little sister, looking nervous.

“Who do you think you are talking to?!” Red Son fired back, then regained his composure and with a haughty look he puffed out his chest “Did you forget my Mother owns the airline? Of course I was able to do it!”

“YES!” Bai He cheered and was quickly scooped up by Mei who jumped up and down with the young girl. MK, meanwhile, jumped on a now blushing Red Son and cheered in victory as he hung off the redhead.

“Thank you, thank you, THANK YOU!” MK cheered into the redhead's bright red face as he kept clinging to the bull boy.

“Oh Bai He, we are going to have so much fun!” Mei cheered as she continued to bounce the young girl up and down.

“Mei!” MK gasped as he finally released Red Son and whipped around to his best friend. “You can’t take Bai He on your motorcycle, she’s too young.”

“I know that!” Mei stuck her tongue out at the brunette. “We’ll take a taxi from the airport you dork.” She then leaned closer to the young raven and whispered conspiratorially. “Look who's being a protective big brother.” Making the girl giggle.

“Yes well.” Red Son finally straightened himself out and cleared his throat. “Bai He and Mei will ride together to California, and MK you will be flying to New York. You are all welcome.”

“Thank you Red Son!” Bai He added, as Mei rolled her eyes at the red head.

Then a few days later they all departed camp with promises to call and write to each other as they did each year. Bai He and MK gave each other huge hugs and promised to call as soon as they were at the other's respective house and give them an update about how the meeting went. Then with that they all departed.

Mei and Bai He landed and as promised Mei got them a taxi to take the young girl to Wukong’s villa. The super star lived in his own little island of paradise with how much land he owned and the gates surrounding said land. It was abundant in peach and other assorted fruit trees, though peaches were the most prevalent. Then the mansion itself was massive, almost like a palace.

“It’s so big!” Bai He gasped as they drove up the long and winding driveway, getting closer and closer to the giant house.

“Right?” Mei said.

In all honesty Mei was nervous for Bai He and MK. MK and Bai He seemed to only be thinking of the positives of meeting their other Father. Meanwhile Red Son and herself had privately talked about how worried they were for the fallout. After all there had to be a reason neither kid knew of the other parent, so who knows what showing up like this would cause or how the Father’s would react. Let alone what it would be like when they had to eventually switch back.

MK had never heard of the Six Eared Macaque, not one word of him. Wukong and him must have split up and it must have been bad to not want any reminders of the man he once adopted 2 children with. Bai He mentioned that whenever Wukong was on TV or mentioned in passing, the man would scoff and say how he was overrated. The girl chalked it up to not liking the celebrity, as Macaque apparently had a number of celebrities he didn’t hide his disdain for, so she just assumed Wukong was one of them. But then she’d catch him staring at the photo she had taken, or looking at a picture of him on a passing magazine with an odd expression before shaking it away.

So Mei was not sure how any of this was going to go, but she wouldn’t leave MK’s younger sister alone, and she hoped MK was doing ok on his end.

Finally they arrived and with an excited breath Mei grabbed Bai He’s hand and guided the girl inside. As soon as Mei pushed the door open, streamers, confetti, and balloons were released in the two girls' faces.

“WELCOME HOME!” Several voices yelled.

Mei laughed to herself, she should have expected something like this, but poor Bai He seemed scared at the sudden onslaught. And she wasn’t the only one struck dumb. The 3 men who had just yelled in their faces now looked at the two girls in confusion.

Bai He tightened her grip on Mei’s hand and swallowed thickly as she looked everything over. There was Pigsy, who she recognized from MK’s description of his pig demon butler. Then next to him was Sandy, a truly giant blue water demon, who was holding up a sign that read “Welcome Back!”. She vaguely remembered MK mentioning him. And then there was Sun Wukong. The monkey was dressed in casual clothes, nothing like what the young girl had seen glimpses of on TV and in magazines. He looked perplexed as he looked from Mei to Bai He.

“Mei? Who’s this?” Wukong asked as he crossed his arms in confusion.

“Where’s MK?” Pigsy demanded, his joyous tone replaced with clear frustration.

“Ah well the thing is…” Mei began but then stopped and bit her lip as she tried to think of how to explain this. “See at camp….well..”

Wukong’s face became more and more anxious as he waited to hear about who this new girl was in his home and where his son was. If anything happened to MK, they would have called him. So he should be fine, but then where was he? And why was Mei dodging the question?

Finally the young girl stepped forward, closer to Wukong and let go of Mei’s hand.

“M-my name is Bai He.” All three men gasped and Wukong immediately took a step towards her. “And I came here to meet my other Father.”

“Bai He?” Wukong gasped as he slowly came closer to the young, reserved girl who nodded at him.

“You and Baba must think alike since MK and I ended up at the same camp.” She rambled, twiddling her fingers together. “And I wanted to meet you and MK wanted to meet Baba so…” By that point Wukong had knelt in front of the young girl and raked a clawed hand through her raven locks.

“You’re so grown up!” Wukong gasped, as tears gathered in his eyes. “Oh look at you!” He then cupped her face with both hands as the young girl’s eyes welled with tears. “You’re so beautiful! And so big!” She let out a wet giggle at that.

Behind them Pigsy choked on air as he was supporting a crying Sandy. “Wukong! You can’t tell a girl that!”

“I don’t mind!” Bai He choked out as she fell into Wukong’s arms. Said monkey’s arms quickly wrapped around the young girl and hoisted her up and held her tight.

-

“I am just making sure-”

“Red!” MK interrupted his friend, turning on him and thus stopping the redhead in his tracks. “I can do this.”

MK had been shocked when Red Son joined him on his flight, The redhead saying he was also going to New York, even though MK knew the bull demon’s family was in Colorado. However Red Son explained he was staying and doing some errands for his family and thus why he was flying with MK. However, throughout the flight the redhead kept fussing and asking MK if he was really sure this was a good idea. Then as they departed the plane the red head continued to follow MK around the airport. Red Son had then announced he planned on going with MK to this Six Eared Macaque’s home, which is where MK put his foot down.

“I get you and Mei are worried about me and Bai He. I’m not an idiot, I noticed the looks you guys had, but….I have to do this.” The brunette stressed.

Red Son let out a heavy huff of air as he took in MK’s look of determination. He had known the boy long enough to know when he wasn’t getting anywhere.

“Very well. BUT!” He held up a hand and pointed at the brunette. “I’ll be at the Hilton near Central Park, where my family usually stays.” He dug through his pockets and produced a Hilton business card. “Call me as soon as you can, or if anything goes wrong you can come over and stay in my room.” MK blushed at the offer but Red Son was all business as he made sure MK kept a tight grip on the business card.

“Th-” MK’s voice squeaked so he coughed to compose himself and then tried again. “Thanks.”

Red Son nodded decisively then indicated toward the airport exit. “Shall we?”

“Yeah!” MK smiled then the two made their way towards the exit. “Oh wait, when I call the hotel would I just ask for your room or just give your name?”

“Just give my name. I’m not sure my Family’s usual suite will be available, but they should connect you if you tell them you’re calling for me.”

MK smiled secretly at the indication that Red Son really did just come to New York for him. But dismissed the thought for now, he had bigger things to worry about.

“There needs to be a way to call people directly no matter where they are.” Red Son mused.

“Like a car phone that’s always on you?” MK mused as the two made it over to the taxi line.

Red Son nodded then closed his eyes as he began developing the idea. “Yes, a phone you can carry and always use. No phone lines needed.”

MK huffed out a laugh at the impossible thought. “That would be awesome.” He then gave the redhead a soft look. “If anyone could create such a thing, it’d be you.”

Red Son’s eyes shot open as his face turned bright red. He then fumbled for his words for a few seconds while MK smiled at his flustered friend. Soon enough MK’s bags were loaded into the taxi and he gave the red head a tight hug as he departed to meet his estranged Father.

The taxi took MK to a highrise building that had gated trees planted in front of it. Excited and full of nerves MK ran up to the door and used the key Bai He had given him to enter the building. He then entered the elevator and used the other key Bai He gave him to access the top floor of the building, which was all owned by Macaque. Once arriving at the top. He once again took a breath to steady his nerves and went to ring the doorbell that was just outside the small hallway in front of the elevator.

Inside he vaguely heard a rush of movement then the door was sprung open by a middle aged man wearing glasses.

“Bai He I forgot about your flight I’m so-….you’re not Bai He.” The man rushed out, seemingly in a panic over thinking he missed picking the young girl up, only to then notice that he was not talking to said girl. MK let out a small smile, knowing from Bai He’s description that this must be her butler Tang.

The man’s face dropped into one of pure shock as he looked MK over. But then quickly pushed the door open more and waved for the young brunette to enter. “Co-come in!” Then under his breath he let out in a whisper “oh my God it’s him.”

“Thanks.” MK smiled as he walked inside the entryway. He noticed the shoes neatly lined up on a shoe rack so he began to stop and take his off, not wanting to come off as rude. “I’m-”

“I know!” Tang interrupted with a jittery smile but then rubbed the bridge of his nose, above his glasses as MK jumped. “Sorry, sorry! No I-I know!” He then held up his arms and made a wait right there motion. “I’ll be right back, wait here!” he was already walking away when he turned back and said “Wait right here. One second!” Then the man was gone around a corner.

“O..ok.” MK replied weakly to the retreating man. MK then stuffed a hand into his coat pocket and gripped the card Red Son had given him. Finding the reassurance that a friend was still near, giving him strength.

Suddenly MK heard a crash, jolting him out of his head. That was rapidly followed by footsteps quickly coming towards him. MK shook out his arms and tried to again prepare himself and then there he was, the black figment from fragmented memories. Standing at the end of the hallway, frozen and just staring at MK.

The demon monkey was in a daytime robe, maybe having just gotten up or having a lazy day. His expression appeared both fragile and closed off, switching between the two as he kept staring at the nervous brunette. Finally he broke the standoff by asking, in a very wobbly voice.

“Wh..what are you doing here?”

The question jolted MK to his core. He wished he still had a hold of the business card but instead flexed his hand as he tried to smile.

“I…wanted to meet you…again.” Macaque’s expression flitted to fragile at the last word, giving MK the courage to continue. “I remember a black hand, and someone appearing almost out of the shadows coming by my bed.”

“When you had nightmares.” Macaque added as he began walking towards the teenager.

“Was that it?” MK let out a wet breath, unaware that he had tears in his eyes. “I don’t-don’t really remember but…I remember you. And I-I wanted, no needed to meet you and-”

“Oh MK!” Macaque let out in a tight voice, as the black furred monkey was clearly trying his best to reign in his emotions. “I missed you!”

MK ran to the monkey who enveloped the teenager in his arms and clutched him close.

-

“Eat! You’re too skinny!” Pigsy barked, but softened his tone at the jolt of fear the young girl he just served gave. Wukong glared death at the pig demon, who rubbed the back of his head sheepishly.

“Don’t mind him Bai!” Mei cut in easily, throwing her arm around the chair the young girl sat in, before taking the seat next to her. The teenager continued to explain. “He sounds all tough but he’s just a big softy with a loud voice.”

“I am not!” Pigsy squawked at the same time Sandy and Wukong chimed in.

“Agreed.”
“Yeah, he’s all bluster.”

“Oh I’ll show you bluster monkey!” Pigsy muttered holding out his wooden spoon in a threatening manner. Wukong only stuck his tongue out at the man, causing Bai He to relax again.

Pigsy looked back to Bai He and added in a softer tone. “If you don’t like anything just let me know, I won’t be offended.”

“No!” the young girl exclaimed as she held up her hands as if to physically stop him from taking any of the many dishes the chef had placed on the table. “I’ve never seen such yummy homemade food!” Wukong snorted at that, but Bai He didn’t notice as she was too busy looking around the table again at all the different dishes. “MK said you made delicious food and I can't wait to try some!”

“Well let’s not wait any longer then.” Sandy cheered with a smile and the young girl nodded in agreement before putting some items on her plate, Mei, Sandy and Wukong doing the same.

However just as Bai He was about to eat a spoonful of dumpling soup, Wukong quickly shoved a hand out and grabbed the spoon.

“WAIT!” He screamed, startling the whole table, and Pigsy who was leaning against the kitchen counter. Frantically the Father turned to Bai He, “Are you allergic to any foods? Do you know if you are?!”

“Oh shit.” Pigsy let out, forgetting to even think of asking that. He had already been working on most of this food as a welcome home feast for MK, but then cooked up some more dishes to get out the excess energy of little Bai He’s return to them. In his anxiety coping cooking, he never thought if the young baby he once met developed any food allergies.

The startled raven girl thought about it but couldn't recall any allergies. “No, I’m not allergic to anything but I don’t like asparagus.”

Pigsy deflated in relief, knowing he didn't almost poison his little niece. He then decided to take on the challenge of the asparagus. “You just haven't had it cooked properly. Leave it to me, you’re going to love it in no time.”

Bai He’s face wrinkled at the thought of eating the food she didn’t like but Wukong laughed and then patted her head. They then began eating again, during the meal they talked about camp and how MK and Bai He met.

At the news of the prank war, Wukong fell out of his chair laughing. Meanwhile Sandy looked pensive at the young girl, however when she glanced at the blue giant he quickly switched to a calming smile. Wukong was delighted and kept asking about the pranks and how she accomplished them, and her skills at poker. At which point Mei jumped in and said she still wanted to play against Bai He. The talk was only interrupted when the phone rang and Pigsy, who went to answer it, pulled Wukong away saying it was for him.

“We have to call MK and Baba.” Bai He said to Mei who was slurping down the last of her noodles she had on her plate.

“We’ll do it later.” She promised.

“Is Baba what you call Macaque?” Sandy asked, and the young girl nodded. “We should let him know you are safe, he must be worried.”

“And we need to make sure MK’s ok.” Pigsy added as he sat with the group. “I can’t even imagine the trouble that boy will get into in New York City.”

-

“And everything went over smoothly?” Red Son asked anxiously.

“Yeah!” MK’s tone was one of pure happiness as he unpacked his bags. “Apparently he’s been keeping track of me through all the paparazzi stuff I’ve been in so he knows a lot about me!” He boasted, he then dropped into a whisper and clutched the phone close. “He never even batted an eye about me being a guy! It’s been so great!”

“I’m so happy for you.” Red Son said genuinely. He was nervous for his friend to come out to this mystery father, and relieved wasn’t enough of a word to describe how happy he was that it went so smoothly for the brunette.

“He’s been keeping tabs on me Red Son! He’s cared about me this whole time! I can’t…I really can’t believe it…” He let out a pleased sigh as he flopped onto the bed, still clutching the phone. Red Son smiled at his friend's tone and happiness.

“And now he’s going to take me around the city! And he said in a few days we can go to Broadway and he can show me backstage!” He jumped off the bed and began unpacking again. “I’m so excited!”

“I bet, just don’t fall into too many tourist traps.”

“Please! Who do you think you’re talking to?”

“...The boy who will go to all the tourist spots and fall into all the tourist traps.”

“Hey!”

“MK are you still unpacking? Do you need any help?” Tang called from the other side of his bedroom door. Luckily, Macaque had a spare bedroom so MK didn’t have to commandeer Bai He’s bedroom.

“No I got it!” The brunette yelled back then quickly said his goodbye to the red head before hanging up and quickly finishing up.

Soon enough MK was out of the spare bedroom and followed Tang to the living room where Macaque was.

“All settled in?”

“Yup!” MK boasted with a smile. “All unpacked and I called Red Son to let him know I’m ok.”

“He’s a good friend.” Tang intoned, “Following you to New York to ensure everything went smoothly.”

“Iron Fan raised him right.” Macaque agreed, and MK boggled at him.

“Wait! You know Red’s mom?!”

Macaque smiled sadly, “I did, though it’s been a long time since I spoke with her.”

“Oh, what happened?” MK asked curiously, but Macaque brushed it off with a grin and wrapped an arm around his son’s shoulders.

“Do you want to talk about that or do you want to see all that New York has to offer?” As he spoke he began guiding MK towards the door, with Tang at their heels.

“Can we go to the MET?” Tang pleaded.

“Like the baseball team?” MK jumped in excited, glancing at Tang over his shoulder. “Don’t they not play right now?”

Macaque laughed as Tang’s face fell. “He means the museum MK.” MK looked chastised but Macaque just ruffled his hair. “We’ll go wherever you want to go.” At that MK beamed.

It’s fair to say Macaque was not prepared for going wherever MK wanted to go. Every tourist hot spot was hit, except the Statue of Liberty since they missed the boat and didn’t want to wait for the next one. Tang had passed out the moment they came back home, barely making it to his bedroom and complaining about his poor feet the entire time. MK was overjoyed at everything he saw, all the pictures he took, and all the stuff Macaque had bought him. He got a few New York shirts (that no true New Yorker would wear), a cheap folding fan, A NYC snow globe, a Disney themed statue of liberty figurine, and many more items.

Macaque didn’t mind, he was happy to be able to spoil his son. His son! It felt so good to see him, in person and not through tabloid clippings and side shots. He could hardly believe it, and kept his tail wrapped around the teenager throughout the day to prove to himself that MK was actually here. So yes he was exhausted and had stood in more lines today than he had in the past 3 years. But it was worth it.

MK was putting his things in his room and Macaque had just sat down when the phone rang. Letting out a loud and annoyed groan Macaque got up and answered the phone, swearing vengeance on whoever called just as he got off his feet.

“Hello?” He clipped out.

“Baba?”

“Bai He!” Macaque’s tone immediately shifted into concern as he cradled the phone with both hands, suddenly clutching it like a lifeline. “Are you alright? Is everything ok? How are you?”

“I’m fine!” Bai He practically chirped back. “I had the most amazing home cooked food! Baba you have to try some! It was so yummy!”

“Oh really?” Macaque inquired as he pulled the phone and base towards the couch. Mentally he thought, ‘Pigsy must still be working for Wukong.’

“Yes! I had some steamed crab for dinner and Baba it was SO good!”

He could practically feel his daughter's happiness and excitement at the food, and he tried not to take it personally. Afterall, it wasn’t like Wukong cooked it, Wukong did just what he did, threw money at someone to cook. Wukong just happened to have a great chef on retainer.

“That sounds great sweetie!” He then switched back to his concerns. “So how are you? Are you comfortable? Do you need me to come get you?”

“No!” Bai he shouted, making Macque flinch at the immediate rejection. “No Baba, I'm having a great time! We’re going to make a fort and watch movies with everyone.”

“OK, if you’re sure.” He gave in, trying not to sound too sullen.

“Is…have you met um,” The young girl tried and Macaque smiled softly as he stared off to where MK’s room was.

“Yes MK is here. We just got back home. We had quite the adventure ourselves today.” He boasted.

“What did you do?”

“Hey Macaque -oh sorry I didn’t know you were on the phone.” MK said as he walked into the living room, jolting in the entryway.

“It’s Bai He.” The black furred monkey held out the phone. “Want to tell her about what we did today?”

“Yeah!” MK cheered, then practically jumped onto the couch next to Macaque and began telling Bai He all about his day. In return Bai He told her brother about her day and both siblings celebrated that everything went so well.

“Is that MK?” Wukong asked, he had just popped over to check on Bai He as she had been on the phone for a while.

She nodded. “Hold on MK, want to talk with Wukong?” Said monkey perked up and hopped over to the phone, ready to take it when offered.

“Dad!”

“Heya Bud! How are you? Having-” He didn’t even get to finish his sentence before MK starts going off about the fun day he had. As his son went on and on about how much fun he had, running around NYC Wukong tried to keep his jealousy low.

So he didn’t take Bai He out on the town, it was his first day with her! He wanted her all to himself, tomorrow he’d take her out. In disguise of course. But just because Macaque took MK out on the first day, and tried to win him over with gifts didn’t mean anything. He could give Bai He gifts too!

“That sounds great buddy! Sounds like you’re having a great time.” He smiled.

“I really am Dad.” MK breathed out, sounding relaxed and happy. And again Wukong tried not to be jealous or negative about this.

“Well that’s great, but when do you think you’ll be coming-”

“Oh I gotta go Dad!” MK cut him off. “Mac’s got the popcorn ready!”

“Oh ok, bye buddy! Love you!”

“Love you too!”

-

Bai He was having a great time! Wukong was so open and welcoming. Not to say her Baba wasn’t, but Wukong was more…approachable. He had an air about him that invited the girl to talk about anything and everything. Like now, when they were going over the pranking war at camp, her and Wukong then talked about some of the friends she made at camp. And unlike with her Baba, she immediately began telling Wukong how she was having a hard time making and keeping friends, and was nervous about losing these new friends she made. Wukong had given her an understanding look and told her how it’s not about the quantity of friends one has but the quality, and to get good friends and keep them you had to be a good friend in return.

“Check in with them. Send them a letter or give them a call just to talk and check in every once in a while.” Wukong advised.

“Is that what you do?” She asked curious, the monkey’s face fell for a moment before he gave a tight lipped smile. “It’s what I do now. Truthfully,” He gave the girl a conspiratorial smile. “I also wasn't that great at keeping friends when I was younger.”

“Really?!” She gasped in shock, after all everyone loved Sun Wukong so to think of him having trouble with friendship was unbelievable.

“Yup!” He smiled. “I kept a few friends, well actually one friend, from my younger years, but now I know you have to work on keeping friendships.” He wrapped the girl in a hug and gave her a squeeze. “So if you need any advice you just ask your old Dad.” He grinned.

“Thanks!” She beamed and Wukong felt like heaven had smiled down upon him.

Then his curiosity got the better of him. “What did Mac tell you when you brought this up with him?”

“Oh.” Bai He began playing with a strand of her hair as she looked away. “Actually I never told him.” She muttered under her breath. Wukong blinked at her and gave her time to explain. “It’s…harder to bring this stuff up with Baba. I mean I love him!” She rushed as if to defend the man. “But it’s difficult telling him about some stuff.” She finished under her breath.

Wukong felt a kinship with Macaque in that moment, though he’d deny it. It was hard seeing something wrong with your child but not being able to be the one they go to for help or guidance.

“I get it.” He sighed. “MK’s like that with me.”

“Really?”

“Yeah, sometimes he’s more comfortable talking about things with Sandy, and that’s…fine.” He tried to sound as if it was, but it was difficult. He knew Sandy was great for MK and really helped him. He just wished MK would rely on him more, so he was happy his daughter could talk with him.

“Sandy? Why him?”

“He used to be a therapist. He still moonlights as one at some trauma centers but mainly he runs a cat cafe not too far from here.” The monkey explained. “If you’d like you can talk with him as well.”

The young girl smiled at the offer. “Maybe, but I like talking with you.” And once again Wukong felt like he was bestowed a gift.

Then the two arrived at the stable that Wukong owned, he only had 2 horses, his and MK’s but Bai He squealed over the animals in joy. Evidently Wukong was a good rider and was very good with horses. At first he had her ride with him but then he got her to ride a small circle on her own.

“You’re doing great kiddo!”

“I’m not hurting her right?” Bai He asked, still nervous about being so high and having her full weight on the horse.

“Nah, you’re like a hat to her, and with you it’s more of a flower since you’re so little.” He smiled as he watched Bai He ride.

The two stayed out riding till Wukong decided to ride Bai He and himself back to the house for lunch. He’d ride and board his horse afterwards. However as they walked in Pigsy gave the girl a smile and him a stink eye.

“Go wash up and I’ll serve you some grub.” Pigsy said to the girl who nodded and quickly ran to the nearest restroom.

“What did I do now?” Wukong asked, already exasperated.

“He called again.” Pigsy grumbled.

“What? I told him I’d call him when the time was right.”

“Well he’s a pushy bitch.”

“Hey!” Wukong snapped angrily, but the pig demon only shrugged and walked back towards the kitchen ignoring the angry monkey he left behind. Said monkey ran a hand through his fur then went to the nearest phone.

-

MK and Macaque were having a calm day in. They both sat on the couch and Macaque had brought out the books he had filled with clippings and magazine shots/blurbs of MK throughout the years. Tang had been with them but then left to go pick up their lunch.

“Ugh I remember hating this picture!” MK groaned as he looked at a paparazzi shot that was snapped of him and his Dad exiting a clothing store. In the picture Wukong was carrying several bags of clothes, same with MK. Wukong was holding the door open for the young preteen MK. Meanwhile MK appeared to be talking a mile a minute as he stared starry eyed at the bags he was carrying.

“Why? I think you look great in it.” Macaque commented pointing at MK’s starry eyed expression.

“I got teased so much because of all the clothes I bought.” MK grumbled and shivered at the memory.

“Ah,” the dark monkey nodded, “Yes I suppose children can be cruel. I hadn’t thought of that.”

They continued looking through the book and MK noticed that in the beginning of these books, whenever his Dad was in a photo with him, he had been cut out or mostly cut out so the photos just showed MK. However as more time went, and the deeper into these books they went, the less and less was his Dad cut out or removed.

“I can’t believe you kept these.” MK said in awe.

Macaque shrugged and put a reverent hand over the page and pictures. “I…I wanted to keep up with you…I missed you everyday.” He confessed and MK leaned into the monkey. “And, as idiotic and selfish as it was for him to expose you to the press.” Macaque growled his finger pressing down harshly over Wukong’s face. For a moment they sat in tense silence as Mac pushed on the picture and MK’s eyes went from the picture to Macaque, worriedly. Finally the dark monkey released the pressure and let out a huff of air. “It did make it easier for me to keep up with what was going on in your life.”

MK let out an awkward laugh and then coughed into his hand. “So, uh, I’m surprised Bai He never found these.”

Macaque let out a weary sigh as he ran a hand through his hair. “I…kept them hidden from her. I’d have Tang buy the magazines for me and keep them in his room, since she doesn’t go in there, till I could find time to…” He gestured at the books.

“....And you never thought to tell her about me?” MK didn’t want to say but couldn’t stop himself from asking. Still in disbelief that Macaque cared about him enough to try and keep up with his life as much as he could, but not enough to ever mention or come see him.

Macaque looked at MK’s hurt expression and quickly looked away. He opened his mouth then closed it, then after a few seconds Macaque spoke.

“Did Wukong ever mention anything about Bai He? Or me?” He looked over to MK, who looked away and bit his lip.

“....” The silence between the two stretched on till MK broke it with a huge and heavy intake of air. “Can we, even after me and Bai He switch back, can we still…talk?”

“YES!” Macaque practically shouted, seizing the brunette’s shoulders. “MK,” He cupped his face with one hand and smiled when MK leaned into it. “I’ve missed you everyday, every single day. Now,” He smiled “I don’t care the distance, or any other bullshit, but I am not letting you go again! That’s a promise!”

“Aw Baba!” MK cried as he threw himself into Macaque’s arms.

That night, MK and Bai He had another call with each other where they each talked about their time with their other father. When MK told Bai He about their Baba’s vow to not let things go back to how they were before, the young girl sounded ecstatic. However it was subdued when she asked MK if he thought Wukong would allow or vow the same. Afterall she hadn’t asked Wukong anything like that or even brought up what would happen when she had to go back home.

And unfortunately MK wasn’t sure what Wukong would say or do. Afterall, he had begged his father for validation in his past memories of Macaque and his Dad did nothing. In fact he tried to disregard it. So what would happen in the future? Would his Dad try to just go back to how they were before? He couldn’t picture that. But he also couldn’t imagine his Dad and Baba meeting up to let Bai He and him spend time with both of them down the line….unless…they do it already. Break the ice, and if it already happened once they won’t be so against it happening again, and again.

“Bai He we have to get our Dads to meet up now!”

Chapter End Notes

Wanted to establish that this is set in the 90s, like the Lindsay Lohan Parent Trap movie, so no cell phones or internet. Also from my memory, celebrity stalking was more toned down from what it is like currently.

MK is put through so much in the show so no in this fic it's all easy acceptance and smooth sailing....well not so much that last bit, but easy acceptance!!

Also, will this plan turn out like most of MK's rushed, not thought out plans??? WHO KNOWS?!

Chapter 3: The Reunion

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

“I mean I haven’t even been able to show MK anything about what I do! But does that matter?!”

“Well I-”

“No of course not! Because when does anything I do come before the mighty and oh-so-great Sun Wukong!”

“Realistically-”

“But of course he can’t handle a child! He was zero help when she was a baby! That’s why I had to take her!”

“Ma-”

“No! That’s not fair! Forget I said that!....I take that back.” Macaque finally slumped onto his bed. The clothes he had been planning on throwing at/near his suitcase, as he had been previously during his ranting, dropping from his hands. “But he’s still an ass!” The dark monkey snarled.

Tang, who had been listening to his boss rant and rave while throwing items together to pack, waited for 3 seconds before finally deeming it safe to actually try to speak again. This time it seemed like he wouldn’t get interrupted or bulldozed over.

“Mac, Mac, Mac, Mackie,”

“Don’t call me that.” Said monkey growled as he glared at the glasses wearing man, who took a seat next to the scowling monkey.

“You’re thinking like this is the end but it’s not!” Tang smiled. “MK will be back and you can invite him to a play you're actually performing in.” Tang waved his hand about, “I know you wanted to show him your world of theater and all that. But he’d probably like it more if he got to see how they set everything up for the big day and then have you be in the big day. Not just some repeat performance of some random play he may not even be interested in.”

“You may have a point.” Macaque grumbled, still upset.

“Of course I do!” Tang responded confidently. At the sound of Macaque letting out another aggravated breath, Tang pulled back his confident demeanor and placed a comforting hand on the darker monkey's shoulder. “I know what you’re really….frustrated about, but you know what you should be focusing on?” When the dark monkey’s eyes met his, Tang continued. “The fact that for the first time in years you will be able to see Bai He and MK together!”

A weight seemed to be lifted off the dark monkey’s shoulders at that thought, and even a smile flitted across his dark face. “You’re right. You’re right!” He slapped his knees and stood up to continue packing. “Both of them together.” He smiled at the thought again.

“And let’s be honest, the California sun will be great! Plus it’s always great getting out of the city for a bit.” Tang smiled at the thought.

“Is that what you are looking forward to?” Macaque asked with a teasing tone.

“Naturally!” Tang beamed.

“Well I’ll say this, if Wukong says one wrong thing I’m going to need you to hold me back from smacking him in front of-”

“I’m back!” MK yelled out as he entered the apartment.

“MK!” Macaque yelled back in greeting.

“What timing.” Tang muttered, ignoring the glare his boss gave him.

“Did you see Red Son off to the airport? He get on his flight alright?” Macaque yelled out as the teenager quickly made his way over to his Baba’s room.

“Yeah, did you and Tang finish packing?” MK asked as he reached Mac’s bedroom.

“I did!” Tang called from inside the room.

“I’m,” MK saw the mess of clothes around and some in the suitcase. “Working on it.” The dark monkey finished.

-

“I thought you said this was an emergency.”

“Did you not hear what I said?! This is an emergency!” Wukong screeched at the tall bald man, who looked 100% done with said monkey’s antics.

“Paparazzi pay offs, public relations fallouts, giant public spectacles.” The bald man rattled off, “Those are emergencies Wukong. Your ex coming to visit is something everyone deals with.”

“Tripitaka!” The monkey whined. “Macaque coming back! How am I supposed to deal with that?!”

“Like any other-” Tripitaka tried to intervene but was interrupted.

“I mean I’m already having trouble keeping Al away! He kept saying he wanted to officially meet MK, but I didn’t want to spring him on him as soon as he got home. And then when I saw Bai He! Ack!” The golden monkey broke off with a smile. “Did I tell you how big she is? She’s so pretty and well mannered!”

“So you said.” Tripitaka added quietly with a small, reserved smile.

“Right, well I can’t say oh hey honey you can’t meet my son but want to meet my secret daughter who I haven’t seen since she was an infant?!” Wukong raved exuberantly. “So now I have to deal with keeping Al away while having my ex over at my house?! I mean how does that look?!”

“Not great, I’ll admit.” Tripitaka grimaced and Wukong pointed at him repeatedly as if, to emphasize that was the monkey’s point. “However we have a tight NDA agreement with Al, if he did ever try to spill anything about you or your family’s secrets he knows he’ll be blacklisted from here to Heaven.”

“Ugh!” Wukong let out a loud groan as he racked his hands through his hair. “Can you please not think like my Manager but act as a friend?! I need help here!”

Tripitaka let out a sigh. “Wukong, I will always make sure your affairs are in order. I am your Manager first and foremost.” Wukong sulked and seemed to sink further into the sofa he was on. “However,” He said pointedly to get the pouting monkey’s attention. “As your friend I’ll say this,” the bald man folded his hands together and gave the monkey a serious stare. “You have a son and daughter who want to be in each other's lives, and in Macaque’s and your lives. You’re going to see him, you need to come to grips with that.” When Wukong looked ready to interrupt, the bald man held up a hand to silence him. “You both are going to be on edge but I know you can be the bigger man here.” He gave an encouraging smile which Wukong reciprocated. “Do not stoop to his level, rise above the petty insults and prodding you know he’ll do. You have to be better for your children.”

Wukong let out a long huff of air. “You’re right. Ugh he’s just so annoying, and he will try to start shit I just know it.”

“Rise above it.” Tripitaka intoned again.

The bald man then steepled his hands together and looked pensive. “As for Al, I would inform him you have a family emergency, as that’s not a lie, and you will let him know once it calms down.”

“I tried that already, he’s just very…enthusiastic.”

“Hmm, perhaps I can see if I can send him a small job in order to keep him busy.” The bald man closed his eyes as he thought of what offer he could send the actor.

“Thank you Tripitaka!” Wukong launched himself at the man and wrapped him in a hug. “You're the best!”

-

Bai He was in the pool with Sandy and Mei, laughing as Pigsy set up their afternoon snacks by the pool. Wukong meanwhile was trying to just enjoy the moment but was on edge knowing any minute Macaque and MK would arrive. However when Bai He would call his name he always smiled at his daughter and gave her his full attention.

“GUYS!” MK’s voice cut across the yard, all eyes swiveling up to the backdoor of the house and there stood MK with his arms spread wide and a huge smile on his face.

“MK!” They all yelled back.

Wukong got up to run over to his son but the knowledge that behind MK, somewhere, was the dark monkey, held him back. Not that it mattered since MK ran towards the pool and all of them. Mei, Sandy, and Pigsy all shouted their greetings and questions. Mei asking about her New York souvenirs, Pigsy and Sandy about camp and his well being.

“Baba!” Bai He yelled as she quickly swam to the side of the pool.

“Bai He!” And there he was. Macaque.

The dark furred monkey was looking to be leaning on a rather plain looking man with glasses. However the dark monkey immediately straightened up upon Bai He calling for him. Oddly, Wukong remembered him looking different yet exactly the same. Macaque still had his fur impeccably groomed, though his tail was fluffier than he remembered. He was wearing a purple and black hafu, and his six ears were still prominent and eye-catching in their beautiful colors. And-

“Dad? Dad!”

And now Wukong was in the pool, and considering he didn’t know how to swim he instinctively began to panic. Luckily Sandy grabbed him and yanked him out of the water. Upon being placed on the side of the pool Wukong began coughing, as everyone gathered around him to make sure he was ok. And of course one voice rang out louder than the others and it was that voice, that wasn’t asking if he was ok but was laughing hysterically.

“Graceful as always.” Macaque laughed, clutching his sides as he continued to laugh at Wukong’s expense.

“Baba!” Both MK and Bai He scolded, however the dark monkey only wiped a tear from his eye.

“Didn’t see the giant pool as you walked right into it?!” He laughed.

“Ok boss, let’s get some of this delicious looking food in you.” The glasses wearing man said as he firmly grabbed and directed Macaque to where the snacks were.

“Are you ok Dad?” Bai He asked gently.

“I’m fine. Really, I’m fine.” Wukong assured everyone as he stood up. “I know I’m all wet but,” He held open his arms to MK. “Give me a hug kiddo!”

MK beamed and launched himself at the golden monkey. “I missed you Dad!”

“Oh I missed you bud!” Wukong gave a tight squeeze

“Also, don’t hold it against Mac.” MK said quietly, “He uh…had a bit too much on the plane and not enough food.”

“Oh!” Wukong’s face lit up like it was Christmas. “He’s drunk!”

“Dad!” MK whispered in a warning tone.

“No, no I won’t say anything.” Wukong waved off as he pulled out of the hug and cast a smug grin at the dark monkey, who didn’t notice his stare, too busy eating some snacks. ‘Rise above it.’ He thought as he looked between MK and Bai He.

“Look at you two! Together!” He beamed. Bai He and MK smiled at each other as MK wrapped an arm around his younger sister. “I’m so happy!” The golden monkey pulled the two into a group hug in his excitement. “I can’t believe it!”

“Neither can I.” Macaque said as he walked over to the three.

Wukong pulled away and let Macaque have his turn pulling their kids into a group hug.

Meanwhile, Sandy and Mei were keeping their distance at the other side of the pool, not wanting to intrude on the reunion occurring before them. And that’s when they noticed Pigsy blushing and fumbling for his words as the man who arrived with Macaque showered the pig in praise for the food.

-

Eventually everyone was getting ready for dinner. Wukong and Macaque were the first to arrive at the table. Wukong had changed quickly and Macaque hadn’t needed to change. Both stood there in the dining room in silence before Wukong tried to break it.

“I’d offer you a drink but from how you could barely stand up straight when arriving, I’m guessing you’re done for the day?” Wukong teased.

Macaque glared at the other monkey as he folded his arms in front of his chest. “At least I didn’t embarrass myself in front of everyone.”

The two glared at each other before Wukong took a calming breath. They couldn’t let the kids come into an atmosphere of this.

“So summer camp?” Wukong asked to try and break the tension.

Macaque barked a laugh. “I can’t believe we both thought to send them there. What are the odds?”

“I don’t know.” Wukong laughed. “...I seriously can’t believe they’re together.”

Macaque shook his head in agreement, he then bit his lip as he looked at the golden monkey before looking away “You…did a great job with MK.” He glanced back at Wukong to see how touched the man looked.

“Well, right back at you with Bai He.” He gave a small smile, and now it was Macaque’s turn to look touched.

Eventually everyone came together and Pigsy served the dinner. During dinner, it did not go unnoticed by anyone how Pigsy could not seem to keep a blush off his face as Tang showered the man in compliments over the course of the dinner.

The next day MK and Bai He went horseback riding as Wukong and Macaque watched.

“Be careful.” Macaque warned as he watched the two do warm up circles.

“Relax, MK’s been riding for years now and I taught Bai He myself.” Wukong gestured over to the two. “Look at them, they’re naturals!”

“Still…” Macaque said, casting a distrustful look at the large animals. “What if they get spooked or-” a snort cut him off.

“I forgot how much of a mother-hen you are.” Wukong chuckled, ignoring Macaque’s glare.

“Well I remember how impulsive and reckless you are.” Macaque shot back.

“Hey!” Wukong said offended.

“You’re going to deny it?”

“...I may have been reckless with myself but never MK, or Bai He for the time she’s been here.”

“May have?” Macaque quoted back sarcastically.

“Oh shut up.”

“And I’m going to remind you of the movie night incident.” Macaque teased as he continued to watch the kids like a hawk.

“Movie night incident?” Wukong muttered as he tried to think of what Macaque was talking about. Then it hit him.

Macaque had been out, so Wukong and a very young MK had a movie night together. Wukong’s choice of movie was something he enjoyed, but was not completely (or at all) suitable for children. MK had been sobbing when Macaque arrived home and had nightmares for a time after that.

“You can’t keep holding that over my head!” Wukong shot back.

“I can and I will.” Macaque said smugly then pointed at where the kids were riding off to “Did you fence off your whole land? Could someone get in or they get out?”

Wukong rolled his eyes at the dark monkey’s worried tone. “You know I did. You were the one always complaining to me about privacy and security.” He said in a mocking tone.

That got Wukong an angry glare from the dark monkey. “I remember you fighting me on it constantly, and disregarding all my concerns about the kids safety and privacy.”

“I did not disregard anything!” Wukong shot back, hackles rising.

Macaque spluttered then folded his arms and glared back at the now defensive golden monkey. “Oh you didn’t?! I told you to keep MK out of the press and what did you do?”

“Not this again!” Wukong groaned, irritation adding onto his anger. “MK being in some shots and stories is fine. I told you I would never let it go too far and I did! I mean look at him!”

“You didn’t give him a choice Wukong!”

“Oh and you did with Bai He?! I bet no one even knows you have a kid with how you wanted them so hidden from everything and everyone!” Wukong ranted.

“I keep her out of the public so she doesn't feel the pressure of the public! You may love the spotlight but not everyone is you!”

“Like you don’t love the spotlight!”

“How would you know?! You were so busy hogging it and trying to rope our kids into it-”

“I went out with the kids!” Wukong exploded. “I’m not going to fist fight every person with a camera cause I want to take my kids on outings! And I’m not going to hide in the shadows and sequester them off like I’m ashamed of them!”

“I’m not ashamed of them! I wanted to protect them!”

“And I did protect them!”

Macaque ran his hands through his hair and let out a scream of frustration. “You are so irritating! You do this every time!”

“Oh here we go.” Wukong groaned and ran his hands through his hair to try and dispel some of his energy and anger.

“You disregard everything I say because you always know what’s best, and heaven forbid someone disagree with King Wukong!” Macaque mocked.

“I did not disregard you, I explained why you were wrong.” Wukong shot back and then gestured to Macaque. “But you can’t be wrong, oh no. I’m always the one in the wrong, not you!”

“You are the one always in the wrong!” Macaque shot back to which Wukong pointed at him in anger.

“See?! That!” The golden monkey let out a growl “And you call me the irritating one. I listened, and I told you I would make sure if anything got too big or anything too inappropriate, I’d have Tripitaka buy or bury the story or picture.”

“Do NOT bring that man into this!” Macaque growled in fury.

“Oh right you hate him.” Wukong let out exasperated. “How could I forget that?”

“You waved our children around on the chance that, that man could magically fix all your problems! You didn’t even-”

“I’m not arguing with you about this again!” Wukong said in exasperation. “And you know what? I don’t have to!” With that Wukong turned and walked off.

Macaque glared at the golden monkeys retreating back. Scoffing, he turned and glared at the barn for lack of anything else to look at.

When MK and Bai He returned, Wukong’s absence and Macaque’s barely restrained frustration tipped the kids off that leaving their Dad’s alone a day after their reunion was maybe not the best idea. When they asked, Macaque brushed it off as Wukong being Wukong. The dark monkey then asked them how the ride was and kept the conversation on them.

When they got back to the house, Wukong seemed to have hidden himself away as he was nowhere to be found.

“Typical Wukong, running away from his problems.” Macaque mumbled and then walked off to get a drink when that earned him a disappointed look from both MK and Bai He.

“MK.” Bai He said as she watched Macaque run off to find a drink. “We’re going to need help.”

“Yup, but luckily I already thought of this!” MK grinned.

-

Sandy sat opposite the two monkeys who were sitting on porch chairs a fair distance away from each other. The big blue man was drinking tea, while Wukong was munching on a peach and Macaque was nursing a glass of wine.

“This is pointless.” Macaque scoffed moving to get up, but Sandy held up his hands to halt the monkey.

“Your children disagree.” And that got Macaque to scoff but sit back down. Wukong only took another bite of his peach.

“You two need to be able to be civil to each other in front of your children.” Sandy lightly scolded. “Personally I think you both are very lucky they are not angrier at you two for keeping them a secret from each other.” Both monkeys flinched at that, and Sandy softened his tone. “So don’t you think you owe it to them to try this?”

“Yes.” Macaque and Wukong breathed.

“Great! Let’s start with something simple. I want each of you to give a genuine compliment to the other.”

Macaque and Wukong shared a look then looked away.

“Come on, one of you has to start.” He encouraged.

“I gave him a compliment yesterday.” Macaque finally said, and Wukong jumped on that with his own response.

“Same here.”

“What were they?” Sandy prodded gently.

“I said he did a great job with MK.”

“And I said he did the same for Bai He.”

“I see, and what did you both mean by that?”

“That MK’s a great kid and…Wukong did a good job raising him.” Macaque elaborated begrudgingly.

“Now what part of MK being a great kid is because of Wukong? Anything in particular?”

Macaque rolled his shoulders, clearly uncomfortable as his tail wrapped around his own leg. The dark monkey was firmly looking at the floor while Wukong and Sandy stared at him.

“With his transition. I…don’t know how I would have handled it…and clearly MK is happy and has friends so…I’m giving credit to Wukong for that.” Macaque glanced over to Wukong, only to startle when he saw the golden monkey looked almost choked up.

“...thank you.” Wukong choked out, trying to contain his emotions. Macaque continued to stare at his ex, conflicting emotions dancing around inside him.

“That is quite a compliment.” Sandy said into the following silence.

Wukong took a deep breath to compose himself to elaborate like Macaque had done. “Well I meant something similar with Bai He. I mean not the transition since-” He cut himself off, “Anyway, I meant that Macaque has done a great job raising her because she’s so well mannered and polite. And,” Wukong grinned then flashed a smile to Mac who blinked at him in confusion. “She’s wicked smart, like wicked with pranks, so I know she got that from Mac.” Macaque let out an airy chuckle as he smiled at that.

“Never let anyone get away with crossing you.” Macaque said and Wukong laughed.

“Yeah that! And hearing how she got back at MK at camp I mean,” He and Macaque chuckled as they remembered the stories the kids had each told them. “Amazing!”

Sandy smiled at the two monkeys and took a drink of tea. “This is good progress.” He intoned. Sandy then went on to give them tips on how to try and remain civil around each other and that each day he thinks it would be best if the two gave one compliment to the other. And to keep in mind that this was for their children, they owed it to MK and Bai He to try and get along. After all those explanations Sandy left the two monkeys alone.

“Thanks for what you said about MK and me.” Wukong said into the silence.

“I meant it.”

Wukong nodded, then shook his head in the negative. “I always thought I handled it all terribly. I mean I still get haunted by things I may have said wrong or when I called him the wrong name or pronoun afterwards. I just…” Wukong let out a tight wheeze of air as he sniffled.

Macaque let out an airy chuckle. “Joys of parenting.” He said sarcastically. “You get to feel guilty all the time.”

“Constantly feel behind.” Wukong added.

“Think you are never doing enough.”

“Or being there enough.”

“Yeah...” Macaque laughed. “Why were we so eager to be parents again?” He joked.

“Because it’s the best!” Wukong said simply and Macaque nodded in agreement.

“Yeah it is.”

Chapter End Notes

Love this chapter, really love Mac and SWK fighting/arguing and then commiserating with each other. Feels very on point with them lol

 

We're reaching the end of my fully pre-written chapters. The others are skeletons so updates may slow down as I flesh them out. That being said if you guys have questions or suggestions please feel free to share them cause it may help me in fleshing out the other chapters. :D

Chapter 4: Airing out the issues

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

The next couple days went a lot smoother. Wukong and Macaque still butted heads but they did their best to keep it manageable. The daily compliments were very helpful. It helped to remind the two that they may not agree on everything but they still see the good in the other. They haven’t just written the other off as “the problem” like they did while separated for all those years.

However the tentative tranquility was not to last. Tang and Pigsy had disappeared for a “shopping trip” that had lasted hours now. So the unconventional family of four had ordered pizza to be brought in for dinner. When the doorbell rang, they had been playing poker. Bai He and Macaque were trying to help MK get better at the game. Macaque left the group to answer the door and came back with no pizza but a stormy expression and a tall bald man following him.

“Tripitaka!” MK and Wukong cheered as the man followed after the scowling dark furred monkey.

Bai He turned her head in confusion and gave her Baba a concerned glance when she saw how angry and annoyed he was just being near this stranger. Meanwhile Wukong and MK asked the man to take a seat and offered to get him something to drink. Tripitaka ended up sitting next to Wukong but declined the drink.

“Hello All,” He greeted. “I know I’m surprising you so I apologize.”

“We thought you were pizza.” MK laughed.

“Yes I got that when Macaque answered the door…and then tried to close it in my face.” He gave the dark monkey a disapproving look who only glared and snarled at the man.

“I wanted you to know you weren’t welcome.” Macaque snipped. MK’s face fell into a confused frown as Wukong scowled and glared at Macque.

“He is welcome!” Wukong snapped back, getting a glare from Macaque.

Bai He and MK shared a confused look, as Tripitaka cleared his throat.

“Rise above it.” The bald man muttered softly to Wukong, who gave a stiff nod. He then took a breath and put on a more business professional face. “I came by to have you sign some things Wukong and to let you know I did pass some jobs on to who we discussed earlier.”

Wukong brightened up at that news then reached for the tablet Tripitaka pulled out of his coat so he could review and sign off on what his Manager requested.

“Do you not know how an email works?” Macaque teased with a mean smirk, and MK muttered a disapproving “Baba!” to him.

Tripitaka merely gave the dark monkey a thin smile. “I was going to email them over but I also wanted to meet Bai He.” He smiled at the young girl who hunched into herself, unsure of this man her Baba clearly did not like. Glancing around the table Wukong gave his daughter a smile, as did MK, Macaque merely continued to glare at Tripitaka. Said man gave a soft smile and leaned forward on the table. “You won’t remember me, but I remember when you were just a baby. You were so small and had only a few wisps of hair.”

She grabbed her hair and played with a few of the long strands and could only nod at the man. Still unsure how to respond or act around this stranger.

“I told Tripitaka how much you’ve grown and how amazing you are.” Wukong boasted as he handed back the tablet.

“She is!” MK agreed and then he gestured to the cards on the table. “Her and Baba are trying to teach me to be better at poker so I don’t keep losing at camp.”
