Tripitaka gave the cards a disapproving look, before giving a polite smile back to the kids. “I can’t say I condone children gambling or gambling in general.” Macaque let out a snort at that which the bald man ignored. “But it is good that you all are spending time together.”

“Even me?” Macaque said bitingly, trying to bait the bald man to look and respond to him. It succeeded in raising Wukong’s hackles but Tripitaka placed a calming hand on the monkey's arm and nodded at Macaque.

“Yes.”

Meanwhile MK and Bai He were confused, and felt more than a little awkward. Thankfully the doorbell ringing broke the mounting tension, and MK bolted to go get it. MK came back with the pizza and to the sight of Tripitaka rising to leave. Wukong tried to invite the man to stay and eat with them, but the bald man declined and said some polite goodbyes before leaving. The tension didn’t dissipate with his departure though. Macaque seemed to be in a better mood as if all was well again, but Bai He could tell it was forced. Wukong however did not even try to hide his annoyance at the darker simian. Leaving Bai He and MK to try and awkwardly break the tension in the air. However the fun mood that was there while they played cards was thoroughly gone now.

“I can’t believe you closed the door in his face.” Wukong finally snapped as everyone was eating. Macaque smirked and took a large bite of his pizza slice, seemingly happy that Wukong snapped first. “And then how you acted while he was here?!”

“I don’t like him and don’t want him around my kids.”

“Our kids!” Wukong snapped and MK held out his arms as if to ward the two away from each other.

“Guys, guys! Please!” MK pleaded. MK then turned and gave Macaque a disapproving look. “Baba, why do you not like Tripitaka? He’s a great guy!” MK tried to plead the bald man’s case. Afterall Tripitaka had been in his life as long as he could remember, just like Sandy and Pigsy, if not a bit more distant and standoffish/socially awkward. “He’s the one who gave me the half of the photo of you.”

Evidently that was the wrong thing to say as that earned a fierce “What?!” from Wukong and Macaque.

“He gave you what?” Wukong questioned, leaning forward on the table.

“Did you give that man all the things I left behind?! Is that why I can't find any of my stuff?!”

“You took your stuff!”

“Photos, decorations, the small stuff you idiot! Did you give them to that man?!” Macaque slammed his hands on the table, causing the kids to jolt as he stood up. “Of course you did! Heaven forbid you make your own decisions! Better let Tripitaka control everything!”

Wukong also stood up and snarled, ready to fire something back before glancing at MK and Bai He. He then deflated and pushed away from the table.

“I’m not arguing with you in front of them.” With that he took a deep calming breath before saying he’d be right back to MK and Bai He and walked out of the dining room.

“Run away Wukong! It’s what you’re good at!”

“Baba!” MK and Bai He snapped angrily.

“What the hell was that?!!” MK shouted before storming off after his Dad.

Macaque scoffed and let out a frustrated huff before flopping back down into his chair. He took an angry bite of pizza and avoided his daughter's gaze.

“...Is he a dick?” Bai He asked in a monotone voice, startling Macaque into a choking fit.

“Language!” Macaque tried to reprimand as he tried to get his coughing under control.

“Is he?” She asked again, not appearing to take his reprimanding seriously.

The dark monkey opened his mouth before closing it again. Eventually he just shook his head and muttered. “You don’t need to worry about it.”

“You just made a scene and were on edge ever since he arrived.” Bai He pressed.

“Look…” The dark monkey appeared to be searching for what to say before letting out a defeated sigh. “It’s not something you and I should talk about.” He finally breathed out.

That got the young girl's shoulders to rise in anger and hurt. She huffed out a frustrated breath and retracted her hands from where Macaque tried to reach out to her. “You never want to talk to me.”

“Bai He…”

But the young girl only stood up abruptly and ran out of the room. Leaving Macaque alone with the remaining pizza and dishes. He scowled at the food then put his head in his hands, yanking on his fur in frustration.

-

“Dad!” MK called as he finally caught up with Wukong, who had made it to the basement wine cellar. The monkey was just looking around, and aimlessly wandering; however at MK’s call he stopped.

“...are you ok?” The teenager asked, unsure how to start this.

“I should be asking you that.” Wukong let out a defeated sigh. “You and Bai He. We shouldn’t fight like that in front of you two.”

“Yeah,” MK rubbed the back of his head. “That was uncomfortable and…awkward.”

“I know.” Wukong let out a frustrated groan as he raked his hands through his mane. “Look, I’ll clear the air with Mac, or at least try to get us back to where we were.”

“Dad-” MK tried, he didn’t want his Dad to take all that responsibility on himself, Mac was the one instigating a fight.

“But!” Wukong interrupted and gave the teenager a stern look. “Why didn’t you tell me Tripitaka gave you a picture of Mac?”

MK blinked at his Dad before biting his lip and rubbing his arm. “I was afraid you’d take it away.” When Wukong went to say something MK rambled on. “Don’t be mad at Tripitaka! He was reluctant to even give it to me. It was only after I begged for an answer, any answer about remembering another Dad that he just handed me the ripped photo.”

“It wasn’t his to give!” Wukong snapped, angry at his Manager and friend. “He was supposed to hold onto those so-...” He cut himself off then continued, “He should have told me or asked me before just giving you anything about Macaque”

“Or Bai He?!” MK interrupted, now angry himself. Wukong seemed to stutter at this outburst and had the decency to look ashamed as MK continued. “I literally begged you, Pigsy, Sandy, all of you for answers! For years! I knew I remembered there being someone else and none of you thought to tell me the truth or even anything at all about Macaque!” He grunted in frustration as he continued. “And now what? You’re mad someone actually gave me a clue I wasn’t crazy?! How is that fair?!”

“It’s not!” Wukong shouted back before slumping to the ground. Racking a hand down his face he muttered out again, “It’s not, it wasn’t.”

MK slowly lowered himself to sit across from his Dad on the floor. He was still upset but also wanted to comfort his Dad.

“I am not good at emotions, faking them, yes. Actually…” Wukong waved his hands in front of himself as if to articulate his point, which seemed to come across as MK nodded at him. “I tried, I don’t know…I thought…” The monkey couldn’t seem to figure out how to get whatever point he was trying to make across.

“Dad?” MK asked softly.

Wukong met his son's eyes before letting out a sigh. “Ah Sandy can yell at me later, and I’m probably doing everything wrong anyway but here we go!” Wukong said quickly then folded his legs and slapped his hands on his knees. “I didn’t want to remember Macaque!” He admitted in a rush. “He left! He left you and Bai He and me!” MK looked shocked at the admission but Wukong kept rambling on. “It was bad, near the end. I mean you see what happened upstairs? That was all the time! I could never do anything right! Everything I did was wrong and we would fight over it! We were constantly angry at each other! And it sucked!” He rushed, his eyes not focusing on anything as memories played out in his mind. “He never liked Tripitaka, and I know it’s because Tripitaka got me star status faster than Mac, he was bitter and mad about it and didn’t hide it well, at all!” Wukong continued to rush out, his tail swishing back and forth in frustration and anger. “Then…” His tail stilled as it instead curled around Wukong’s waist as if to comfort. “We had another fight. Mac had an audition in New York, I had something, can’t remember what it was now. I told him I’d talk it out with Tripitaka and he blew up. It was…” The golden monkey shook his head. “I came back from meeting with Tripitaka and he just took off! Said he was going to his audition.” He spat the last word, “I tried getting him to stay to explain Tripitaka’s plan on how we’d juggle his thing, my thing, and you kids but of course! He didn’t listen!...And he just never came back. He didn’t call me, he’d call Pigsy to talk to you or give directions I guess, but he just left. Only came back when we were signing the divorce papers and splitting…” Wukong glanced at MK at this, and MK pointed to himself.

“Me and Bai He.”

Wukong grimaced and nodded his head before hanging it lowly. “I just wanted to forget him. I know we were both at fault, but for a long while afterwards I kept thinking it was all my fault. We were broken, because of me.”

“Cause he blamed you for everything.” MK added, anger underlying his tone at his Dad’s defeated look.

Wukong let out a long breath and wrapped his tail around MK’s closest leg. “We’re both different now.” MK scoffed but Wukong continued. “We just each have our sore points. I shouldn’t have started the argument-”

“He was needling you and Tripitaka! It was so obvious!” MK blurted out, scooting closer to his Dad.

Wukong squeezed his leg via his tail and raised his hands placatingly. “Tripitaka is one of those sore points I should have known not to push him on.” MK still scoffed and Wukong was both happy his son sided with him and disappointed in himself, he didn’t want to ruin MK’s relationship with Mac. “Yes he was being a dick.” That got a laugh from his son, making Wukong smile. “And I'll bring that up when we talk. But he hasn’t brought up any of my sore points so I shouldn’t have pushed on his.”

Together Father and son left the wine cellar and MK went off on his own while Wukong went to find Bai He, to make sure she was alright. The young girl was in her room, and was writing furiously in her diary before slamming it shut when Wukong announced his presence.

“Hey, I just wanted to make sure you were alright and to apologize for me and your Baba.” Bai He gave a stiff nod but said nothing more, however her scooting over to make room on her bed, signaled to the golden monkey he was welcome to stay. Taking a seat he reiterated. “I shouldn’t have started the fight and I already told MK I’m going to smooth things out with Mac. But I’m sorry that you guys had to see that.”

Bai He nodded again and bit her lip before asking. “Did that man do something to Baba?”

“...It’s complicated.” Wukong allowed, not wanting to spill everything like he did with MK.

“So he did.” Bai He said matter-of-fact.

“No, see Tripitaka is my Manager and I think him and-” Wukong went to defend his friend but Bai He only folded her arms and pointedly turned away from Wukong.

“Baba wouldn’t act like that around someone unless they really upset or hurt him.” She then gave a glare at the wall. “Not that he’d tell me about it.” She muttered under her breath but Wukong caught it.

“What do you mean by that?”

“Nothing!” She bit out.

“You can talk to me, remember?” Wukong tried, he turned more towards the closed off girl and waited to see if she would say anything.

Bai He glanced at Wukong then back to the wall she was glaring at, and did this for a time before scooting further from Wukong, making the monkey’s face fall. “I don’t want to talk about it.” She mumbled.

“OK.” Wukong tried to go for an easy and approachable tone. “That’s ok. But if you ever do, you can talk to me or Sandy or anyone, you know that right?”

Bai He nodded her head and Wukong took that silence as his cue to leave. Standing he turned back to his daughter once more before leaving.

“Are you going to be ok?”

“I’m fine Dad.” She said stubbornly and Wukong allowed it with a bow of his head as he left to go find Mac.

Mac meanwhile had stumbled across MK, or more accurately MK had gone outside for some space and to clear his head where he found Macaque sitting in a lawn chair in the dark, nursing a drink of water. MK hadn’t seen him sitting in the dark though so when Macaque called out to the teenager he startled him.

“Sorry, I’m sorry.” The dark monkey chuckled as MK clutched his chest. “I didn’t mean to scare you.”

“Yeah well you did!” MK snapped, not happy to be around the dark monkey after hearing everything from his Dad.

Macaque’s laughter immediately died upon hearing MK’s angry tone. Figuring his son was still mad at him for how he acted, he figured he’d apologize.

“MK, about what happened earlier…” MK folded his arms and glared with a surprising amount of anger at Macaque, causing the monkey to stop and just stare at him for a bit before standing up and coming closer to the teenager. “It’s complicated but I’m sorry.” MK’s expression didn’t change so Macaque figured he’d elaborate to try and soothe his son. He already had his daughter upset at him, he didn’t want MK upset with him too. “I won’t lie, I don't like Tripitaka. But I acted immaturely and I’m sorry for that. And for not listening when you tried to calm me and Wukong down.”

“You kept trying to pick a fight.” MK huffed, arms folding and refolding.

Macaque swallowed and nodded his head. “Yes I was.”

“...why do you hate him so much?”

Macaque hesitated before giving the same answer he gave Bai He. “It’s not something you need to worry about.”

“Is it because he made Dad famous?” MK asked, his tone implying this was the correct answer.

Macaque was stunned before letting out a harsh huff of air and smacked his lips together. Shaking his head in disbelief the dark monkey tried to hold in the anger that immediately boiled to the surface.

“Did Wukong tell you that?” He gritted out, glaring at the door leading into the house. MK only shuffled his feet and kept his stance defensive. At the silence Macaque looked back at MK, he held the teenager's stare before scoffing and slumping back into the lawn chair.

“That’s not it!” He growled. “It’s far more complicated than that, but of course Wukong doesn’t see it that way.”

“So why don’t you like him?” MK pushed, gaining only silence as Macaque took a large drink of water. “Baba!”

“It’s complicated.” Was the gritted out response.

“So’s everything right now!” MK gushed as he threw his hands up in the air. “Why can’t you just tell me!”

Macaque leaned further back into the lawn chair and gripped his cup of water tightly. “I already said I’m sorry.” Macaque said, ignoring MK’s aggravated huff at his words. “I don’t want to rehash everything! I’m already dealing with enough just being here!” The dark monkey exploded, he then harshly pointed out to the distance. “I picked where the fruit trees should go!” He then stabbed at where the gazebo with the built in wooden swing was “I designed that gazebo!” He then pointed towards the house, “This was my home too! And there's nothing of me in here anymore!” His voice cracked at that and he took a quick drink of water before shaking his head at himself, upset that he was upset. MK rubbed his arms awkwardly, now unsure what to do at seeing Macaque getting so upset. “I…am trying for you and Bai He.” Macaque stressed, “I don’t want to relive this! I just want you two to be happy!”

For a while both were silent, MK looking unsure as he studied the ground and Macaque curled into himself as he tried to regain control over his emotions.

“....if we talk about everything and try, maybe things can be better than they were…how they are.” MK said as he scooted closer to the dark monkey, who let out a weighted sigh.

“MK,” He reached out and ran a hand through the teenager's hair. “You have a good heart, but all I want is to make sure I can still talk and see you after this Summer.”

MK nodded his head immediately as he exclaimed “Of course you can!” Macaque smiled a small genuine thing, which dropped at MK’s next words. “But can you please try? You and Dad?” He pressured.

Mac, who was exhausted and wished he grabbed wine instead of water only nodded weakly, unsure if he actually meant it. However, that seemed to appease MK who leaned into the monkey who in turn gave the teenager a hug before MK went back inside. Leaving the dark monkey alone to compose himself before he went back inside to grab the now dearly needed glass of wine. But of course the universe could not give him a break because there was Wukong pouring himself his own glass of wine. For a second they just stared at each other before Wukong grabbed another wine glass and filled it. Macaque went over and took the glass from the golden monkey and took a large swig. He then followed Wukong out to the more lit up part of the terrace.

“I’m surprised Pigsy and Tang aren’t back yet.” Wukong said as he leaned against the wall of the house, meanwhile Macaque curled up on the nearest chair.

“If Tang has his way they won’t be back till breakfast.” Macaque chuckled and Wukong looked shocked at that. “Come on Wukong you had to have noticed how those two interacted.” He teased.

“I know Tang likes his cooking!” Wukong admitted flustered. “Bu-but he actually likes Pigsy?!”

“You are still so oblivious.” Macaque chuckled as he swirled his wine. “We’ve been all over the world. Tang has a very refined pallet when it comes to food…and men.” He smirks and Wukong huffs before letting out a chuckle.

“Well damn, your and my butler, who would have thought it?” He mused as he took a sip of his own drink.

The two lapped into an oddly comfortable silence. Both just listening to the sound of the night and sitting in their own thoughts. Wukong then pushed off the wall and sat in the chair closest to Macaque’s.

“About tonight-” Wukong tried to start

“Do we have to get into this now?” The dark simian interrupted, curling into himself and taking a large drink of wine. “I already know you turned MK against me about Tripitaka, so congrats there!” He bit out sarcastically and held his glass up in a mock toast.

“I didn’t turn him against you!” Wukong snapped back.

“Did you talk to our daughter too? Turn her against me?”

“I talked with both of them to make sure they were ok after we fought like that in front of them.” He pointed back inside the house to indicate what he meant by “that” and then crossed his arms. “And don’t worry, Bai He has picked up on your hatred and immediately adopted it.” Macaque furrowed his eyebrows at Wukong, unsure what he meant since last he spoke with Bai He she had run from him. “She doesn’t like Tripitaka, and wouldn’t even let me explain.”

“Oh “explain”?!” Macaque put up air quotes with a scoff. “Sure, and good!” He bit out with a glare. “At least one of our kids won’t be brainwashed by him.”

Wukong’s hackles rose but he tried to remind himself how he had to get them back to at least a peaceful coexistence for the Summer. So through gritted teeth he said, “We can’t fight like that again, not in front of them. It’s not right.”

“You started it.” Macaque smirked and Wukong immediately jumped on it.

“You know, you started it! You were just waiting for me to take the bait and like an idiot I did!” He then threw out an arm and exclaimed “And I just did it again!” He growled and took a large drink while Macaque scoffed and glared off to the side. “We have to stop this! We have to be better!”

“Oh we have to be better?!” Macaque shot back, glaring at the golden monkey now. “I’m not the one telling our kids that you hate someone because you weren’t a star quick enough!”

Wukong slumped back into his chair in defeat, he knew he shouldn’t have shared all that with MK, but he didn’t know what else to do at that moment. “I told him not to hold it against you and that we’re different now.”

“Oh my God!” Macaque enunciated each word with disbelief and anger, he repositioned himself in his chair to look at Wukong more clearly and glared at him. “Are you serious?!”

“I had to explain our past to him!” Wukong defended himself.

“You still really think that’s why I hate that man?!” Macaque said in utter disbelief.

“Come on Mac!” Wukong snapped back. “We fought about it all the time! You didn’t hide how mad and bitter you were about my rise to fame.”

“He controlled every aspect of your life Wukong!” Macaque snapped disbelief quickly turning to anger. “Everything!”

“He did not!” Wukong disagreed with a wave of his hand.

“You went to him before me!” Macaque snapped back, anger clear in his tone. “I was mad because you stopped planning things with me and let him do all your planning! Even about me and the kids!”

“That’s not true.” Wukong said though his tone was flimsy, as he recalled he did rely on Tripitaka to plan everything, every day planned down to the letter.

“You were his little star studded puppet!” Macaque snarled. “He said jump and you said how high.”

“He got me to where I am!” Wukong defended. “I know it was tough but we both knew there’d be challenges in making it big!”

“Challenges we would face together!” Macaque fired back. “But it stopped being you and me, you had to go to him!” Macaque then began to quote back some of the things Wukong had said to him in the past ““Let me just call Trip and make sure I can.” “Let me verify I can with Tripitaka.” “Just have to call Tripitaka real quick.” “Let’s make sure Tripitaka approves”!” Macque snarled and Wukong seemed a bit stunned at this revelation.

“...why didn’t you ever say that?” Wukong asked quietly.

“I did!” Macaque growled. “And you threw the same thing you're saying now in my face!”

Wukong shook his head in the negative so with a vicious smile Macaque reiterated a memory for him. “We had recently moved in here, I told you to stop letting Tripitaka make all our decisions and that I needed you to be with me and the kids! And you told me how you had to do all this so you could keep building your career, so you could be the super star you always dreamed of being.”

“And you said,” Wukong interrupted, defensive and he recalled this fight. “You also needed time and space to work on your career and how it wasn’t fair I was the only one growing!”

“Because it wasn’t!” Macaque shot back, only to have Wukong give him a grin as if in victory. Macaque snarled and shot back. “Yes I wanted my career to grow, but I had to go around your schedule and your -or should I say Tripitaka’s- time so much that I couldn’t!”

“See!” Wukong pointed in victory only to get snarled at.

“No Wukong! After I said I wanted to focus back on my career I told you, you had to take back control of your life and stop leaving everything to Tripitaka! Especially in regards to the kids!” Macaque was so tempted to throw the rest of his wine in the golden monkey’s face but held back. “But you went off about how I shouldn’t be so jealous and that this was for us, even though you hadn’t even consulted us for months!” Macaque huffed out then pointed an aggravated finger at the distance. “I even went to that man and told him to stop controlling your every move! To loosen your leash!”

“You did?” Wukong blinked at that, never having heard that till now.

“Yes!” The dark monkey snapped, “But he just said how he’d try to talk to the theater district about getting me jobs. Like that was the problem!” He screamed and afterwards the two monkeys were left in silence.

Wukong looked contemplative as he thought all this over while Macaque chugged the rest of his wine. Finally Wukong spoke, in a small voice.

“Why didn’t you explain that to me?” When Macaque went to fire back, Wukong held up a placating hand. “I know now you did the one time, but I can’t remember you ever bringing it up again…but maybe I…brushed it off?” He said with no small amount of guilt in his voice.

Macaque opened his mouth, ready to lie and say he brought it up thousands of times and that Wukong had been every bit the asshole Mac had built up in his mind these past years, and brushed it all off each and every time. But remembering their kids and his half assed promise to MK not even an hour ago, Mac shut his mouth. He regretted chugging the rest of his wine.

“I didn’t.” He admitted quietly. “I was so mad that all you thought of me was some bitter husband, mad he wasn’t as popular, that I just…” He let out a grunt and folded his legs underneath himself. “I just started picking your career apart.”

Wukong looked at him with almost shock and hurt? Mac wasn’t quite sure what it was, but Wukong took a deep breath as if to steady himself.

Wukong was reeling at that revelation. As he rose in star status, Macaque had ridiculed him relentlessly, to the point of his self esteem deflating significantly. Taking Macaque to a show was like linking arms with his greatest critique. During showings he would whisper Wukong’s mistakes or how he had failed to draw the right emotion, how he wasn’t good enough for the movie. Whenever an advertisement with him popped up on TV or in a magazine, Macaque only pointed out his mistakes or flaws in scenes and photos. No words of praise, only critiques and harsh words.

“All those comments and poking…everytime you tore me down… it really hurt.” He admitted honestly.

“Yeah well,” Macaque huffed and folded into himself, defensive. “You brushing off everything I said and boiling all my concerns about us and the kids into some petty jealousy wasn’t a picnic either.” The dark monkey glared at his empty cup, willing it to be full again. “Besides you had enough people praising you, someone had to check your ego.” He jabbed.

Wukong let out a sound of disbelief and leaned back in his chair in bafflement. Macaque glanced at him before curling up more, trying to quell any guilt that bubbled up inside him.

“I get I should have listened more about Tripitaka,” that earned an eye roll from the dark monkey. “but do you even care how much that hurt?” Wukong asked honestly. After all, he was a wreck after the divorce. His self esteem in his work and self was at an all time low, shouldering the full blame for their marriage failing, missing his baby daughter, and suddenly a single father.

“You say that like you never hurt me.” Macaque replied, still staring at his empty glass.

“I never ripped you apart!” Wukong snapped. “I never told you your acting was terrible, or you looked ugly, or that everything you touched was doomed to fail!” He snarled, anger and past hurts coming right back to the golden monkey.

“No.” Macaque replied evenly, still not looking at the angry golden monkey. “In fact you did nothing.” He then put the empty glass down on the floor and gave the angry monkey an even look. “How many of my plays did you go to?”

Wukong looked thrown at the question, then quickly thought back on it and remembered the first major play Mac had been in. It was before the kids, he had a minor roll, had some lines but nothing too major. Still Wukong went to the show and brought flowers for his husband, ecstatic he had gotten in a big performance. The next play Mac still hadn’t gotten a major role but did have some speaking lines and Wukong had again shown up with flowers and it had repeated like that till…til his career had made him too busy to attend and he’d just send flowers or have them at home waiting for Macaque. And then once the fighting had ramped up he stopped with the flowers all together, too mad and hurt himself.

At Wukong’s silence Macaque continued. “You never tore me down with words, you just stopped caring. My career could go on hold for yours, and I was there to support you through everything when you had stopped supporting me.”

“I sent flowers.”

“But you weren’t there.” Macaque shot back, remembering how much it stung when he’d look out at the crowd and not see anyone. It became so common he stopped looking for a familiar face. “I was there for every screening and showing of yours, even near the end.”

“Yeah and you tore me down throughout them.” Wukong shot back as he threw back the rest of his wine, finishing it. He tried not to think about the times before the fighting, when Macaque would sit beside him and smile at him encouragingly when his career first started. Those times were so far back, replaced with mean smirks and even meaner words.

Both monkeys glared off into the distance, neither looking at each other, but both had their tails curled around their own waists as if to comfort themselves. Finally Macaque broke the silence.

“Our last fight while married, do you remember it?”

“When you left? Yes!” Wukong said, not without bitterness.

“I had a major audition, Broadway reached out to me for the main role.”

“They did?” Wukong asked, “You didn’t tell me that!”

Macaque shrugged, “We weren’t exactly conversing a ton at that time.” He continued, “I needed you to drop one thing for me so you could be with the kids and I could go do something for myself.” He then glared at Wukong, “And your response was to go talk to that freaking man.” He growled out. “So I left, and you never came for me. You never even called.”

Wukong blinked at that but slumped further into his seat and just said back blandly “Do you blame me?” Macaque visibly flinched at that, but the golden monkey only gestured to the space between the two of them. “You were mad I didn’t listen one time, and then you spent the rest of our marriage tearing me down. And then you just left….” Macaque looked away and seemed to just glare at the ground as Wukong shrugged in defeat. “I didn’t know you wanted me to go after you but…I don’t think anything would have changed even if I did.”

Macaque grit his teeth and continued to glare at the wall. Of all the conversations to have, he didn’t want this one. He couldn't take this one! Wukong really thought nothing would have changed. But Macaque had built…so much into the thought (fantasy) of everything being different if he did come after him! If Wukong made the effort and put him first, if Wukong chose him over his career and Tripitaka. Everything would have been better if that happened! And to have Wukong just shatter it so effectively…Macaque knew he hurt Wukong, that was the whole point of all his petty insults. He wanted to hurt Wukong like he was hurt. Wukong disregarded his concerns for the kids, waved away his requests, listened only to Tripitaka, and stopped supporting him. He was hurt damnit! So yeah he wanted Wukong to hurt as well, and he used Wukong’s precious career to do so. The thing he sacrificed so much of them for, he wielded against the golden monkey. But he wielded his weapon too well apparently and he never realized. He had dealt with so much, was hurt so much and still held onto the hope of…something. But Wukong got insults and just gave up? Yeah some had been cutting, some he even instantly regretted after saying but never took back because the brief hurt on Wukong’s face healed some of his own for a brief moment. But that was all it took?

Macaque grit his teeth and resisted the urge to get up and pace in anger. “Yeah I insulted you but you had everyone building you up, all the time!” He didn’t see Wukong’s look of disbelief at that, the golden monkey unable to grasp why Mac couldn’t see that his opinion was valued more than some reviewers or co-workers or friends. “In everything you did! Everyone loves Sun Wukong! Everyone wanted to support you and be on your side!”

“But you weren’t-” Wukong tried but Macaque barreled over him, not listening to him at all anymore, stuck in his own anger.

“Even after our divorce! DBK and Iron Fan stayed friends with you, not me! Everyone picked you!” His voice cracked and the dark monkey swore loudly at that. “I was alone! Friendless, with a baby, and in a city I barely knew! In an apartment that wasn’t my home!”

“You left!” Wukong shot back.

“You left first!” Macaque snapped right back at him, the two now glaring directly at each other. “You pushed me away! Tripitaka, your career, I pushed for us over and over! The one time I really tested you, pushing you to come for me, you gave up?!”

Wukong swallowed hard as Macaque’s now tear filled eyes searched his own. Finally Wukong looked away and tried not to let the huff of disappointment/hurt from Mac get to him. This wasn’t worth it. This was a fight years too late to be having.

“We’re not going to solve this…” Wukong sighed, and saw Macaque slump back into his chair, curling into himself. Giving Mac and himself a few seconds of silence to recoup. Then when he deemed himself ready Wukong took a deep breath and steadied himself, “Look, we were both just….not right for each other. We hurt each other and didn’t talk or listen…but all that’s in the past. We have to focus on the future. We have to be able to be in the same place and be at least civil…for MK and Bai He.”

He glanced at the dark monkey who was turned completely away from him, body language screaming ‘do not approach’.

Biting his lip Wukong added quietly. “For what it’s worth, I am sorry.”

He didn’t elaborate on what for and Macaque couldn’t take anymore of this talk, of this night, of being here, all these revelations and-

The dark monkey jolted upright, still not looking at Wukong he gruffly muttered. “Yeah, let's do better.” And he went to march back inside, empty wine glass forgotten and disregarded.

A strange sense of panic overtook Wukong. Maybe it was all this talk of when Macaque left or how their once beautiful relationship soured into what it was now. But a deja vu overtook him, flashing back to when he said he’d talk with Tripitaka instead of talking with Macaque about the audition and kids. So he jolted upright and grabbed Mac’s tail, halting the ebony monkey.

Mac’s shoulders rose and his hand twitched with the want to turn around and punch Wukong. He just wanted this to be over.

“My compliment of the day.” Wukong rushed out, puzzling Mac as they had already done their daily compliments. Wukong said he played poker extremely well and Macaque complimented Wukong’s way with horses when they had visited the stables earlier in the day.

“We already did that.” He tried to remind the golden monkey, while still not looking at him.

“You did better than I ever could have.” Wukong absently pet the tail in his hand, as he continued. “A new home, new city, and with a baby…I wouldn’t have made it through that.”

“...yes you would have.” Mac muttered, not seeing the golden monkey shake his head in the negative.

“No.” Wukong stressed, “I really would not have, not like you did. You raised an amazing girl and became an international star.” Wukong complimented, still absently petting Mac’s puffed up tail.

Unsure if he believed him but wanting to, Mac said nothing and only swallowed down his emotions. He then tried to pull his tail out of Wukong’s grip. With a small sigh of defeat Wukong released his grip and took a step back. And the sigh prompted Mac to return the compliment.

“You have always been an amazing actor.” Mac said quietly, jolting Wukong to look at the black monkey's back. Mac glanced over his shoulder at Wukong and smirked. “And you’ve never looked ugly, ever.”

Wukong smiled and felt lighter as he watched Macaque walk back inside.

-

When Macaque made it back to his room he was torn between the want to cry, and the want to shut down and sleep. Flopping down on the bed face first he tried to zone out, tried to disassociate from everything that happened tonight. His son thought the worst of him, Bai He was upset with him, and Wukong would never come for him.

Yes it did feel a bit good to get everything aired out with Wukong…but to what end? Wukong and him were done. Wukong was never going to come. He had apparently ruined that, like he ruined whatever he had with his daughter and son.

With a groan Macaque sat up and decided he couldn’t go down that road. So he quickly stripped and reached into his bag for his emergency sleeping meds. He was done with tonight.

And he had a butler to kill since Tang was supposed to be his buffer in situations like this!

Chapter End Notes

Mac “I don’t want to rehash everything” then proceeds to rehash basically everything with SWK.

Also, I see a lot of fics with all/most the blame being on SWK or Mac (mainly SWK seems to get the blame in most fics). I picture their ending/break up being on both of them in LMK’s “canon”, not just one of their faults but a combo of both. They’re both to blame so I tried to do that in this AU. They both see themselves as the victims, when they both are in fact the villains and the victims. They were toxic and needed to grow outside each other before reconnecting (and yes that is coming I swear lol) Gotta see where it all went wrong though before you can make it right.

I will say, Mac is hurt more in this chapter because he hasn’t tried to move on from Wukong. Wukong has tried to move on, and is dating Azure now. But Mac has been on a fantasy loop of what if and a possible someday. So this was revelations topped with a reality check of that fantasy never happening, it’s a hard blow.

 

As I said last chapter: We're now at the end of my fully pre-written chapters. The others are skeletons so updates may slow down as I flesh them out. If you guys have questions or suggestions please feel free to share them cause it may help me in fleshing out the other chapters.

Chapter 5: Memories, Memories, Reality

Chapter Notes

Remember how I said the chapters aren't fully written out anymore and that I had to flesh them out? Well on top of that I had my baby, so being a new Mom means these updates are grinding to a very slow pace. Just wanted to give you all a heads up about that.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Wukong woke up bright and early, always being more of an early bird himself. However today it was because he barely got any sleep the night before. Yesterday was an emotional day, and to think it started so pleasantly. All night his and Macaque’s talk had run through his head, keeping him up.

As he made himself a nice bath in his huge jacuzzi like bathtub he reflected, once again, on everything that had come to light the previous night. And unfortunately with it came the guilt that they had fallen apart because of him.

But it wasn’t just him! He didn’t listen to Mac one time and the man then verbally abused him about his career and how he wanted to raise the kids. But if he had listened properly, if he didn't jump to the conclusion the other was just jealous…but Mac didn’t try to tell him that! Except he did…once.

Wukong let out a loud groan as he plunged himself into his bath. He couldn’t win! This was just a spiral of blame and grief. And that’s not even factoring in the kids!

As he resurfaced he began to lather himself up as he tried to think of a battle plan. Bai He clearly was closing herself off, whether it was just from him or from him and Mac he wasn’t sure. But he had to touch base with her. Try to get her to open up to him again. Meanwhile MK…another audible groan left the monkey.

He had to talk with MK, he now had to clear the air about Macaque and Tripitaka. And evidently MK had already talked with Mac after their conversation so he had to try to catch MK as quickly as possible to clear the air. He didn’t want to ruin MK and Macaque’s relationship, especially in light of the real reason Macaque did not like Tripitaka.

Which made so much sense now that he knew the truth! Their fights about privacy and him saying Tripitaka would fix things of course only made Mac more mad, it was part of the root problem for the dark monkey. He was mad that Wukong went to Tripitaka for everything. The monkey submerged himself completely as he let out a scream. He couldn’t believe this! If Mac had just said it again, even just one more time! Or If he had just listened the first time!....But he didn’t and Mac didn’t try again. They both let things fall apart.

But that was enough of his one-on-one time. Time to get ready for the day and talk with MK and Bai He.

He briefly used his hair dryer to barely dry his wet fur. He then wrapped himself in one of his fluffiest robes and made his way outside to the patio. Dragging a chair to make sure it was fully in the sun, Wukong unwrapped his robe a bit and basked in the sun, using it to dry himself.

A few minutes later an angry looking Pigsy stomped outside, loudly setting down a bowl of fruit.

“Hey Pigsy.” Wukong greeted cheerfully, blissfully ignoring the angry glare the pig demon gave him.

“You know you leave water all over the house when you do this?!” Pigsy complained, hands going to his hips. “Just get more hair dryers and use them!”

“But I prefer the sun!” Wukong whined petulantly.

“And I prefer not to clean up all the water you leave in your wake!” Wukong only stuck his tongue out at the man, who then motioned towards the fruit bowl. “There’s your breakfast, don’t come back inside till you're completely dry!” He threatened.

Wukong again stuck his tongue out but grabbed a peach from the bowl. “You know, you should be a little nicer to me since I’m not teasing or hounding you about yesterday.” Wukong gave the pig a teasing grin.

Pigsy only lifted an eyebrow. “Why?”

Wukong waggled his eyebrows. “You and Tang?” Pigsy blushed as Wukong continued. “You didn’t come home last night.”

“We couldn’t! The food would have gone bad!” Pigsy sputtered out, still blushing at the monkey demon's insinuation.

“...the food?”

“Yeah! Get your head out of the gutter!” Pigsy reprimanded, smacking Wukong upside the head. “We went to that really great food market for some vegetables, I wanted to get the best asparagus so I can work on getting Bai He to like them. Then Tang seemed interested in all the food so I suggested the good meat market. We had time so we ran to that market even though it’s a ways out, he seemed on board with the idea. Well then a huge traffic jam popped up and the meat would go bad if we stayed in it so I got us two hotel rooms so we could store the food in the fridges.”

“So you two weren’t on a date?”

“NO!” Pigsy fumed, his face turning bright red. “I-I couldn't-”

“DAD!” A loud yell interrupted the two. “DAD!”

“Out here!” Wukong called and Pigsy huffed as he turned and stomped inside, passing Bai He as she made her way outside. “Morning sweetie.” Wukong greeted with a smile.

“Hi Dad, where do you keep the extra hair dryers? Baba’s asking.” Bai He gestured back inside with her thumb.

“Oh I only have the one in my room.” He gestured towards himself. “I prefer to sunbathe then use those loud hair dryers.” In fact, back in the day he and Macaque used to sunbathe to dry their fur together. Macaque wasn’t a fan of how loud the hair dryers were, as they hurt his ears, and so the two would relax outside and comb each other's fur as they let the sun dry them.

“Only one?!” Bai He boggled. “Baba has four at home!” She then snickered as she turned to make her way back inside. “Oh he’s going to hate hearing this.”

“MK might have one as well.” Wukong yelled after her and from her dismissive wave Wukong wondered if she was going to share that information with Macaque or not.

“One of what?” MK asked as he walked outside with a plate of waffles and pulled a chair over to sit next to his Dad in the sun.

“Hair dryer.” Wukong answered with a shrug. “Evidently Mac uses a lot of them in New York.” Which was weird for Wukong to think about since the black monkey hated them years ago. However he had bigger things to focus on now that he was alone with MK.

“Actually Bud I need to talk with you.” Wukong began and MK cocked his head to the side as Wukong turned to give him his full attention.

-

“Baba!” Bai He yelled at the closed bathroom door. She was then answered by a distant “Yes?” along with the sound of the shower still running. “Dad says he only has one hair dryer, though MK may also have one as well.”

“One?!” Macaque screeched. “He’s lying! There’s no way he can dry himself with just one hair dryer!” Bai He snickered as she heard her Baba cursing and mumble some other words that she couldn't and wasn’t meant to hear.

“He says he sunbathes! He’s doing it now!” Bai He waited for a response but when all she heard was silence she elaborated. “I mean he must have taken a bath or a swim or something cause his furs all wet and he’s just sitting outside.” Still no answer other than the sounds of the water running over someone. “Baba?”

“Sorry.” Bai He thought she could hear a sniffle through the door and so she pressed herself against the door to try and hear better. “Sorry honey.”

“Baba are you ok?” Bai He tried to say carefully.

“I’m fine, thank you Bai He.” The sound of water being shut off was heard, “Why don’t you go grab breakfast and I’ll be down in a few minutes.”

Bai He felt like something was wrong but wasn’t sure what and she knew her Baba enough that he wasn’t going to talk with her about it. With a defeated sigh she asked “Do you want me to grab Dad’s hair dryer?”

“No, I’m good, I'll grab it.”

Bai He let out another sigh then folded her arms and went to go grab some breakfast.

Meanwhile Macaque waited till he could hear Bai He walk away before opening the bathroom door. With a towel around his waist he ran to Wukongs room and was going to go to his adjacent bathroom when he was struck by Wukong’s bedroom.

This had been their bedroom. The sheets were different but the bedframe is the same, the light brown walls were what him and Wukong had painted together. The room was messier than it was before, which is saying something cause him and Wukong weren’t the best at picking up after themselves. What with being busy with the kids and work they allowed their room to get messy, and now it was even more of a stye. Macaque let out an amused huff at how Wukong’s messiness had only seemed to have gotten worse with time, unlike himself where he had gotten neater. However, looking around the room Macaque’s nostalgic mood was replaced with bitterness. All the nicknacks and little items he had left behind, all the decorations he remembered picking out and hanging up were gone. Like the rest of the house. Probably packed up in a box and sitting somewhere with Tripitaka of all people. That or thrown out.

Now in a bad mood again he stomped over to the bathroom and almost slipped due to all the water Wukong left behind. “Of course.” Macaque grumbled as he found and used the hair dryer.

He tried not to lose himself in any more memories, yesterday was emotional enough. Today he needed to not go down that emotional road again. The flashbacks of him and Wukong sun bathing and seeing his old bedroom was enough, he needed to get a hold of himself. And looking around the messy bathroom Macaque found himself the perfect distraction.

-

“So it is more complicated than just jealousy.” MK said after Wukong had given him a brief recap of his and Mac’s discussion yesterday.

“Yes. Again Mac and I were at fault…but that’s the past.” Wukong gave a smile he hoped was encouraging. “We’re both ready to try and be better now and going forward.”

MK nodded as he took another bite of his waffles. A small smile flitted across his face “I’m just happy you guys talked. When Baba agreed he’d try, I won’t lie I kinda doubted he actually would but I’m glad you and him talked.”

“Me too bud.” Wukong smiled back and he meant it. It was a lot to take in, but it did help clearing the air at least a little bit.

Soon the two were joined by Bai He who seemed closed off again, her arms around her body as she sat down in a chair closest to MK.

“Bai He, want some fruit?” Wukong showed the bowl of fruit to the girl.

“Or Pigsy’s got some waffles inside.” MK showed his plate as proof.

“Waffles sound good.” She then disappeared inside and a short while later came out with some food.

At her still subdued mood Wukong frowned. “Everything ok?”

“Fine.” Bai He breathed out and both MK and Wukong shared a glance at the clear lie.

“Well what do you two want to do today? We can go for another ride, pool day, or we can go check out the orchard and see how the fruits are doing?” Wukong suggested.

MK looked at Bai He and she only looked at him, waiting for him to take the lead.

“I’m fine with whatever.” MK shrugged and Bai He almost grumbled at the lack of decisiveness in her brother.

“Well I know what Wukong’s doing today.” Macaque interrupted as he came up behind Wukong, grabbing a banana as he walked by. He was still wet, though he was dryer than Wukong had been when he left his bathroom this morning.

“Oh?” Wukong couldn’t help but cock an eyebrow at his ex as Macaque nodded at him.

“You are going to clean that mess you call a bedroom and that pond you call a bathroom.”

“Pond?!” Wukong squawked as Bai He and MK giggled at their Dad’s face.

“I had to practically swim through all the water you left behind just to get your hair dryer. Which by the way, could have killed me with how much water was left in that bathroom.” He teased.

Just as Wukong was about to defend his mess, MK nodded at his Baba and pointed at Wukong. “Pigsy was complaining about all the water you left behind.”

 

“He complains about everything.” Wukong grumbled.

“Is it really messy?” Bai He questioned.

“Remember when that stage hand fell into the staging area and ripped apart the fake snow bags?” Macaque questioned, to which Bai He nodded. “That was much cleaner than Wukong’s mess of a room.”

Bai He scrunched up her face and gave Wukong a disgusted look. “Oh Dad, you have to clean your room!” She lectured.

Wukong puffed out his cheeks and tried to not pout. MK only laughed which was a mistake since Macaque turned his attention to MK. “Don’t think you’re off the hook either young man, I saw your room.”

MK quickly shut up and looked sheepish. “It’s organized in its own unique way?” He tried, to which Macaque only shook his head in the negative. Bai He laughed at her brother and father as they both pouted.

After breakfast MK went to his room to attempt to clean it up. Bai He had stayed to
“assist” him, meaning she lounged on his bed and went through his many art books that he had scattered around. Wukong had tried to stay with the two and look over the art books as well, but Macaque had grabbed the golden monkey’s tail and hauled him off to his own bedroom. Bai He and MK laughed as they heard their Fathers bicker with each other.

“I’m glad they’re getting along better.” MK beamed at Bai He who gave a small smile back but then her face fell. MK noticing this, went over to his sister and sat next to the girl. “Hey you ok? You seem off today.”

“I just…” She trailed off but with a nudge from MK she continued. “I wish Baba and Dad would talk to me. I know Baba’s not as carefree as he’s coming off as, but he never says anything to me.”

“They just don’t want us to worry.” MK tried to comfort.

“I’m already worried!” Bai He fired back. “I’m not you, I didn’t remember Wukong at all! This is all new to me!” MK looked taken aback at that as the young girl continued. “And they’re acting like, ugh!” She groaned. “It’s confusing!”

MK could only put his arm around the young girl as she leaned into him. He didn’t know what to say. He hadn’t thought of how she didn’t have a distant memory of a mystery father like he did. To her, she went to camp with a rare picture of a super star and suddenly found out she had a brother and another Father. And now her Baba, who was her only parent as far as she was aware till this summer, was putting up a front that she apparently could see through and wouldn’t talk with her about it.

“I know I had a distant memory of Macaque but it’s not like I remember a ton of stuff, so I know this is scary and…well you know you can talk to me, right?”

Bai He let out another groan. “Everyone keeps saying that.”

“Well we want you to know you can talk to us!”

Bai He pushed her brother away and with a huff grabbed the closest art book and buried her face in it. “I don’t want to talk about it!” She said petulantly.

Seeing he wasn’t going to get anywhere MK let her have her space and went back to cleaning up his room.

Meanwhile, Wukong let out a grumble and a string of curse words as he used towels to mop up the bathroom floor.

“If you bitch a little less and scrub a little more you’d be done.” Macaque teased as he leaned against the door frame, looking smug as Wukong glared up at him.

“I was going to clean this later.” Wukong grumbled.

“Bullshit.”

“Oh shut up, like you keep your space clean! I know you're just as messy as me.” Wukong said as he threw the now filthy towels into the laundry hamper. He then went about grabbing and going through the clothes he had littered around his room.

“Things change Wukong.” Macaque said easily as he glided into the bedroom and began pulling the covers off the bed, deciding to help the golden monkey. Behind Macaque’s back Wukong’s face fell at his words.

“Yeah?” He breathed out, not really meaning it as a question but evidently Macaque took it as one.

“I keep my house tidy, it’s a big apartment but I don’t want to clutter it up with unnecessary messes.”

“And you using hair dryers now?” Macaque stiffened at that question and Wukong noticed but tried to keep his tone light, he didn’t want to start a fight. “I thought that bothered your ears.”

“Can’t sunbathe in an apartment in New York Wukong.” Macaque answered shortly. “Neighbors complain about nudity, even with the fur, and winters a bitch. Plus no balcony.”

“We have a balcony.”

Wukong and Macaque both jumped at the new voice and swung around to stare at the newcomer in the bedroom door. There stood Tang who also gave a bodily jolt at Wukong and Macaque’s reaction.

“Tang!” Macaque barked but the glasses wearing man held up his hands in placation.

“Sorry, sorry! I was just passing by.” Tang rushed out. “But we do have a balcony.” He turned to Wukong and glanced at Macaque who shook his head then turned back to stripping the bed.

“We have a balcony now, but when I first moved to New York I did not have a balcony. I had a fire escape and that is not the same.”

“Yeah.” Tang agreed, coming further into the room, he looked over to Wukong “Movies tend to over romanticize fire escapes, they’re not nearly as nice or as usable as you see on TV.”

“Ah.” Wukong let out then went back to going through his clothes.

“So Tang.” Macaque purred as he deposited all the bedding in the hamper. He then poked his head out into the hallway and upon seeing the coast was clear he turned with a devious look to the human. “How was your night?”

Wukong barked out a laugh and Tang’s face fell into one of disappointment. Macaque meanwhile looked confused and glanced between Wukong and Tang. Wukong came over and gave the human a pat on the back.

“I heard about your “wild” night from Pigsy.” The golden monkey teased and Tang blushed before letting out a sound of defeat.

“I thought we were on the right track till he said two rooms.” Tang whined and Macaque only folded his arms in confusion.

“What happened?”

Tang now stuck his head out into the hallway and double checked the coast was clear before pulling the monkeys further into Wukong’s bedroom. Wukong was still grinning and Macaque kept his confused expression.

“I thought I’d go with him to the food market and we could turn it into a date, you know? Grab some drinks, some food, get some alone time. And it was going great! He even started talking about the meat district.” Tang was blushing now and tapping his fingers together.

“Which you thought was a euphemism.” Macaque grinned and Wukong let out a squawking sound in his surprise. Tang only blushed further and nodded.

“Turns out it’s an actual district for processing and packing meat.” Tang muttered in embarrassment as Macaque burst out laughing.

“So you just went grocery shopping?!”

“Unfortunately.” Tang grumbled. “I got excited when he said we’d have to get a hotel room.”

“But it was for the groceries.” Wukong teased.

“Two rooms!” Tang bemoaned. “I couldn’t believe it!”

“Did you try to go over to his room later?” Macaque asked, and Tang shook his head in the negative.

“My pride needed a break.”

“Pigsy’s thick-headed buddy.” Wukong comforted while patting the human’s back. “And he’s very serious when it comes to food.”

“I’ll say.” Tang muttered

“Well I’m going to take Tang and help him rebuild what’s left of his pride.” Macaque said as he wrapped his arm around his butler and started guiding him towards the bedroom door. “Meanwhile Wukong you can keep cleaning up here.”

“It’s clean!” Wukong defended as he gestured about the room which was still in disarray and still had clothes and items scattered around.

“It’s really not.” Tang said as Macaque only gave the golden monkey a deadpanned look.

“This is ridiculous!” Wukong complained but went back to cleaning up the room as the other 2 left.

Tang and Macaque made it to Macaque’s bedroom and Tang face planted on the bed. “I appreciate the offer to console me boss but I’ll be fine. I just need to adjust my approach.”

“I’ll say.”

“So what happened while I was out? Everything seems to be going well.”

Macaque barked out a cruel laugh and moved to sit on the bed next to Tang. “I wouldn’t say that.” The glasses wearing man tilted his head and sat up when he saw how Macaque wrapped his tail around himself.

“What happened?”

“Oh you know.” Macaque tried to go for a casual tone, “Got into it with Wukong. Got MK and Bai He mad at me. Then Wukong and I argued about our issues on why our marriage failed.”

Tang boggled at him “....in one night?”

“Yup.” Macaque popped the “P” as he flung himself back on the bed. “I was going to ring you out since you were supposed to help me out with shit like this, but seeing as you weren’t busy getting lucky but held up because of groceries.” Tang grumbled at that and Mac smirked at him. “I figured you’ve been punished enough.”

“Well Bai He and MK seemed fine when I walked past them in MK’s room. Are they still mad?”

“I don’t know. Bai He seems fine, standoffish but not mad at me.” Mac ran a hand through his mane. “But I haven’t properly talked with MK about it, so I don’t know if he still thinks the worst of me or not.”

“Kids are often mad at their parents.” Tang tried to comfort.

“Yeah.” The dark monkey sighed.

“And from what I saw, it seems like you and Wukong talking may have been for the best.” Mac then let out a groan and turned to bury himself in his pillow. “Or not…?” Tang added on lamely. They sat in silence for a few seconds before Macaque spoke.

“He said we weren’t right for each other.”

Tang winced and gave his boss a sympathetic look, however with Mac’s face buried in his pillow he didn’t see it.

“Mac-”

“I knew coming here was a mistake!” Macaque seethed. “I didn’t come here for this and now here I am…with these shitty feelings!”

“He may not have meant it.” Tang whispered as he tried to comfort the depressed and stressed monkey.

“He said those exact words Tang.” Macaque bit back.

“And I’ve heard you say you regret ever having met Sun Wukong but we both know that’s not true!” Tang rebutted and finally dared to put a hand on the monkey’s tense shoulder.

Mac only let out a huff of air and buried himself further into his pillows. Tang then let out his own puff of air and figuratively rolled up his sleeves.

“Look, I don’t think he really meant that. I think you don’t believe it either! But it’s easier to believe he did mean it so you can keep pushing down these feelings that you never really addressed.” Tang lectured. “I bet that talk wasn’t fun. No one likes getting their mistakes thrown back in their face, but this is a real chance! You guys can move forward with this. You’re no longer both standing still with your feelings and pain, but you talked things out and are still able to tease and be near each other the next day. Doesn’t that mean something?”

Macaque remained quiet but peaked his head over towards Tang and gave a small nod. “It might.”

“I’m sure it does!” Tang smiled. “This is a great chance to build something again! Maybe not right away mind you. But you both changed!”

Macaque remembered how he had just told Wukong things change and gained confidence in Tang’s words. “He does seem different.” He muttered as he sat up.

“And I know you're different from when you first hired me!” Tang encouraged. “Trust me, this summer is going to be the summer of love!” Macaque made a face at that so Tang let out a nervous chuckle and scratched the back of his head. “Well it will be a good summer!”

-

At lunch Bai He made a face at the pile of asparagus Pigsy plopped on her plate. Macaque also quirked an eyebrow at the vegetable as he was handed his own plate. MK and Wukong meanwhile were making a show of how exhausted they were. Both were leaning their heads on the table and doing great impressions of their bodies being made of noodles.

“She doesn’t like asparagus.” The dark monkey said as he gestured to the young girl's plate.

“That’s cause she hasn’t had them prepared properly.” Pigsy said back, he then pointed at the parmesan covered veggie. “Trust me I’ll get you to like them.” He smiled at the girl who only poked the asparagus with her fork.

“If you don’t like them, you don’t have to eat them.” Macaque said to the young girl who smiled at him.

“She’ll like them.” Pigsy said confidently.

“And if she doesn’t, she doesn’t have to eat it.” Macaque fired back.

Pigsy went to open his mouth but Wukong piped up from where his face was squished on the table. “Agreed! Some foods are just bad.” He picked his head up and beamed at Bai He. “If you don’t like it you don’t have to try them.”

“Hey!” Pigsy shouted, and now Tang jumped in as he grabbed his own plate.

“Can you please at least take a bite Bai He?” The glasses wearing man pleaded. “Pigsy went through a lot of effort to make sure he found the best asparagus the food market had to offer.”

Bai He nodded at her butler “OK, I’ll try it.”

“Be brave sis!” MK laughed and that got a huff of frustration out of Pigsy and chuckles out of the rest.

Bai He did take a bite and ate it but then pushed the vegetable further away from her other food. “Sorry Pigsy.” She muttered quietly, not lifting her head to look at the chef. “It was good but I just-”

Macaque immediately piped in with “Nothing to be sorry about.”

“Yeah,” Wukong agreed. “You had already told him you didn’t like them.”

Both parents sent a look to the disappointed looking pig demon, their eyes both saying he better say something of comfort to their daughter or he would be dinner.

“Eh, don’t worry about it. I’ll keep trying. If you don’t like any of them then at least I can say I tried.”

“Here here.” Tang added.

Bai He grimaced at the thought of having to eat more of the vegetable she didn’t like. Macaque and Wukong shared a look then shook their heads at the stubborn chef. They all then began eating their lunches.

Macaque smiled as he ate, thinking how nice it was for him and Wukong to be on the same side; how easy that repertoire was. And unbeknownst to him, Wukong was thinking the same thing as he ate his food.

Wukong kept stealing glances at Macaque as they ate. It had felt…really nice to be on the same page with Mac. To be a united front, even with something as small as going against Pigsy about asparagus. Plus it had been nice that him and Macaque could tease each other and talk this morning. Wukong was a bit worried the ebony monkey would slink away or avoid him today, after yesterday. He won’t lie he was tempted to try and avoid Macaque himself but then with how easy Mac approached him, he found that temptation evaporate.

And having Macaque help clean his bedroom had brought up some memories. Memories of the past with both of them attempting to clean before giving up and both agreeing they’d save it for tomorrow. But tomorrow never came as they always pushed cleaning off for other things like the kids, work, or sexual activities. Till finally the mess had gotten to be too much and Pigsy would yell at them and then they’d both clean.

Thinking back to their talk while cleaning the room he couldn’t help but feel guilty. Macaque had to change, became neater since he no longer had a mansion to lounge in but an apartment. And worse, Mac had to endure hair dryers that he at one point described as masochistic torture that he was firmly not into. All because he didn’t listen…

It was weird to picture Mac in an apartment. Well that’s not wholly true, the golden monkey closed his eyes as his mind wandered. It was easy to imagine Macaque in an apartment but the image brought up only the memories of when the two of them had first moved out here on their own. They had a small apartment, the bedroom was also the living room, with a TV on their dresser at the foot of their bed. Most days were spent out and about so their small apartment was fine and never bothered them, both happy to just have a place of their own. Though Wukong covered it in plants that he and Mac had taken care of. They couldn’t sunbathe back then either, well not legally, but that didn’t stop the 2 mischievous young monkeys. They would sneak up onto the rooftop with folding chairs and sit out there and dry their fur. They even made friends with one of their elderly neighbors who kept a garden on the roof. Well Wukong made friends with her, Macaque was never as social as the golden monkey. She never minded the two drying their fur, Wukong would even help her garden sometimes. Those were good times.

He opened his eyes and glanced over to Tang. He wondered when Macaque had hired the human. He knows after the divorce they both agreed no child custody payments were needed between them, what with their arrangement with the kids. He also remembered that Macaque had gotten quite a bit of money in the divorce, nothing outrageous but what they both thought was fair. But how long did that last Macaque? His career hadn’t taken off at that point, Wukong knew that. So he had to have saved his money, made budgets and planned accordingly. The ebony monkey had always been the better planner between the two of them, Wukong flew by his intuition and whims while Macaque planned everything out. So how long was he alone with no live-in help? They had had Pigsy with them right from the first time they brought a baby MK home, so they had always had some help.

He remembers how lost he felt when the dust settled and it was him and a young MK with Pigsy. Wukong wonders briefly how that must have been, to suddenly have no help.

“I was alone! Friendless, with a baby, and in a city I barely knew! In an apartment that wasn’t my home!”

Macaque’s words from yesterday blared in his head and he shook it as his body felt weighed down with even more guilt. He had to make this right. He had to fix…this...whatever this was anymore.

Stealing another glance at Macaque he smiled as he watched the ebony monkey take the asparagus from Bai He’s plate. The young girl grinned at the monkey then got up to deposit her plate in the sink.

Macaque glanced over to Wukong and caught him staring, Wukong jolted as the dark monkey quirked an eyebrow at him.

“H-heat lamps!” Wukong blurted out, and mentally face palmed.

“What?” Mac questioned as he tilted his head.

MK who was still at the table also gave his Dad a confused look. But the golden monkey only waved his hands in the air and gestured to the ceiling.

“You know they can install heat lamps in bathrooms so you can use that instead of hair dryers.”

Macaque’s confused face slowly morphed into a touched smirk; while MK still looked confused.

“Have you been thinking of that since this morning?” Macaque questioned.

“W-well not only that!” Wukong defended. “But obviously it’s a better alternative for you!” He gestured to his own ears as a reference to Macaque’s sensitive hearing.

“Why don’t we have that?!” Pigsy grumbled as he thought back to the water puddles he had to dry this morning.

“We’re in Sunny California Pigsy! We don’t need them.” He waved off the pig demon's angry glare as MK laughed.

Meanwhile Tang nudged Macaque’s foot under the table and gave him a wink. Macaque only scoffed at the man and quickly took a bite of food to hide the smile that threatened to grow on his face.

Mei had called after lunch and wanted to come over for a pool day so Bai He and MK ran to get ready, because as MK said “when she says 10 minutes she means 7, so we better be ready to go swimming or she’ll throw us in!” Leaving the adults at the table, who were all doing some small talk before Tang tried to ramp up some flirty comments to the pig demon. Wukong and Macaque shared a look and took that as their cue to leave.

The two monkeys ended up walking to the gazebo outside and watching MK and Bai He as MK tried to blow up a giant duck floaty, while Bai He was putting on sunscreen.

“Gotta hand it to you Wukong,” Macaque began in a teasing tone. “I thought you’d run away from me the first chance you got today.”

Wukong blinked then let out a forced chuckle. “I won't lie and say the thought didn’t cross my mind, but like you said things change.”

Macaque’s tail flicked back and forth with nerves as he tried to piece together what he wanted to say here. Tang was right this was a good way to take steps forward, they just had to not screw it up.

“I talked with MK,” Wukong said looking at Macaque and waiting for the ebony monkey’s reaction. “told him I was an idiotic asshole and-”

“What did you tell him?”

“Not everything!” He rushed out sensing Macaque’s rising anger. “but I told him I was wrong before and that your anger at Tripitaka was understandable and that it was more complicated than I had described before.”

Macaque deflated as he took a seat on the porch swing inside the gazebo. “Well you got that right….so he doesn’t hate me?”

“He never hated you Mac.” Wukong said in comfort as he took a seat next to his ex. “This is just a tough situation for all of us.”

“Yeah.”

They fell into silence but both perked up when they heard the distant sound of the kids' laughter. Wukong smiled at the sound then fiddled with his shirt.

“Is…do you think Bai He is mad at me?” Wukong asked in a very self conscious tone.

“What?” Macaque quirked an eyebrow at the golden monkey that seemed to be caving into himself.

“She used to talk to me more but now I feel like I’m getting shut out. I don’t know if she’s mad at me or what, but I keep telling her she can talk to me but she just…hasn’t.”

“Ah,” Macaque nodded his head in understanding. “Bai He is usually pretty reserved. She’s probably overwhelmed with everything right now. We just have to give her time to adjust.”

“Should I have Sandy schedule some time with her?”

“Wukong she’s an 11 year old that just found out about a secret Dad and brother, Sandy’s not going to fix that.” Macaque lectured with a pointed look. “We just need to give her space.”

“Or we need to help her open up about it! We don’t want her bottling everything up.”

“Says the pot about the kettle.” Macaque whispered but the golden monkey heard him and sent his ex a glare.

“Says the other pot!” Wukong shot back and even gave Macaque’s shoulder a gentle shove.

Macaque rolled his eyes and let the comment roll off of him. “Yeah yeah we both bottle up our emotions, but-”

“But!” Wukong interrupted. “Don’t we want our kids to be better than us?!” He gestured over to where they heard the kids all playing and splashing around. “We should want them to be better than us, not repeat our mistakes.”

Macaque blinked at Wukong then flitted his eyes away, instead looking away and letting his eyes roam the expanse of land around them. Wukong seemed to take his silence as agreement or the end of the discussion as he began to mentally add “talk to Sandy” to his list of things to do today.

“In the spirit of opening up, did you…” Macaque started but then couldn’t seem to bring himself to finish. He glared at the ground as Wukong gave him a curious look.

“Did I?” Wukong pushed.

With a sigh of defeat Macaque changed his question “Why did you give all my things to Tripitaka? Or are some of them just in the garbage?”

Wukong blinked at the question then rubbed the back of his head. “Ah, no most of the things you left are with him.” Macaque nodded but kept looking at the golden monkey for an explanation. Wukong let out a forced chuckle as he looked away. “And this is going to sound childish but…I wanted to forget you. Hard to do that with pictures of you and your stuff all around. So I packed it all up and hid it away.”

“And of course you gave them to him.” Macaque growled but Wukong didn’t rise to it, he didn’t want another Tripitaka fight. Neither did Mac apparently since he didn’t push it like he normally would. Didn’t give anymore needling comments.

“Mac-”

“Wukong!” Pigsy screamed extremely loudly, causing both monkey demons to jump in surprise. They could even hear the kids immediately fall into silence ast the pig demons bellow. “Wukong!”

“Someone’s in trouble.” Macaque practically purred as he poked Wukong in the arm.

Said monkey only sighed and stood up with a groan. “What did I do now?”

“Wukong!”

“I’m coming!” Wukong yelled back, slowly making his way over to where he heard Pigsy.

Apparently he wasn’t fast enough since Pigsy raced into the gazebo, looking completely out of breath. The pig demon leaned against a wooden post in the gazebo and huffed and puffed as he tried to catch his breath. Macaque leaned more comfortably in the swing, preparing to watch the show of Pigsy yelling at Wukong.

“What’s wrong?” Wukong asked in a tone that wreaked of boredom and being 100% done with everything.

“A-Al on his way. Driving up…right now!” Pigsy huffed out and Wukong’s whole body went ramrod straight at that. Macaque went to ask what that meant but Wukong took off. In a flash the golden monkey had run towards the house and Macaque could only blink at the spot the golden monkey was before.

Chapter End Notes

So Azure is about to make his entrance, that small job did nothing to deter our lion man. Sorry Tripitaka.

Meanwhile I don’t love this chapter, I like how Mac and Wukong get some bonding in and we get a peak into their past. And there’s an inside peek into Bai He and why she’s closing herself off, but I wish it didn’t feel so choppy. Ah well!

Chapter 6: A.L.

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Wukong ran as fast as he could to the front door. He swore he left a cartoonish whirlwind in his wake but he still wasn’t quick enough. By the time he was about to reach for the front door it was opened by none other than Azure Lion.

Azure or Al as Wukong was allowed to call him, was a tall and muscular lion demon with blue skin and a bright gold mane, much more gold than his own orange tinted fur. He stood a whole head taller than the monkey now looking at him with dread.

“Wukong!” Azure boomed with a large smile.

“Al! What are you doing here?!” Wukong questioned harshly.

He had told Azure there was a family emergency and he would let him know when he could come over. And yet Azure kept calling and trying to come around. So much for Tripitaka’s small job keeping his boyfriend busy. Evidently that didn't even buy him a day!

Azure’s face fell at Wukong’s tone, but he rallied quickly. “I know you said you’re going through a family emergency and I know it has to be bad if you tried distracting me by giving me that advertising gig.” Wukong boggled at the taller man who folded his arms. “Yes I figured out you and Tripitaka pushed that job over to me to keep me away.” His tone sounded hurt and Wukong quickly ran a hand through his mane.

“Look, everythings a bit crazy right now so I just need you to leave and-”

“See that’s where you're wrong.” Azure interrupted and then continued on with a comforting tone “If there’s something going on I want, no I need to be here! If there’s something wrong with you then I should be here for you! Or worse if there's something wrong with MK and he finds out we were dating and I didn’t even try to help him!” Azure looked distraught. “I wouldn’t blame the boy for not liking me or holding it against me in the future!”

“Azure!” Wukong sighed only to be interrupted by a vase being knocked to the floor.

Whipping around and Azure looking ahead, both men spotted Tang who was now standing over the smashed remains of the vase that had been on the foyer table.

“S-sorry!” Tang stuttered as he fidgeted around the table and started edging backwards. “S-sorry! Didn’t mean to intrude. I’ll just, uh, I’ll just…”

“Tang!” Wukong tried but the glasses-wearing man took off. “Shit!” Wukong swore as he watched the man retreat.

“Who was that?” Azure questioned as he watched the man leave.

Wukong turned around and gave the lion a glare.

-

Tang was sweating bullets as he ran out of the house and into the backyard. He couldn't believe what he just heard. ‘He’s dating someone?! And I just told Mac -! Oh God!’ Tang’s thoughts spiraled as he ran. He couldn't believe what he had overheard. And now he just needed to remove himself from that situation. He had to keep his boss away from that! He had to warn Macaque! ’”Summer of love” I’m such an idiot!’ Tang reprimanded himself as he burst out into the backyard.

There he saw Macaque by the pool where the kids all were swimming. Pigsy was also with them and Macaque seemed to have a bone to pick with the pig from the way he glared at him.

“MK!” Macaque finally barked, moving closer to the pool. “Who’s Al? Pigsy won’t tell me.”

MK only looked confused “Ummm I’m not sure, I don’t know of an Al.”

“Why?” Mei asked as she swam closer to the pool's edge.

“Evidently he’s on his way here but Pigsy won’t tell me who he is.”

’Oh thank God!’ Tang thought as he trudged over to everyone, trying to make it look like he hadn’t just flew out of the house.

“Well let’s leave Wukong and this mystery Al alone.” Tang said, glancing at Pigsy who nodded.

“Agreed.”

“Why? Who is he?” MK now asked and Macaque folded his arms and glared at Pigsy, demanding an answer with his eyes. Meanwhile MK and the other kids eyes were just curious.

“Uhhh” Pigsy trailed off so Tang literally jumped in by jumping and clinging to Macaque’s arm.

“Who cares?!” He then tugged on his bosses arm. “Can I talk to you for a minute?”

“What's going on?!” Macaque was now fully suspicious of this.

First Wukong literally flew into the house, then when he casually asked Pigsy who Al was the pig demon responded with “i’m not touching this with a 10 foot pole” and walked over to the kids to avoid Mac’s trailing questions. Now Tang just came sprinting out of the house and also wanted the topic of this Al person dropped? Something was wrong.

“Oooohhhh!” Mei let out and suddenly got a comically dark look on her face. “Think he’s some creepy stalker that Wukong acquired?”

“What?!” Bai He and MK shouted at her. Macaque only blinked at the girl thinking the theory over, that would make sense.

“It has to happen right?” Mei continued. “I mean Wukong is the super star of our time! He has to have a ton of weirdos ready to stalk him.”

“But they wouldn’t make it to the mansion!” MK argued.

“Maybe that’s why Pigsy’s all defensive about it, cause he has.”

“Is that what’s happening?!” Macaque barked as he stomped to loom over the pig demon. “Is this some stalker?!” However before Pigsy could even try to answer, Macaque growled and fisted the front of Pigsy’s shirt, hefting the pig demon up. “Are my kids in danger?!”

“Baba!” Bai He and MK called then quickly got out of the pool to try and pull Macaque away from the struggling Pigsy.

“Macaque!” Tang tried to help in getting the pissed off demon to let Pigsy go. “Calm down, he’s not a stalker!”

“Then who is he?! And how do you know?!” Macaque growled as he finally released the pig demon, who quickly shoved himself away from the ebony monkey.

“Is it paparazzi?” Mei once again piped in and ignored MK’s pleading look of ‘please stop talking’.

That did not seem to quell Macaque’s anger. If anything it was the final straw holding the monkey back from storming inside.
