“Yeah! He’s actually a friend of ours.” Sandy chimed in.

“Technically he was a friend of my girlfriend I had at the time.” Pigsy elaborated, not noticing Tang’s face fall into disappointment.

However Sandy noticed and quickly tried to fix the situation. “That was Julie or something right? The girl just before your boyfriend Fen.” Tang visibly perked up at the word ‘boyfriend’ much to Sandy relief.

“Yeah I think Julie was her name. Julie or Jules.” Pigsy waved the names away as irrelevant. “Regardless, I met Tripitaka through her and we got along so well that we maintained our friendship. But anyway he wanted Wukong to be eating healthier. Don't blame him either, if that damn monkey had his way he’d be eating nothing but peaches and peach chips!” Pigsy groused. “And I thought I was a stubborn hot head, but oh boy.” Pigsy huffed out a laugh and Sandy chuckled as well. “I met my match in Wukong.”

“Oh really?” Tang asked, amused.

“Yeah! Oh we butted heads the whole time I was preparing their meal. He kept trying to fight me for the kitchen.” Pigsy recalled how he and Wukong did get into a shoving match and how he had to throw away some mixtures cause Wukong would throw his own idea of ingredients into it. They had almost come to blows before Macaque had literally dragged Wukong away. But Wukong had come back, glaring his distrustful glare at the pig demon who was trying to do his best to cook the damn ape some food. Luckily for Pigsy though he stayed in the doorway so they only bantered with words. “His face when he first bit into my meal though!” Pigsy beamed a smile of righteous victory. “Priceless!”

“Macaque had hired me on the spot and asked me to become their personal chef. I was a little confused since Tripitaka had already hired me, and this was a trial run but ‘eh I was too focused on Wukong’s reaction.” He shook his head at the memory. “He kept putting up a token fight. Don’t know who he thought he was fooling! He licked the damn plate clean!” All three men laughed.

“So you’ve been working with him for awhile then.”

“Yeah.” Pigsy nodded then a soft and serene smile graced his face. “I was there when MK was brought home.” His smile twitched higher as he recalled the memory. “So small but had the biggest smile. Melted all our hearts.” He shook his head back and forth slowly. “I knew there was no leaving after that. I’ll never leave MK.”

“I get what you mean.” Tang added as he looked down at his tea cup with his own small smile. “I’ve watched Bai He grow up and I can’t imagine not being in her life.”

Pigsy’s face soured, and he physically pushed away from the table. Tang looked up at the disgruntled pig demon then shared a concerned glance with Sandy. The blue demon reached a hand out to Pigsy cautiously.

“Pigsy if this is about Azure-”

“What?” Pigsy blinked in confusion then scrowled harder. “No! No! It’s ain’t -well ok now I’m thinking about that but I wasn’t- Ugh!” He cut himself off with a frustrated growl. “It’s just, it’s hard to hear about missing Bai He grow up cause I was there too!” He finally blurted out. His tone rising in anger and distress as he continued on. “I was there when they announced it to MK. I helped plan her homecoming! I helped with the late nights! The bottle cleanings! The diaper changes! I was there and then-” Pigsy cut himself off with a choked off cry, he raised a hand to his mouth and pointedly swallowed back his feelings.

Tang bit his lip, knowing he couldn’t offer comfort here but desperately wanting to. Looking over to Sandy he saw the blue man looking sad as well but not as devastated as Pigsy.

“Then she was gone.” Pigsy finally finished quietly. “I was never more tempted to quit than during their divorce. Those kids are the only reason I’ve stayed.” Pigsy threw his hands in the air in frustration. “And now Azure comes and he connects with her instantly?! To the point I get a lecture like I’m the bad guy!”

“You’re not the bad guy.” Tang comforted.

“She thinks I am!”

A beat of silence passed as Tang tried to think of what to say. However Sandy was the one to speak next.

“I think,” Sandy said slowly and carefully. “Bai He has difficulties socializing and thus was easy for Azure to get on her side, as he does know how to come off as everyone's friend.”

“DIfficulties socializing?” Tang tilted his head then went on to explain. “No, no you should see her back in New York! She talks with all the staff at Mac’s various work locations and with my various associates.” He fixed his glasses. “She’s quiet but she’s an observer.”

“That may be but does she talk with people her own age? Does she have any friends over?” Sandy’s tone was free of any judgment; yet Tang couldn't help but feel offended and criticized.

He wracked his brain for a memory of Bai He inviting a friend over and felt as if he was dumped in a pool of ice when he realized the answer. Seeing the answer on Tang’s face, Sandy saved him from having to answer verbally.

“Listening to her stories about camp I got the idea of her having trouble socializing with people her own age. From what you say she has no issues talking with people older than her, but with her peers she seems to have difficulties.”

Tang slumped further into his chair, his face one of devastation and failure. “I-I can’t believe I…”

“Don’t beat yourself up.” Pigsy chimed in as he took his seat again. “Kids keep secrets. We can try and figure them out but they’ll always be stuff we miss or need help spottin’.” Tang still seemed devastated so Pigsy reached over and grabbed the human’s hand, jolting the man out of his own head. “Hey, it ain’t your fault.”

“Not my-?! How did I not notice?! Of course it’s-!”

“It ain’t!” Pigsy cut him off loudly and gripped the human’s hand tighter.

“I want to stress here that nothing’s wrong with Bai He and it’s pretty common.” Sandy pipped in. “It’s just something she could use help with.”

“And it’s probably something Azure exploited, the asshole!” Pigsy cursed.

Sandy nodded in agreement as he thought it all over. “Alienating the one who often alienates themselves. A common tactic for a bully.”

Tang put his free hand to his head. “This is a lot of information to absorb.”

Pigsy gave another reassuring squeeze to the human’s hand. “We just gotta keep Azure away from MK and hope his claws in Bai He lessen. Or better yet, hope that Wukong dumps him.”

“You really don’t like him.” Tang commented.

Pigsy dropped Tangs hand and scratched the back of his own head. “Like I said he’s always the victim and I don’t trust him. He’s hiding something or lying about something, I can feel it.”

-

Pigsy glanced at the silent man in the passenger seat. Tang’s glasses made it hard to see his face but the tense silence filled the pig demon in on Tang’s mood.

“It’s really not your fault.” He voiced into the quiet.

“...I didn’t notice she had no friends.” Tang stressed, his tone reeking of failure. “I’ve taken care of her, her whole life. I’ve done her hair, we go shopping together, we team up against Mac together and yet I never once saw that she was having trouble making friends.”

“Kids are good at hiding what they aren’t ready to admit.” Pigsy held up a hand to stop Tang from saying anything further. “How do you think Wukong and I felt when MK came out to us?”

“...oh.”

“Yeah…” Pigsy re-adjusted himself as he continued driving. “Felt like an idiot looking back in hindsight but…” Pigsy didn’t finish but didn't seem to need to as Tang nodded at him.

After a beat of companionable silence, Tang said. “Thank you Pigsy.”

To which the pig demon gave a small smile. “Don’t worry about it.”

Chapter End Notes

Some freenoodleshipping content since it’s been lacking. Also some backstory to how they got to meet Macaque and Wukong! Honestly Sandy does have a backstory but it involves having sessions with MK and SWK so he just didn’t go further into detail.

Chapter 11: It's All Coming Back

Chapter Notes

This fic can be summarized by Celine Dion's song "It's all coming back" hence the chapter title.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

“Wow, you are terrible at this.” Macaque chuckled as he looked at the remains of the apple Wukong sheepishly held up.

Wukong only pouted harder but kept his failed apple bunny up high. “Shut up! It’s my first complete one.”

The two were in the kitchen, standing at the counter. After Macaque had successfully taught MK how to make the bunny treats, Wukong decided he also wanted to learn how to make “the cute looking food”. Each monkey demon had their own cutting board and knife in front of them and they had raspberries, blackberries, apples, and hot dogs all out on the countertop.

“With the fact there’s a bushel of butchered apples ready for the horses.” The dark monkey gestured to a bucket on the counter that was filled with all Wukong’s previous failures. “And this is the first one you got completely whole.” He then pointed at the attempted bunny apple. “I think it’s time to call it. You are not good at this.”

“I can learn! This is just hard!”

“Wukong how many apples are we going to go through? I mean it’s been over an hour now that we’ve been at this.”

“You have somewhere to be?” Wukong challenged to which Macaque only scoffed. Taking the win, Wukong grabbed another apple. “OK now walk me through it again.”

Macaque groaned and put his face in his hands in an exaggerated show of defeat and frustration.

“Chop chop Macaque!” Wukong said yet already began cutting the apple he had grabbed.

“Ok,” The dark monkey sighed but upon hearing the slice Wukong just made he whipped his face over to the golden monkey. “Wha- did you seriously just start off on your own?! Again?!”

“You were taking too long!” Wukong defended as he continued to cut into the apple.

“Taking too long?! It was a second!”

“It was longer than a second, you drama king.” Wukong teased as he continued to slice and attempt to carve the apple.

“Wukong stop!” Macaque groaned. “Ugh! See this is why you still can’t do it, you don’t listen to directions!”

“Your directions take too long.” Wukong sassed.

“They do not!” Macaque fired back.

“Yeah well who needs them when I can make…..THIS! All on my own!” Wukong beamed with pride as he held up another failure. Yet the golden monkey kept his persona of victory and glory.

Macaque gave the lump of apple a deadpanned and unimpressed face. Then with a blink he moved his expression over to Wukong. Staring the golden monkey down till he began to sweat.

“It’s an abstract bunny.” Wukong added weakly as a last defense.

That got a laugh to bubble out of Macaque, who only shook his head in mirth. “I’m just amazed that after, God I don’t even know how long anymore, but after so many years, we finally found something you genuinely suck at.”

“Hey!” Wukong laughed, but a feeling of pride also welled in his chest at Macaque’s words.

He always had a knack for picking up things quickly. Art, poetry, modding, gaming, writing, acting, horseback riding, etc. It never took him that long to figure things out and just…do them; and do them well.

“Remember that time with those skaters?” Wukong asked as he gathered all the apple bits to throw in the bucket for the horses.

Macaque smiled and nodded as he grabbed a clean apple and knife. “They were so pissed at you.”

“I swear the one guy was ready to crush my head.”

“Oh he definitely was.” The dark monkey agreed. “Remember how I had to throw that, what was it?”

“It was a fence!” Wukong laughed. “You threw a whole ass fence at them!”

“It wasn’t a fence.” Macaque rebutted.

“Fence or a gate! Whatever you call it. You picked it up and chucked it at them!”

“I had to! They would have beat our asses! Well technically your ass.”

“Oh no no.” Wukong jumped in with a teasing grin. “You were in on that too! You're the one who made them bet money on it. I just wanted to prove a point.”

Macaque shrugged unashamed. “Hey, I got us the money for our date later that night.”

“Our victory feast!” Wukong chuckled. “I hadn’t even stepped on a skateboard till then.”

“Yeah but I knew you’d be able to do it.” Macaque said plainly, as if it was just a common fact. And the golden monkey’s chest felt a jolt erupt within him.

“So let’s go through this one step at a time. Again.” Macaque said with a teasing grin as his tail threw an apple at the golden monkey.

“Not giving up on me?” Wukong teased as he grabbed his knife again. Macaque only shook his head, not seeing Wukong’s small and secret smile before the golden monkey shook his head and screwed his face up, catching himself.

He may or may not be experiencing jolts, shocks really. And they come up sometimes and Wukong knows they’re all around Macaque. But it’s just memories from the past, and Macaque’s different approach to things now versus the past. Those things are causing the jolts. That’s part of the reason he had all Macaque’s things moved to Tripitaka. He wants to avoid all that, and now being unable to avoid it -these shocks are just popping up. And he reacts to them and so he just has to watch how we reacts to them. Because Al is here! And MK, and Bai He. So he can’t be distracted by these things. They belong in the past and he just had to move forward.

“-So that’s why we don’t cut the…” Macaque looked over to Wukong and startled at him. “Are you serious?!” He slammed a palm to the countertop. “Have you not been listening?!”

“I was!”

Macaque’s glare only intensified so Wukong backed down. “OK I may not have been listening.”

“UGH!” Macaque made to throw the apple at Wukong’s head but the golden monkey ducked and then grabbed a handful of raspberries and threw them at the dark monkey.

The raspberries hit, leaving juice marks on Mac’s fur and face. “Oh its on!” Macaque exclaimed and raced to where Wukong was on the other side of the counter.

The golden monkey let out an “eep” as he ran to avoid Mac. Both monkeys ended up laughing as Mac got his own hit in with a cold hot dog. They danced around the kitchen throwing some food bits at each other and laughing as they ducked and weaved. It escalated when Wukong dove into the pantry to avoid a barrage of apple bits.

Macaque was smirking in victory, he armed himself with two fistfulls of various food items that they had been working with (raspberries, blackberries, hot dogs, and apples). Lifting them up he had his aim set for the door.

“Come out Wukong, you can’t hide in there forever.” He taunted.

“Oh I’m not hiding!” Wukong laughed with the tone of assured victory. The door then burst open with a plume of white exploding outwards.

Macaque boggled but could do little else as he, along with a good portion of the kitchen and kitchen appliances, were doused in flour. In the wake, Wukong’s laughter was so loud, Mac swore it caused his ears to ring. Yet all he could do in response was smile as the shock of the flour avalanche wore off.

“Y-Y-You look-” Wukong stuttered as he was laughing to hard to properly get air into his chest. He doubled over, slapping some of the flour that landed on his own legs. With heavy laughter he walked closer to the now white monkey.

“O-oh! Oh man!” He wheezed and Mac’s shoulders also shook in repressed laughter. With hands shaking from mirth Wukong reached up and wiped a heavy amount of flour from Macaque’s face.

Macaque blew out a breath, causing a puff of flour to fly off his face. Both monkeys cracked up at that. Wukong rocked back at the ridiculousness of it all while Macaque laughed in place. Wukong rocked back towards the flour doused monkey and open his mouth to tout his victory, when a handful of now flour covered berries and hot dogs pieces smacked him in the face and was pressed into his fur.

WIth a squawk he tried to flee but Macaque was quicker and brought up his other fistful of ammunition. He grabbed Wukong around the waist and proceed to rub the flour covered food bits all over the twisting and giggling monkey.

Finally Wukong was able to free himself and he jogged through the flour covered floor to put some distance between him and Mac. Turning back the two stared at each other. Macaque was completely white, flour covering his entire front and top of his head. Wukong meanwhile had smears of berry juice all over his face, hair, neck, and arms. His shirt and upper back where the shirt didn’t cover were also covered in berry juice. Bits of hotdog and apple also peppered his fur. Blowing out his nose a piece of hotdog flew out. Both monkeys stared at the hot dog piece as it landed on the flour with a puff of white, before bursting out laughing.

-

“Uhhh what are you guys-” Pigsy asked as he spotted Tang, MK, and Bai He all seemingly frozen in the hallway. They were hugging one of the walls and leaned close to each other -as if whispering to each other.

He was quickly silenced as MK rushed and smashed a single finger against his face, lips, and cheeks. All while whispering “shhh” right in Pigsy’s now frustrated face.

“Kid, what?” He said quietly though it must not have been quiet enough. Tang and Bai He also rushed him and each sternly held up a single finger in the way that meant ‘be quiet’.

His confused expression was broken by the sound of booming laughter. His eyebrows shot up as all the others turned to the noise like rabbit ears honing in on a stick breaking. Smiles broke out on the three human’s faces and they all shared happy looks. Pigsy placed the basket full of fruit down as gently as possible then asked the humans as quietly as he could.

“Is that Wukong and Macaque?”

He was met with various whispered affirmations and he couldn’t stop the smile from gracing his face as he looked at the pure happiness on MK and Bai He’s face.

“They’ve been bonding all morning!” Tang explained with a grin, but then was physically pushed to the side by MK.

“First they were bickering, you know, mocking each other and giving each other grief.” The excited boy added.

Bai He then bounced into Pigsy’s vision and further explained. “But then they started laughing and teasing and now listen to them!”

They all turned their heads to where they could still hear the monkey demons cracking up at whatever antics they were up to. Azure had work so he wasn’t able to come over today, all the better in Pigsy’s opinion. But it gave everyone a day to chill and truly do their own thing and decompress from the always present tension when the blue lion is around.

“Wonder what’s got them laughing so hard?” Pigsy pondered.

“Who knows.” Tang shrugged. “They’ve been in there for a while.”

After a second of everyone straining their ears to hear more; MK ran his hands down his face and put on an expression of faux anguish. “I so badly wish I could take a peak and see!”

“I probably could.” Bai He offered. “But they’re in the kitchen so-”

-

The monkeys laughter died down with only a bit of trailing giggles, mainly coming from Wukong. Macaque looked down at himself and shook his head, then groaned as that caused more flour to rain down. Wukong broke into giggles again as he made his way closer to Macaque.

“You know this,” He gestured to Macaque. “really reminds me of when we were young.”

It took only a second before the dark monkey connected the dots and he almost nodded his head in agreement but held back, not wanting more flour to rain down on himself.

“Yeah,” He raised his arms up and explained how white they were. “Yeah you’re right.” He let out a breath through his nose. “But I wasn’t this white!”

“I don’t know.” Wukong hedged as he grabbed one of the raised arms. “With the black fur showing through it’s almost the right shade.”

“Oh right.” Mac rolled his eyes and pulled his arm away. “Like you remember my old fur better than I do.”

In their youth, when they were young, very young, Macaque had had white fur instead of black. But after an accident, Mac’s light colored fur had fallen out and was permanently replaced with the dark fur he now had.

“I remember it was like the moon but on a thinly clouded night.” Wukong mused as he recalled the ebony’s monkey old coat and how he used to stand out against any and all backdrops (the night sky, sunset, sunrise, etc). His white fur and six multicolored ears always captured everyone's attention. “Not a stark white, but not dull at all and definitely not something that could be ignored. It was like moonlight, that’s why I call you my”

Looking up to Mac’s face he was arrested by Macaque’s startled and embarrassed expression. The flour covered monkey’s eyes were wide, a blush shining through the flour still on his face, and his six ears were fanned out at the side of his head, colors shining through the flour coating.

“...Moon.” He finally finished in a whisper.

Wukong sucked in a breath as he felt another shock erupt within him. And then the reality of what he said hit him. A blush was quickly covering his face as well. So he opened his mouth to say something to try and derail…whatever this was before it could go further. Yet nothing came out. His mouth just hung open and he felt the same way he did when he first saw Macaque arrive here this summer. Dumbfounded, and as if in a trance he leaned closer to the six eared monkey who also leaned closer to him.

“IN MY KITCHEN?!”

Pigsy’s scream of rage jolted both monkeys out of whatever trance they had been in. Simultaneously, both monkeys turned towards where the scream could be heard. Then they shared a look and grimaced.

“Shit.” They both sighed, knowing there was no way to avoid their fate.

“If we book it out the back, we can hop in the pool-” Macaque reasoned, making to go for the back door but was stopped by Wukong grabbing his arm.

“Running will make it worse.” Wukong sighed, mentally preparing himself for what was to come.

And come it did. Pigsy and the three humans trying to hold him back made it to the open family dining room that connected to the kitchen. Upon seeing the absolute mess the two monkey demons had made. The three humans quickly let Pigsy go, so that they would not have to face any of the pig demons fury.

One long rage filled tirade from Pigsy later. Wukong and Macaque had been literally hosed down by said pig demon. Who wouldn’t let them go take a proper bath but blasted them with the garden hose. Now Wukong and Macaque were in the kitchen cleaning and complaining.

“Sweeping isn’t doing anything!” Wukong complained. “I’m just moving it around!”

“Then do it better!” Pigsy snapped. He refused to leave the two monkeys alone in his precious kitchen again.

“Let’s just call a maid service.” Macaque groaned as he leaned against the appliance he was taking apart in order to clean all the parts the got covered in their food fight.

“Oh I called them, they’re coming. But till they get here you two are cleaning your mess!” Pigsy barked

“What?!” Wukong and Macaque both glared at the pig demon.

“If they’re coming then why are we-” Wukong tried but was interrupted by Pigsy screaming and glaring the two defiant monkey’s down.

“Because this is your punishment, you morons!” He screamed, and Macaque winced at the volume of it; Wukong merely cringed away at the chastising. “Didn’t think I’d have to tell grown adults that you shouldn’t have food fights, but look who I’m talking to!” He pointed viciously at the two demon monkeys. “You two think you just get do this and get off scot free?! In MY kitchen?!”

“Technically-” Macaque began but was effectively silenced by Pigsy’s harsh glare.

“Don’t even think of pulling the “he started it” debate!” He crossed his arms. “I don’t care who started it!”

Macaque smirked and threw a conspiratorial look at Wukong; who quirked an eyebrow but then smirked as well and nodded to the dark monkey. Macaque put down the appliance part he was cleaning and leaned against the flour covered countertop.

“I was going to say,” He paused dramatically, earning an eye roll from the chef. “that technically this isn’t your kitchen.” Macaque’s tone was his needling tone, one usually reserved for Wukong but now it was directed at Pigsy and the pig demon rose to the challenge.

However, just as Pigsy was about to refute Macaque’s claim, Wukong spoke up. His tone lack-a-daisy.

“Your right Macaque. Technically this isn’t your kitchen Pigsy.” The death glare of the pig demon was on the golden monkey. “And come to think of it,” Wukong tapped his chin in a fake ponder. “I don’t remember ever saying we had a house rule against food fights, do you Mac?”

“Can’t say we did.” Macaque agreed with a grin.

Meanwhile the vein in Pigsy’s forehead was about to burst with how done he was with these two. “That’s not something you say! It’s something you just know!”

“Now-” However Macaque didn’t get a chance to finish before Pigsy rounded on him and ran towards the dark monkey.

“Out of my kitchen!” The chef roared.

With a grin Macaque lept away from the charging pig demon and ran towards Wukong. The golden monkey jumped in place then promptly dropped his broom and ran with the dark monkey out of the kitchen. Pigsy meanwhile grabbed the broom and shook it after the two retreating monkeys.

“You good for nothing menaces!” Pigsy’s screamed at the retreating monkeys, who laughed as they ran.

The two ran outside the home, and only stopped once they saw MK and Bai He. MK was drawing on a sketch pad while Bai He was writing letters, presumably to her friends she made at camp. The fathers made their way over to the kids, while congratulating themselves on their get away.

“Hey Dad, Hey Baba!” Bai He greeted but then tilted her head at them. “I thought you guys were cleaning?”

“We were.” Wukong nodded and then shared a dark grin with Mac. “But, ah, we got thrown out.”

“You made him madder?!” MK asked, with the tone of one questioning a person's sanity.

“We pointed out some flaws in his logic and it may have angered him more.” Macaque added.

MK shook his head at the lost causes that were his parents. He then turned back to his drawing. “If it comes down to it, I’m on Team Pigsy.”

“Same!” Bai He agreed with zero hesitation as she went back to writing.

“Hey!” Both monkeys squawked.

“His food’s amazing, so I’m staying on his good side.” Bai He explained in a matter-of-fact tone, not even sparing the monkeys a glance.

“I think you guys are nuts.” MK said bluntly. “When he saw what you guys did to the kitchen I thought his head would explode.” He made hand gestures of an explosion to which his sister nodded in agreement. “And now you made him angrier?” The young man shook his head as if in disappointment. “I’m out.”

“The betrayal.” Wukong gasped dramatically while clutching his chest.

“Hey we’re not cleaning anymore so I’m taking the win.” Macaque boasted.

“Small victory when you’re gonna lose the war.” Bai He chimed in with a grin as she ignored her Baba’s glare.

“Yee of little faith.” Wukong fired back while clasping a hand on Mac’s shoulder. “We’ve faced down Pigsy’s wrath and lived to tell the tale! We-”

“Are so dead when it comes to dinner time.” MK cut in.

“Hope you aren’t hungry.” Bai He added.

“Oh don’t worry.” Macaque said with confidence. “I have a plan for dinner.” Wukong gave Macaque a curious look to which Macaque mouthed ‘you’ll see’. Wukong nodded, figuring he’d follow Mac’s lead when the dinnertime came around.

-

“Wukong started it!”

“What?! I did NOT!”

Pigsy groaned loud and long as Macaque and Wukong began firing back and forth about who started the food fight. Bai He, MK, and Tang were all at the table, their plates loaded with the delicious food Pigsy had created. Tang shook his head like a tired parent, while the two kids only shared a look before shrugging and eating their food.

“Enough!” Pigsy barked and then waved the two monkeys over to the table. “Go eat. But you're both doing dishes tonight!” Pigsy made to sit down and then gave the monkeys one last glare and finger wag. “And no lip about it either!”

After dinner Wukong grumbled under his breath as he and Macaque brought all the dishes to the sink.

“Well that worked out quite well.” Macaque mused sounding proud.

“What do you mean?” The golden monkey asked warily.

“Wukong.” The dark monkey gestured to the now empty table. “We got to eat dinner and only got stuck with dish duty.” He rolled his hands, gesturing for Wukong to follow his line of thinking.

Which after a brief second the golden monkey did pick up on what Macaque was explaining but not saying. “This was your plan.” He said in awe.

“Yes.” Macaque grinned.

“Huh.” Wukong smiled and then let out a breath of laughter. “Man, I forgot how you always think 12 steps ahead.”

Macaque quickly turned away and fast walked back to grab the remaining plates and silverware on the table. Wukong looked curious as the quick retreat but then went to begin washing the dishes.

After a few minutes of steadying himself from the unexpected praise, Macaque cleared his throat and said with a tone of forced airiness. “You're really doing a number on your compliment of the day punch card.”

“Huh?” Wukong turned to look at the dark monkey, but Macaque’s back was to him. “Well I wasn’t really…” But he stopped himself. Because it would be weird otherwise, wouldn't it? The complement of the day would give him and Mac an out. No, an explanation. Not an out. Nothing was here for there to be a need to be an out. They were divorced. He was dating Al, he just had to be nice to Macaque. Deal with him for the remainder of summer and then…

“I wasn’t doing it for that.” Wukong said slowly.

Macaque slowly turned to look at Wukong over his shoulder and he saw the golden monkey standing stiff as a board at the sink. Wukong was staring resolutely into the sink, and Macaque took a slow breath. With a bit of hope and a flicker of another emotion he would not name, Macaque breathed. “Oh?”

“Yeah,” The golden monkey said slowly, hesitantly. “I…I” Then in the splash of a second, Wukong shook his head to the right and then let out a ‘tsk’ and Macaque’s shoulders fell “I’m just pointing out facts.” He said matter-of-factly and then began scrubbing with vigor. “Plus everyone deserves a compliment every once in awhile right? No Sandy rule required.”

’Coward.’

Macaque rolled his shoulders and brought the remaining items to the sink. “True enough.” He agreed.

The two then did the rest of the dishes in a silence neither would admit was tense.

-

Azure arrived first thing in the morning the next day. At first he was overjoyed to see Wukong and Macaque avoiding each other. But the longer the day went on the more he felt the tension, specifically just between Macaque and Wukong. It was gone when everyone else was around but if it was just them or just them near each other; there was tension. And it had a different vibe than from any other time Azure observed them. Azure could read it in the body language, the eye movement, the way they spoke as if carefully picking their tone of voice and words. And worst of all, in how they touched each other. Before it was more unconscious. A hand on the shoulder here, an elbow nudge there, a pat or slap on the arm when necessary. But now, there was a hesitance, a conscious thought of where Wukong or Macaque would touch the other monkey demon that wasn’t there before.

It was the same the next day, and the day after that. If anything, it irritated the lion more as time went on. Because not only was there this tension and hesitance but there were these moments.

These moments where Macaque would make both his own and Wukong’s coffee without being asked. And the morning when Wukong told Pigsy to let Macaque sleep in. And there was the dinner where Wukong had just glanced up at Macaque, then left the table and came back with a glass of wine for the dark monkey; and only after serving Mac did he ask Al if he wanted anything. Or when Macaque threw a peach over to Wukong upon returning from the grove, and without missing a beat, the golden monkey caught it. No prompting or warning. Just a delayed noise of surprise from Bai He. Yet Wukong just caught the peach as if expecting it to be tossed to him after hearing the door open. Azure feared he knew what these were; habits from when the two were married. And worse, they were returning. Old habits from when they were together blossoming up again due to their proximity.

Whenever the two seemed to notice they had fallen into these old habits they seem to both hunch inwards and then the tension would double down. And Azure knew what all this meant. Something happened. Something changed or shifted between the two ex-husbands. And he didn’t like it.

So he believed it was time for this family reunion to end.

Chapter End Notes

So I was having the hardest time with the pacing for this chapter and the fic in general after Chap 10 for some reason. I wrote and rewrote this chapter then finally scrapped it and started from scratch and didn’t follow my skeleton structure at all and honestly I love it XD lol So good news there.

Bad news is since I ditched my skeleton structure it's gonna be a roller coaster going forward. Also lmao at me who thought this would be 7 chapters total and then 12 chapters total. I’m such a fool lol

Chapter 12: It All Fell Down

Chapter Notes

Trigger warning for past sexual abuse in this chapter. There’s nothing explicit but it’s mentioned in this chapter so please take care of yourselves.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Macaque looked out at the sprawling grass from his spot on the outdoor swing. He casually pushed himself with his feet while sipping on some lemonade MK had made with Tang and Pigsy. Bai He and Wukong had left not too long ago. In disguise; if you called a hat, hoodie, baggy clothes to stuff a tail in, and sunglasses a disguise. Though it was cute to see Bai He match Wukong’s disguised look with her own baggy clothes, hat, and sunglasses. The two had headed out for some incognito shopping.

Taking a sip of his lemonade, Macaque felt lighter inside as he reminisced on Wukong’s past words.

”that’s why I call you my…Moon.”
“Why I call you my…Moon.”
“Call you my…Moon.”
“Call you.”

He had said ‘call you’. Present tense.

His heart felt like it did a flip even as he tried to stamp it down. But it had to mean something. Even with Azure Lion, maybe this means something. Lately there’s been…something between them. Even Wukong can’t deny it. They haven’t touched it but from the glances they’ve shared and looks they each caught the other giving.

Macaque knew that Wukong knew there was something. It didn't have a name yet. But something was happening. It made hope flare up in Macaque even as he tried (admittedly half heartedly) to stomp it down.

“Macaque?” Azure’s voice greeted as he walked across the lawn towards the dark monkey. He had the air about him of someone just walking around their own home. It pissed Macaque off, but he made sure not to show it. “I was just looking for you.”

“Oh?”

Azure nodded in reply as he took a seat next to the dark monkey. The wooden swing creaked with his added weight. The lion demon also held a glass of lemonade and held his up in comradery inclining his head towards Macaque’s own glass. Macaque lifted his glass and clinked it against the lion demon’s in a cheers.

“I was just thinking how wonderful it’s been having both MK and Bai He here.” Azure smiled as he looked over towards the house. “So lively, and the kids really seem to love it here.” Macaque hummed in agreement. “I mean Bai He has really opened up here. She seems to be thriving.”

Macaque tensed up and used all the power within him to restrain himself from whipping his head towards the blue lion. He took offense to Azure’s wording and Macaque wasn’t fool enough to think Azure didn’t know that would cause a reaction.

“She does seem to enjoy it.” He said as evenly as possible. His tail tense and angry behind him.

“Yes,” Azure nodded, but still didn’t look back at Macaque. “and that brings me to my or.” He chuckled as if he had misspoken “Or should I say our idea.” He stressed the word our and paused afterwards. Macaque clenched his jaw but didn’t react other than that. He would not fall for these word games, he wouldn’t give Azure the reaction he was desperately poking for.

“See with Bai He doing so well here. And having such great connections with Wukong and myself.” The lion gestured to himself and now turned to look at Macaque again. “Don’t you think it’s best she stay here?” Macaque stared the blue lion down but Azure didn’t flinch. He kept the deceitfully friendly looking smile on his face as he elaborated. “She’s made friends with Mei and talked with her friends from camp here. And from our talks.” Azure drops his voice to a whisper and Macaque grinds his teeth in anger at the implication that he knew more than Mac did. “She has a lot more confidence here.”

Macaque breathed deeply through his nose and did a hard blink as he slowly let it out. He then looked back at Azure and said as evenly as possible, fighting to keep the anger out of his voice.

“So you want her to stay here?”

“Oh!” Azure put a hand to his chest as if in offense. “It’s not about what I want, but what’s best for Bai He.”

A visceral anger flared up in Macaque at the patronizing tone, at the assumption that this man -who just met Bai He- knew his daughter more than him, and knew what was best for her over him. He couldn’t stop the glare and growl that came out of him as he snapped back.

“I know what’s best for my daughter!”

“I never said you didn’t.” Azure said innocently and raised his free hand in placation, but his eyes spoke of victory. “But,” he tilted his head “Bai He’s said she isn’t comfortable talking with you.”

Macaque’s expression shattered. He knew his daughter and him had never gotten into deep talks. But he assumed that she was like him and kept everything close to the chest.

“Whereas she’s very comfortable talking with Wukong and myself.” Azure continued and Macaque centered himself enough to glare openly at the lion demon. “So wouldn’t the best thing for her be staying here? Where she’s confident and comfortable?”

“In that case then MK should come back to New York with me.” Macaque smiled viciously. “He and I communicate openly.” He then gave Azure a smug and needling smile. “Whereas you and MK are what? Surface level friendly?” Azure’s open hand clenched into a fist and Macaque smirked. “It’s obvious he doesn’t wish to be alone with you. What with how he’s been avoiding you lately.”

Azure’s face dropped and he pursued his lips before shaking his head and taking a long sip on the lemonade. Macaque meanwhile enjoyed his victory. Two could play this game and Macaque would win it. Suddenly, Azure lifted his head, clicked his teeth and seemed to rally himself.

He spoke slowly as if wading out on thin ice. “MK’s at such an impressionable age.” Macaque’s mind whizzed with theories as to where Azure was taking this, and he came up with rebuttals of all of them. “It may not be best for him to be alone with you.” The blue lion turned to Macaque. His eyes flitted all around the monkey, studying him, looking for a reaction. “Given how you rose to fame.”

Macaque kept an even stare with the blue lion but quirked an eyebrow in bewilderment. “Through Broadway?” He asked rhetorically.

Azure kept his analytical eye on Macaque but seemed to change the subject all together. “Have you ever heard of Baigujing? She’s known for playing the stoic, or one may say, ice demoness roles?”

“Yeah,” Macaque answered gruffly, defensive as to why Azure changed the subject to her. “She’s the recurring villain, the Lady Bone Demon on that hero franchise.”

Azure nodded but again kept his eyes on Macaque, searching for…something the dark monkey was unsure of. A reaction presumably, but he couldn’t guess what that actress had to do with him.

“I met her at a gala.” Azure waved his hand dismissively. “Her latest, hmm, how to put this…paramore.” He finally settled on. “And I talked a lot that night. And then we met up again.”

At this point Macaque was drinking some of his lemonade. He kept his hackles up but was still lost as to why Azure changed the topic to this.

“He’s quite the talker when drunk.” Azure chuckled and Macaque rolled his eyes. “I’ll admit his constant smile was a bit unsettling. Though I guess that’s leftover from his life in politics.” Azure was speaking pointedly again and eyeing Macaque for any reaction.

The dark monkey merely turned away and instead stared back at the house and lawn. Giving off the air of boredom.

“I can’t recall his name but I’m sure you could, after all he used to be the mayor of New York.” Macaque tensed immediately but quickly tried to make it seem as if he was tensing in boredom and lingering frustration. Yet, like a shark smelling blood in the water, Azure seemed to have noticed because his tone became more sure, more pointed.

“He told me all about how he was a” the lion demon let out a derisive chuckle. “Patron of the arts.” Macaque didn’t move, he sat still as a stone. Which seemed to only encourage Azure. “He mentioned how he had an allure for those with…” Azure made a show of looking at the side of Macaque’s head, at 3 of his 6 ears. “Unique features. And that his past conquest was quite. Oh what would be the best word to describe all he told me..…adventurous! To say the least.” The monkey demon felt the tendrils of panic try and overcome him, but he stomped them down. Desperately trying to show no reaction, to not shake, to not twitch, to not move. “Conquest may not be the right word though, right? Since it wasn’t that he conquered you but bought you, yes?” Macaque’s hand clenched on the glass of lemonade as he felt his entire body seize up.

Azure knew. He knew.

Macaque couldn’t do anything about the shame immediately rushing to drown him. He tried to rebuff it like he does when those thoughts would creep in and attempt to haunt him. He did what he had to. He needed a leg in and the now ex-Mayor offered him that.

In a blink, Macaque was back in his first apartment in New York. It wasn’t bad, or terribly small, but it wasn’t the sprawling mansion with rolling hills he had grown used to. It was modest, affordable as he didn’t want to spend all his money at once. But the worst part about it was it was filled with boxes because it was official; Wukong and him were divorced. The papers were signed and the children split, yet it still didn’t feel real. Macaque still thought Wukong would pick him over his career. Wukong would realize he needed Macaque, just like Macaque needed him; and he’d be in the front row of Macaque’s first leading performance. Because he had to have looked it up, looked into the role that made Macaque leave.The role and play he sacrificed his marriage for, the hill he died on.

Macaque put everything into that character. The play itself had a mediocre script and storyline, but it was Macaque’s first leading role and he was determined to shine and to make this play the best. But he failed. The play did not sell well and was pulled earlier than scheduled. The producers and directors decided to use him as a scape goat. For even though he performed well and got great reviews. They had disclosed in interviews and with other directors and producers that Macaque was the issue of the play, the reason it failed. They said he demanded too much, was too difficult to work with, would try to change everything, and worse that he was just an attention seeker, that he sabotaged so he could hog the spotlight. They said they shouldn’t have taken a chance on him but stayed with the well known performers. It was hell. Macaque had tried to fight back, do his own interviews to clear his name but was mostly ignored.

He remembered completely breaking down one night. Sobbing, screaming, and even destroying some things in his apartment. He was lost in his despair. Everything he did, worked for and sacrificed for, gone in one play. He was so deep in his own sorrows it took him awhile to realize he was not the only one crying. His infant daughter was wailing in her crib, in her bedroom. The guilt that washed over him as he raced over to comfort his daughter was immeasurable. He didn’t even know how long she had been crying. That night he thought he had truly lost it all; yet it seemed destiny had other plans.

A certain ever smiling politician had, evidently, seen the play and became quite the fan. The Mayor had approached him with a deal. Macaque had turned him down. Yet the Mayor persisted to the point of annoyance and Macaque locking the doors quickly behind him on several occasions. But failed audition after failed audition started to get to Macaque. He was at an all time low mentally and emotionally. It was after another, presumably, failed audition where they had barely wanted to give him a chance in the first place, that he gave in and made the deal.

The Mayor was clear that he did not want a relationship, he wanted sex. He would call in his favors and lobbyists that had ties with Broadway and get Macaque’s career back on the map. In return Macaque was his latest boytoy. Anytime he wanted sex Macaque would have to come and do whatever the politician wanted him to do. They also had standing sex days and times; where Macaque would come to his office at City Hall for whatever the politician was in the mood for.

Macaque and Wukong had been adventurous in their sexual escapades so the dark monkey had thought he would be able to handle whatever the politician could think of. Turns out Macaque had some limits he wasn't aware of. Eventually Macaque's career did take off thanks to the Mayors influence. He was able to get multiple lead roles and the rumors of him being a spotlight hogging diva died out.

They parted eventually and Macaque had never told anyone about their deal. Mentally he aligned what happened with the mayor as a friends with benefits arrangement. Not to say he wanted to dwell on his time with the man, but he did what he needed to do.

And now Azure knew.

“Crass as he is, you should be happy to know he didn’t name drop you.”

A small beat of relief was felt before he internally began beratinging himself. That meant that Azure only suspected it was him and Macaque had confirmed it with his reactions. He grit his teeth and let out an exhale through his nose.

“But with MK being at such an impressionable age” The fake concern coming from Azure pissed the dark monkey off. “I don’t think it would be in his best interest to be alone with someone who slept their way into their career.”

“Like you’re doing with Wukong?” Macaque barked back, finally moving as he bodily turned to confront Azure. His glare accusing as he took in the smug lion demon.

“I adore Wukong.”

“Name three things you adore,” Macaque spat the word back at Azure. “About him that aren’t his money, connections, and status!”

“I don’t have to explain anything!” Azure fired back, getting angry and defensive.

“You’re doing the same thing just calling it a different name. You adore the star Sun Wukong, you don’t care about him!”

“And you’re just deflecting!” Azure growled. “You’re leaving today or I’ll tell your kids all about how you sold yourself for fame!” The two continued to glare each other down before Azure let out a triumphant huff of breath and leaned back against the swing. “Though it’s late in the day to get a flight.” Macaque felt as if he could break the glass in his hand with how hard he was squeezing it. “I’ll give you till tomorrow afternoon.” Azure said in a tone of one giving a gracious gift.

Azure rose and seemed nonplussed by the glare Macaque was continuing to give him. He finished his lemonade with a last large gulp, and afterwards let out a refreshing and exaggerated “Ah!” He turned back to Macaque with a look of victory. “When I come back tomorrow I expect you to be gone.”

-

“You said you needed me? Wait, why are you packing?” Tang asked as he came into Macaque’s bedroom.

The dark monkey looked like a feral cat. His fur was bristled, shoulders hunched high, and he was erratically and with force throwing clothes by the handful over to his suitcase that was messily opened on the floor. All while he growled and muttered curses under his breath.

“Get in here and close the door!” The dark monkey barked and Tang jumped to obey. As soon as the door was closed Macaque gave another demand. “You need to confess to Pigsy!”

“WHAT?!”

“Publicly!” Macaque punctuated with another forceful fist of clothes going towards the suitcase. “Make a big scene of how-”

“Woah! Now wait-” Tang tried but Macaque kept going as if he didn’t hear him.

“You can’t leave him and need to be near him-”

“I’m not-”

“I’ll still pay you so you don’t have to worry about that.” Macaque assured in a brisk tone as he moved onto the bathroom to grab the toiletries he had brought.

“That’s not what’s worrying me!” Tang tried again, his own face and body language becoming frantic.

“And while you're here you need to watch out for Azure!” Macaque instructed as he came out with some shampoo bottles and firmly pointed at Tangs chest.

“Azure?” Tang tilted his head but then quickly grabbed Macaque’s wrist in a vice grip. “Explain!” Macaque looked ready to fight but Tang pointedly sat on the bed, still gripping his boss's wrist so Macaque had to follow. “Macaque…talk to me. What’s going on here?”

Macaque looked off to the side in anger and frustration. He didn’t know how to explain this, how would he even begin to tell Tang about Azure’s blackmail? Then his eyes landed on a magazine laying on the top of one of the bedroom dressers. It was something Macaque had bought in the airport to occupy his time with the crossword, not that he ever finished it. But looking at that and back to Tang he was hit with a flashback of another time Tang had said those exact words to him.

They were living together at the time. It was late in the night, maybe around 2 or 3 am. And so Macaque was working on his scrapbook of his son. He always did it at night so as to make sure he wasn’t caught. Sometimes he found joy in it. Decorating the pages with stickers and annotations while carefully securing pictures of MK in the pages. However it could also be hard, knowing this was the closest he would get to MK.

That night had been one of the harder ones. Macaque heard Tang’s door open but could barely hide what he was doing. Tang had looked so caught off guard seeing Macaque so emotional, and attempting to hide it under bravado and anger.

But Macaque had opened up to him and had explained everything. And Tang had been a needed shoulder to cry on. The next day Tang silently slipped Macaque a magazine he had purchased with a picture of MK in it.

“Azure’s blackmailing me into leaving.” He confessed as he sat next to the glasses wearing man.

“Huh?” Tang gasped. “With what?”

Macaque took a deep breath and released it slowly. Resolutely not looking at Tang the dark monkey asked. “You remember how we met?”

“Yes but what does that have to do with anything?”

“You may have guessed it but the Mayor and I, we had a…a sort of…”

“You were dating.” Tang said gently.

“No!” The monkey nearly shouted, making Tang lean away on instinct. “No, we were not dating!” Tang raised an eyebrow but Macaque kept studying the floor. “We had a deal.” He spat the word. “He got me into Broadway and I gave him…whatever.” He muttered.

“Whatever” Tang silently mouthed, and then it hit him the reality of what that meant. “Oh!” He breathed. “Oh Mac.”

“Azure’s demanding Bai He and MK stay here and I leave!” He growled and then turned to look at Tang. “I can’t leave them alone with him! I won’t!” An actual growl followed his declaration. “So I need you to confess to Pigsy in front of everyone. That way you can stay here, with your budding relationship, and watch him around the kids!”

“Ok, ok, ok!” Tang chanted as he rubbed his temples. “But then what?” Tang’s eyes danced back and forth as he tried to imagine how they’d handle what came next. He also tried not to dwell too deeply on the upcoming confession he’ll have to do. “I can’t live here forever! And if he’s blackmailing you now he’ll try and find more and just!” Tang threw his hands up “Customize everything to his liking!”

“I’m going to go digging!” Macaque said with determination. “I’m not the only one with baggage, especially in this industry.” He scowled. “I just have to find it and if we just offset our blackmail over each other then so be it!”

“Or-”

“No!” Macaque cut in immediately, startling Tang. “There is no ‘or’ here Tang.”

“But-”

“We’re not telling Wukong or the kids about this! I didn’t even want to tell you!” He finished with a note of finality and Tang slumped inwards and nodded.

“I’ll call to let you all know I made it back and then, when you're alone” He stressed. “Call me and give me an update on…” Macaque swallowed hard and took a breath as the reality of him leaving MK and Bai He behind came closer and closer to the forefront of his mind.

Tang seemed to understand this and placed a comforting hand on Macaque’s shoulder. “I will.” He vowed.

-

“What’s with the bags?” MK asked as he stood in the hallway of their front entrance.

Tang gave a grimace as he glanced between the teenager’s fearful face and Macaque’s taunt back. Tang and Macaque had just put the two large suitcases of Macaque’s things by the front door. They had tried to be as silent as possible so they could get everything ready. Once Wukong and Bai He came home Macaque would have a meeting with everyone and tell them about him having to leave. But MK found them all the same. The dark monkey took a steadying breath then turned with a forced smile on his face.

“I” He swallowed hard but kept the false smile up. “I have to go back to New York.”

“What?” MK breathed, the word literally leaving him in a gasp as his body slumped and face fell into confused sadness. “Wha-what? What? Why?!”

“It’s complicated but-”

“So you're just going to take Bai He and go as soon as she gets back?!” MK accused, his confusion quickly turning into anger. The dark monkey shook his head but MK barrelled over him. “Why didn’t you tell us?! Do you have a job or something?!”

“I…I can’t get into it MK!” Macaque tried to sound firm but he kept waffling and MK could sense it.

“What do you mean you can’t get into it?!” MK asked harshly, he would not let his Baba off with noncommittal answers. “Why are you leaving?!” He shouted and Macaque flinched and physically turned his face from the boy.

“What’s going on over here?” And that voice made Macaque growl, something that MK picked up on but didn’t comment on. Instead the boy only gave Azure a passing glance, spying Pigsy also coming up behind the blue lion.

“Baba’s leaving and apparently only told Mister Tang!” MK gestured strongly to Tang who jumped at the accusation.

The glasses-wearing man glanced at everyone as all eyes turned to him; sans Macaque who was still glaring at the ground so as to not glare directly at the lion demon. He fumbled for a bit and the suspicious and incredulous look the pig demon gave him did not do wonders for his confidence. Panicked he wondered if this would be the right moment to make his declaration but also he wasn’t sure he had the strength to.

“You’re leaving?” Pigsy gruffly asked glancing at Macaque but then looking back at the fidgeting human.

“Oh, ah!” Tang tangled his fingers together to try and relieve some of his nerves. “You see…” He started but then deflated. “I was-”

“We’re home! Oh!” Bai He let out a noise of surprise as she walked into and knocked over one of Macaque’s suitcases.

“Careful!” Wukong immediately caught the young girl and then boggled at all the people in the foyer. “Woah, what’s going on?” He asked slowly as he took in everyone.

Azure gave a small wave and shrug while Pigsy ignored him but kept looking at Tang. Tang meanwhile, seemed extremely nervous but was trying to maybe put on a brave face? More concerning, MK was glaring at Macaque who was glaring at the floor, but was now rubbing his face with both hands and seemed…tired? And as he watched Bai He pick up the knocked over suitcase Wukong felt a panic ignite inside him, a sense of deja-vu overwhelming him and sweeping him backwards in time. Those were Macaque’s suitcases.

Bai He noticed that as well, because after righting the fallen luggage she looked up at Macaque with dread.

“Baba? Are we leaving?”

Macaque’s claws dug into his flesh and he wished he could tear his skin off. He took a deep breath and then another and upon his second exhale he released his face and turned to his daughter.

“A-Actually, I thought...” He stuttered and swallowed to try and keep his voice steady “I thought it’d be-” He couldn't get the lie out so he changed his speech and squatted so he was at more of an even level with the young girl. “Tang and I are going back to New York. You’re going to stay here with Wukong and MK for awhile.”

“What?!” Everyone yelled, though Bai He’s was a gasp.

“Is-is this because of the shopping?!” Bai He panicked, as she latched onto Macaque’s arms. “I didn’t get seen! I swear! There were no photos!”

“It’s not about that.” Macaque tried to reassure her but she kept going, trying to make sense of what was happening.

“Was I comparing too much? I love our home! I do!” She said urgently, and Macaque combed his fingers through her hair as he kept trying to keep her from spiraling.

“Is that it?!” MK ran over and also yanked on Macaque’s arm. “I loved it there! It was great! And-and we can go to New York right?! Right?!” MK threw a questioning look at Wukong and Bai He joined him, throwing desperate looks at their Dad who seemed to jolt back to the present.

“Yeah! Yes, of course!” Wukong agreed, still sounding dazed as he came out of the memory of the last time he came home to find Macaque just gone. The slow panic that took over him back then as he first walked and then ran from room to room; looking for his husband so he could share Tripitaka’s plan.

“Is that what this is about?” Wukong asked and Macaque shook his head almost furiously.

“No, NO!” He barked, his frustration and emotions boiling over. He yanked his arms back towards himself and crossed them as he rocked back on his heels. “It’s not about that! It’s just-I just! I have to go back!”

“Why?!” MK questioned again

“Why can’t I come?” Bai He asked in a wobbly voice as tears gathered in her eyes. “I don’t want to stay here without you.”

“Oh Bai He.” Wukong cooed as he gently ran a hand through her hair in hopes of offering his daughter comfort. He then glared up at Macaque.

“Here’s a question!” Pigsy chimed in, anger clear on his face and in his tone. “If you two,” he pointed at Tang and Macaque. “Have to go so badly, why didn’t you tell anyone sooner?!”

“That’s what I asked!” MK added.

“What’s so damn urgent you're not even giving these kids time to adjust?! Not to mention we haven’t called schools or-”

“Schools! Yeah!” Wukong jumped in and took over as he gently pushed Bai He behind him so he could thrust a finger directly at Macaque. “Why didn’t you tell me this was even an idea in your head?! I haven’t enrolled her in school! And with my jobs lined up and the press!” Wukong jolted upright at that thought. “The press! What’s your master plan for me handling that Macaque?! For all your shit talk about it over the years and literally.” He got closer to the dark monkey and growled in his face “this morning you sure don’t seem to care now!”

“I do care!” Macaque fired back only to be thrown off whatever else he planned to say by Bai He crying out.

“I don’t want to be followed by cameras!” MK quickly pulled her into a hug and tried to sooth her as he glared at Macaque. “MK says it’s awful!” MK’s face immediately blanched at that bomb his sister just dropped. Wukong immediately rounded towards the teen, looking devastated.

“What?”

“Uhhh!” MK panicked but as his Dad’s face dropped more he quickly tried to clarify. “It’s not awful! I...it’s just…”

Wukong looked only more devastated so MK tried again. He gripped Bai He tighter and his sister reciprocated as she was now even more upset since she accidentally caused more drama.

“Dad, I really don’t mind it!”

Wukong took a steadying breath but didn’t look away from MK for a second. “But?” He pressured.

MK buttoned his lips shut, however after a few seconds acquiesced “But it’s a lot sometimes. But it’s not always!” He rushed to assure his Dad.

“Why haven’t you told me that before?” Wukong still looked stricken so Bai He chimed in.

“At least he’s not hidden away.” She threw a look at Macaque who appeared stunned at the jab.

“Hidden away? I’ve been protecting you!” Macaque defended. “I didn’t want all that attention on you.”

“Some attention would be nice. No one wants to live in the shadows forever.” She mumbled.

Macaque looked flummoxed and shared a look of comradery with Wukong who looked guilty and unsure what to even do at both kids' revelations.

“PIGSY I WOULD LIKE TO STAY AND GO OUT WITH YOU!” Tang literally screamed as he clutched his shirt. His declaration broke the tension with a sledgehammer; and once again all eyes turned to the glasses wearing human as a hush settled over everyone.

“Uhhh….what?” Azure asked, breaking the awkward silence that followed.

“At first,” Tang began looking only at the pig demon who looked befuddled at everything happening. “I just liked your cooking. It’s true culinary art!” his eyes sparkled as he listed out compliments on the various dishes Pigsy had cooked over the span of their time together. All eyes went from the ranting human to the now bright red pig demon. “And then, we got to know each other and had our talks.” Tang tapped his fingers together in a show of nerves. “Your gruff exterior hiding a kind heart just made me more attracted to you.”

Wukong let out a surprised breath at Tang’s bravery in just confessing all this, and in front of everyone. Glancing at Macaque, his ex-husband didn’t seem surprised so maybe Tang was just like this. However Bai He looked confused and almost as stunned as MK, though MK also looked excited at this change of events.

“So!” Tang puffed up his chest in hopes of showing some bravado. “I’d like to stay and go on dates! And…well I guess go from there!”

The madly blushing pig demon could only open and close his mouth, doing a great impression of a fish out of water.

“That’s Pigsy speak for absolutely! Right? Right?” MK jumped in with a smile looking between Pigsy and Tang.

Pigsy whipped his head towards the teenager and then back at the nervous human and gave a stiff, and still very embarrassed, nod of agreement.

“This is all a lot to take in.” Bai He admitted as she rubbed her forehead.

“Right!” Wukong stood upright and began to try and do damage control. “Tang you’re free to stay here. And of course so is Bai He!” He then pointed at the dark monkey. “But you have to give me some warning here Macaque! You can’t just spring this on us!”

Macaque ground his teeth together and it took everything in him not to glare death at the lion demon. He could feel the smug satisfaction radiating off the asshole.

“I know you don’t talk to me about anything! But I thought you’d at least talk to me about where I’d be living.” Bai He glared and that felt like a stab in the chest to the dark monkey.

“You're breaking your promise!” MK added in, his smile from the brief romance interlude gone and in its place was a glare of righteous anger. “What happened to ‘I’m here for you and Bai He’ and your-your whole ‘I’m not going anywhere’?! What happened to that?!”

“Look!” Macaque finally snapped, his emotions overtaking him as he proceeded to shout at the three glaring him down. “I don’t want to go, but I have to!” He then roughly grabbed the one suitcase closest to him and threw it over his shoulder. “And since I’m such a shit father and husband I’m sure you’ll all be happier without me!” MK and Bai He froze up with guilt at Macaque’s self degradation and Macaque used that to grab the other bag.

“Macaque maybe-” Tang tried but the dark monkey rounded on him with a fierce growl, making the human flinch back.

“NO!”

“Hey you can’t-” Pigsy defended, stepping up to be next to Tang.

“Tang!” Macaque barked at the fretting human who was now biting his lip while looking anxious. “Stay here, ok?!” The dark monkey then abruptly turned to his kids who jolted at the attention. Macaque’s face softened minutely, and a look of pure sadness seemed to overtake him before he quickly shoved it aside.

“I’m sorry! I really, truly am! I didn’t want to break our promise and I definitely-...I…” He yanked the kids into a tight embrace, both children easily gripping onto Macaque yet they were still stiff (no doubt still angry about everything). Macaque swallowed hard and with a last squeeze let the two go. “I love you both so so much, never doubt that!” Bai He began to cry as MK quickly gripped the dark monkey’s arm. “I’ll call when I land ok?”

He then rushed outside, slamming the door behind him so as to try and not hear Bai He and MK break down. However his hearing picked it up, and his knees buckled out from under him. His bags falling to the wayside, he heaved deep breaths.

He could feel a breakdown try and overwhelm him; he felt the fingers of it grasping at him from all around. He just had to make it to the car and then Tang or someone could grab the stupid vehicle from the airport later or whatever. He didn’t care, he just had to go! He couldn’t be near all this!

Blackmailed into leaving, getting proven right about the paparazzi, only to then be accused of hiding his daughter away. He was grateful to Tang for buying him a few seconds to cool down and regroup himself with his confession. But it wasn’t nearly long enough, and those last words from MK and Bai He were daggers lodged in his heart. Their tears that he could vaguely still hear were haunting him, and he knew he’d be hearing that the whole plane ride.

With the goal of not breaking down in front of the house. He wiped his face with his arm to get rid of the tears. And with a familiar mantra of ‘I’ll breakdown later. I’ll breakdown later.’ he was able to make it to the car. He had just loaded the bags in the back when he heard the front door hastily open and close.

And in those few seconds from when he heard the door close till he glanced back; Macaque let his imagination get the best of him. He pictured Wukong running after him, knowing instinctively that something was wrong. That Macaque wouldn't act that way without there being something else going on. So Wukong would swoop in, make all his problems disappear, and be there for him.

Then those seconds ended and the reality of what that delusional dream in the past had gotten him came to the forefront. Wukong never came for him back then, never even called, and now -Macaque turned from his place by the car and saw Wukong running towards him, expression furious - and now was no different.

“Hey! Hey! Get back here!” Wukong yelled as he ran after Macaque and literally jumped on the hood of the car to make sure the dark monkey couldn’t drive off. “Don’t you dare start putting the guilt on us! On them!” He slammed his fist into the hood of the car. “You’re the one rushing out the door! Again!”

“This is the part where you should be ecstatic Wukong! I’m out of your hair! You don’t have to deal with me!”

“When did I ever say anything like that?!” Wukong grabbed his hair and pulled on it as he quickly tried to think of whenever he said or implied something like that when it hit him what he was doing. “Wait!...No! NO!” He slammed his hands down, frustration at an all time high. “You’re not doing this to me again!” He screamed and it actually made Macaque flinch back.

“Doing what?!” Macaque fired back with some genuine confusion in his tone.

“Making me think this is all MY fault!” Wukong gestured to himself as he got off the car and walked to be in front of Macaque.

Macaque paled and reached out to Wukong. “I’m not try-”

However Wukong rebuffed him and took a step away from the dark monkey. “You’re the one wanting to leave Macaque!” Macaque opened his mouth to rebut but Wukong kept going. “You always get on me for running away but you're the one who’s left! Twice now!”

“I’m not-this isn’t!” Macaque let out a huge groan as he ran his hands through his hair. His emotions were all jumbled and he wanted to lash out, to hurt like he was hurting, to cry, to lay down, to just give up, to fight. It was all raging inside him and he couldn’t figure out what to say. He kept gripping his head, his eyes closed with the effort to think of words to say.

“What?!” Wukong asked cruelly. “Going to say you ‘have to go’ again?!”

“I do!” Macaque grounded out through gritted teeth as he looked up and stared the golden monkey down. Wukong scoffed and folded his arms while rolling his eyes. Macaque threw his arms out in anger.

“Wukong!” Macaque yelled, his voice breaking in the middle of said monkey's name. “Look at me!” Macaque gestured towards himself, but Wukong’s body language and glare remained. “Do I look like I want-?” The dark monkey cut himself off and threw a hand back towards the front door. “Do you think I wanted them-? I mean even with leaving Bai He-?! And-and the guilt tripping I didn’t- in the past yes but not now!” He wasn’t completing a sentence and he knew it, but he just had so much fighting to get out and no plan on how to say it properly without going into all the details. He forced himself to focus his vision, his mind’s eye and eyesight zeroing in on Wukong. And it hurt.

He didn’t know what he expected till he didn’t see it. Evidently Macaque was still a dreamer when it came to their relationship. Because what he didn’t see was understanding; was someone realizing something was wrong. No, all he saw was Wukong being angry that Macaque would abandon him and their children without so much as a day-no not even an hour's notice.

Macaque could try. He could ask Wukong for a second and think of a formulated response. He could also tell Wukong the truth. But those were thoughts that would happen in hindsight. What-ifs he’d toil and torture himself with while on the plane back to New York.

Presently, Macaque gave up. His son was furious and felt betrayed. His daughter felt he had hidden her away and now abandoned. And Wukong was just angry at him for it all. Shoulders slumping, he felt as if his whole body deflated in defeat. With a click of his tongue Macaque turned away from the golden monkey,

“You were right.” He sighed. “We are just not right for each other.” Unseen by the dark monkey, Wukong flinched at those words.

“Wha-?” Wukong’s face became washed in confusion but the six earned monkey only turned and got into the car.

“I’ll call when I land.”

And then he drove off leaving a stunned Wukong behind.

Chapter End Notes

So first and foremost I want to stress that Wukong, MK, and Bai He’s anger/hurt is completely justified from their POVs.

Secondly, I couldn't find a way to put this into the fic but the reason Wukong didn’t even try and help his recently ex-husband after Mac’s play failed was because that was during his Mac purge. He didn’t look up anything to do with Mac and didn’t read/look into anything to do with live performance cause it reminded him of Mac. He was grieving his own way, by being an ostrich and sticking his head in the ground.
