Peng had walked outside and noticed their hose was not in the same position it was previously. And Azure recalled the young girl buying a water bottle when they had stopped at a convenience store. He didn’t pay attention to her drinking it, but all that added up and Azure figured Bai He sabotaged his car.

He had planned on confronting the young girl but got derailed by Wukong and now. Now he didn’t know what to do! His talk with Wukong couldn’t have gone worse! He walked further into the house, looking for signs of anyone but, unfortunately, all he saw was Pigsy.

The pig demon gave him an unimpressed look and folded his arms. “I think I heard Wukong leave, so that’s your cue to go home.” Pigsy said gruffly and gestured back to where Azure came.

“I need to talk with Bai He.” His tone was harsher than he knew it ought to be, but he couldn’t help it.

“Well she ain't here.” Pigsy shot back, standing firm and continuing to stare the lion demon down.

“She’s not-” Then he remembered how Wukong first greeted him. He had said the kids were all at Mei -the green highlighted girl’s- home. “Oh. Right.” With that he turned and left.

He did not know where this Mei person lived but he knew one thing. He needed to get a grip on things again.

-

“OK gang!” MK smacked the corkboard behind him that had various papers and pictures pinned to it. “Let’s review everything we got! Mei go for it!” He pointed a pen at his friend.

The girl gave a cheeky salute then stood up from her place on the couch where Red Son, Bai He, and Tang sat. She went on the other side of the corkboard and pointed to a picture MK had drawn of Azure.

“We know Lion man here has a massive superiority complex.” She then indicated towards the pinned interviews with the two prior managers. “He blames everyone for everything and thinks he’s better than what he is. Dude is clearly desperate for the spotlight! Which your Dad basically owns!”

MK nodded in agreement, holding up a quick doodle he did of a chibi Wukong up on a stage and under a spotlight.

“So he’s just using Dad?” Bai He questioned, glancing to Red Son and Tang. Tang tried to think how to answer delicately but Red Son beat him to it.

“All evidence points to that. This pathetic demon would rather be handed glory than actually work for it.”

“Ahem!” Mei declared, getting everyone's attention back on her. “We also know, he’s an asshole!” She pointed towards copies of complaints and notes they had taken from various staff members.

“I mean you’re right.” Tang’s head wavered back and forth, “But there’s another way to word it.” Mei just shrugged unapologetically.

“And finally!” Mei and MK shared a look and MK quickly smacked a picture he drew of Azure, Peng, and Yellow Tusk as cartoon style mobsters. “He’s a mobster!”

Bei He leaned closer to get a better look at the art work while Red Son looked exhausted at his friends' antics. Tang only face palmed.

“He’s not in the mob.” The adult grumbled.

“You said you found out he was arrested for being a gang leader!” MK defended and Mei pointed to the notes on the board provided by Tang.

Tang stood up and pointed to the print out he had obtained. “Azure, Peng, and Yellow Tusk were convicted of racketeering.”

“Yeah and when we asked what that meant you said gang type stuff.” MK pouted.

“It is but that doesn’t automatically mean the mob.” Tang defended. “I don’t have transcripts from the cases yet, but they were all convicted of racketeering and had to forfeit some property they obtained illegally as well as spend some time in jail. Though Azure and Yellow Tusk appear to have gotten released early for good behavior.”

Bai He raised her hand, “I thought the mob was a gang?”

MK and Mei nodded in agreement and glared at Tang. Who put his hand to his face again, and this time Red Son did as well.

“The mob is a type of gang you imbeciles.”

“Correct.” Tang added.

“Doesn’t change the fact that your guy's Dad” Mei pointed at MK and Bai He “is dating a mob boss.”

“He’s not a mob boss!” Red Son and Tang yelled in exasperation.

“OK!” MK put his hands up in surrender. “So he’s not a mob boss.” Tang and Red Son nodded a yes. “But we are dealing with the mob.”

Tang’s whole face fell as Red Son threw his arms up in the air. “I give up!” The red head cried.

“No.” Tang closed his eyes then tried to think how to properly explain this. “They are not in the mob. They are their own gang. Or were at any rate.”

“You can’t just quit being in a gang!” Mei cut in looking at everyone. “I mean right? I feel like that’s not allowed.”

“So Dad is dating a gang boss?” Bai He asked.

“Leader.” Red Son corrected. “The term is gang leader. And yes.”

“Maybe!” Tang tried to soften the information. “We don’t know if Azure was the gang leader or if him and his brothers were just caught up in a gang.”

“I bet $20 that Azure’s the leader.” Mei whispered to MK who nodded in agreement.

“Yesterday I told Macaque about finding out Azure had a criminal history. He was probably also doing his own digging about it today.”

MK clapped his hands together “Well now all we need to do is update him and tell Dad about everything.”

“We should get security in place.” Red Son added. “We do not know how dangerous of a gang these three were in.”

“The mansion’s secure.” MK said but the red head scoffed.

“The mansion’s remote. We need safety on standby!”

“What about Sandy?” Mei offered. “It’s less suspicious having Sandy around more often then suddenly security guards showing up.”

“He is an intimidating figure.” Red Son mulled the thought over.

“Yeah!” MK agreed, “And he used to be a soldier! Pigsy said he used to be a crazy good fighter!” He mimed giving a few punches and Mei joined him as they acted out a pretend fight.

“I could see if my Father could come as well. It’s been awhile since we’ve had a visit so it would be plausible.”

“Oh! A Daddy DBK sighting.” Mei teased and the redheads face immediately boiled to match his hair.

“Do not call my Father that!” He raged.

“You don’t have to do that Red.” MK was touched at the offer but was hesitant to get anyone new involved in all the drama that was unfolding.

“Mei can we use your phone to call Baba?” Bei He asked Mei after gently pulling ont he girls clothes.

“Yeah!”

“I’ll call and explain everything to him.” Tang volunteered.

“Then bring the phone in here so we can talk with him.” Bai He urged to which the adult nodded.

After Tang explained all that they had found to Macaque, the father had predictably and understandably gone into a fit at the discovery that his children were with a gang leader. Tang had corrected that so far all they knew was the Brothers were convicted of racketeering and had to give up property on some Camel Ridge Road. That of course did not calm the father and he declared how he would be on the first flight back to California.

“That went well.” Mei said sarcastically into the silence left after Macaque’s phone call ended.

“Honestly? That went better than I thought it would.” Tang said with a shrug.

MK grabbed the phone and then called his house. “Pigsy? Is Azure there?”

“No, he left this morning. Are you all still at Mei’s?”

“Is Dad there?” Bai He asked.

“He left this morning as well. You all do know it’s almost dinner time right? Are you guys coming back?”

“Yeah we’ll be heading over.” MK waved a hand in the air. “Can you call Sandy and have him come over?”

-

Azure pulled back into the mansion with a battle plan ready. His regrouping with his brothers was helpful in not only planning but in calming him. Sometimes he could get so zero focused that he ignored the larger picture. But he would keep his head. He would ignore what happened to his car (even though he knows Bai He did it) and instead go back into staying in Wukong’s good graces. He’ll ask Wukong for that date and keep the romance alive. He made a misstep this morning with the monkey but he could fix it.

When he parked he saw an array of cars and a motorcycle. He tried to go into the front door but found it locked. Perturbed, the lion demon went around to the pool area and found the door down there unlocked. He didn’t announce his presence, something in him said to keep quiet. Listening to his instincts he slowly made his way upstairs towards the main floor. He could hear people in the distance, they appeared to be in the main sitting room. The one with a large fireplace and various chairs and couches in it. He approached slowly and quietly, his ears tilting forward as he tried to parse out what was being said.

“Enough with the mobster impressions!” He heard the pig demon chef yell, hushing all the other voices.

“Again, them being in a gang does not mean they are in the mob.” The awkward glasses wearing butler, Tang said.

Azure froze in the hallway and pressed against the wall so as to ensure he remained in the dark as he listened further. Some grumbling and bickering ensued before the chef spoke up again.

“So we don’t know how violent Azure and his brothers were in the past, right? All you got is they were convicted of gang-like stuff?”

The lion demon’s eyes narrowed and his lips curled into a snarl. ‘How did they find out about that?!’ He thought.

“I’m still waiting on the court transcripts. Typically those can take a week or so to be faxed over.”

“But if they served their sentence,” Now that voice was one Azure couldn’t place immediately, but then remembered Sandy. “Maybe they are reformed and are trying to live a better life.”

“Maybe they aren’t gangsters anymore Sandy.” Azure’s menacing look was replaced with one of horror. That was MK that had said that. They had found out about his past and had told Wukong’s son?! “But what about the other stuff we found?”

“Yeah!” Azure’s stomach dropped further as he recognized Bai He now speaking. “Like how he’s only dating Dad to get attention!”

There was more talking but it faded to the background as Azure became lost in his thoughts. They knew! How did they know?! How did they find out about their past?! That damn Tang mentioned ordering the court transcripts, how dare he! He had worked so hard! He had been so close! And it was all for nothing now?!

His spiraling thoughts were abruptly cut off when he heard MK announce “We just have to tell Dad all this.”

Wukong didn’t know!

And he wouldn’t know! Azure’s eyes narrowed as he pushed away from the wall.

He could pinpoint the moment he was first noticed. The big blue water demon had stood straighter and moved quickly to put himself between Azure and all the people gathered. Everyone turned to see what Sandy had noticed and everyone tensed upon seeing him.

“Am I interrupting?” He greeted with a sharp grin.

MK ushered Bai He behind him while Red Son and Mei flanked MK’s sides. Pigsy went to stand next to Sandy while Tang tensed in place behind Sandy but before the kids.

“I don’t know why you all decided to snoop into my life or what you think you found but-”

“Because you lied and you blackmailed Baba!” Bai He interrupted with a glare and the three friends quickly gave the young girl a look. MK kept a firm hand on Bai He’s arm and once again pulled her so he was in front of her.

“What?” Azure was genuinely floored by the knowledge that Macaque had told them he was blackmailed. He had thought the man to be more secretive than that.

“Azure,” Sandy said and held up an arm. “I think it’s time you leave. You are-”

“I think it’s time for honesty.” The lion demon stepped to the side to get a clear view of everyone behind the blue demon’s bulky form. “That’s what you are looking for right?” He didn’t wait for confirmation before slowly taking steps forward. Everyone pivoted, as Azure walked they moved. “You call me a liar, but that’s being hypocritical of everyone else here.”

Azure stopped and leaned an arm on the fireplace’s mantle. He now had a clear view of everyone. Sandy and Pigsy flanked either side of him, only a couple footsteps away. Tang was directly in front of him, with a coffee table between them. The man had his arms spread in a pitiful attempt to shield the youngsters behind himself; who were backed directly up to the couch, if not on top of it. He could see Mei to MK’s left, Red Son to MK’s right, and Bai He kneeling or maybe even standing on the couch and peeking out from behind her brother.

“Or should we go into all the stories the Long family has bought or blocked in order to keep the lie about their prestigious reputation?”

Mei growled and moved to step forward but was held back by MK. “Don’t you talk about my family!”

“My dear there could be novels filled with all the offenses your family commits.” Azure tutted. “Yet they seem to get off on charges due to all the ties your family has. One reporter even dared to try and expose that. But of course your family silenced them.”

“You’re lying!” Mei hissed, fire dancing in her eyes.

“Oh? Then why hasn’t the news or tabloids been going nuts about your cousin Ao Bing eloping with that man?” Azure leaned forward but when Sandy raised a hand he leaned back.

“Because the affairs of her family are not news worthy!” Red Son defended, though Mei threw her friend an affronted look.

“Perhaps.” Azure shrugged. “But the affairs of yours certainly have been.” Red Son glared at the lion demon.

“Your Mother has quite the reputation.” Red Son launched himself forward and was only held back thanks to Bai He, MK, and Mei leaping on top of the red head. Red Son was screeching threats and obscenities at Azure, and Tang turned around in order to join the friends in talking Red Son down.

Azure only chuckled at the scene. That ticked Pigsy off who growled and stepped closer to the lion demon. “You had your laugh now, get the hell out!”

“I think not. You see we have yet to talk about what you all think you know about me.”

“That you’re an asshole!” Mei jeered.

Azure smirked and went to lord more information he and his brothers had found about the Long family over Mei, but MK cut him off with a firm “Stop!”

“Just stop!” MK commanded, slicing a hand through the air. “We know all about your criminal past, and worse! That you don’t even care about Dad! You’re just using him!” MK’s stern face crumbled at the thought of his father just being seen as a pawn to someone. “You don’t care about him or us!” Azure had tried to get a word in but MK’s stern look and firm tone wouldn’t allow it. “You got rid of Baba and you keep pushing this announcement of your relationship!” MK then pushed past Tang and held up the papers with their notes on it. “You just want to use Dad to get ahead. You don’t care about who you hurt in the process.”

After a few seconds of silence Azure blinked hard, “No. I don’t.” He then took a few steps closer to MK. Everyone tensed and Sandy moved closer, ready to jump in if needed.

“My brothers and I built a paradise back in Oklahoma. Only for it to go up in smoke. You think I cared about everyone who got in my way there?” He glared as he scanned the room. “You think you have the upper hand here? You don’t.”

He looked over at Sandy who had taken another step towards him. “The ex-soldier who enjoyed violence so much he did underground cage fighting afterwards.” Sandy paled and fell back. “Shame what you did down there.” Sandy gulped audibly, and the mood of the room leaned towards terror as they saw Sandy being cowed. “And the pigs bismol attempts to elope with the cooking star Chang'e.” Azure thumbed Pigsy who’s glare immediately dropped to a look of dread.

“I won’t reiterate what I have on the Long family.” Mei glared hotly, “Like I said, it could fill novels little speed racer.” His gaze then swept over to Red Son, “And as tawdry as your family is. I know they’ve been enjoying their peace now. Would be a shame for your Fathers infidelity to come to light.”

Red Son was all fired up to receive another insult about his mother, but like a firework doused in water he sputtered. “Wh-what? He would never… he…”

MK, Mei and Bai He gave Red Son a concerned look as the fiery red head looked as if his world was turned upside down. Bai He recovered first glaring hatefully at Azure. “You’re a jerk!”

“Only when pushed.” Azure fired back.

“So what?! You want to blackmail all of us too?” Pigsy snapped. “You think that will keep us from going to Wukong?!”

“If not then MK’s secret will.”

Everyone gasped, and MK turned white. Mentally the teenager told himself there was no way he knew. That whatever Azure found was some random secret. Like the fact that he forged his Dad’s signature on a math test once or twice. Or that he broke Mei’s dad’s prized pinball machine. Or that he had a crush on a red head.

“A certain camp counselor was quite helpful.” Azure taunted as he moved forward till he was hitting the coffee table, directly across from MK, who fell back against his sister.

“Y…Y-you wouldn’t…”

“A secret daughter living in New York is quite the headline but an even better one, is Wukong having a transgender kid!” Bai He blanched and MK just heard white noise as Azure continued to taunt them. “Maybe there will be guesses between you and Bai He on which it is.”

Azure’s maniacal chuckles were cut off abruptly by Tang punching the lion demon in the face.

Azure’s face jerked to the right from Tang’s blow. The wide eyes of everyone in the room turned to the human. Tang was glaring and breathing harshly with anger. He had one foot planted on the coffee table in order to reach the lion demon, he still had his arm up from the blow he delivered.

“You!” Azure growled as he slowly righted himself to glare at the pissed off butler.

“Yes, me!” Tang retorted. “I have no life or great accomplishments! I know you have nothing on me, but we have plenty on you!” Tang stepped back and angrily pointed down at the papers, leading Azure to look down as well. “And I’m not the ex-gang member who broke into someone's home to threaten minors!”

Pigsy huffed an impressed breath as the terror from seconds ago was lifted from seemingly the whole room thanks to Tang. The pig demon looked over towards the kids and MK’s color was coming back, as was Bai He’s and Red Son’s.

However, everyone jolted as Azure dove at the various papers. Grabbing fistfuls of the sheets, crumbling them, Azure tried to grab as much as he could before anyone else could spur into action. He then turned and chucked all the papers into the fireplace, where the fire quickly ate up everything, turning them to ash.

“No!” MK and Bai He cried, but Azure only spun back around with a full glare on his face. His expression a storm of fury.

“This is how things are going to play out!” He growled. “You all will keep your mouth shut and I will as well.” He glared right at MK and Bai He. “This isn’t forever, just until I’ve reached my goal. Once I’m on top then we can be rid of each other.” At the defiant look into the two’s eyes he continued. “But if you try to sabotage me, then I will make your lives hell and don’t think I don’t know how.” He looked directly at Bai He. “I could have you arrested for what you did to my car.” Bai He gasped and ducked into MK’s shoulder. “And you know what I could do to you!” He glared out at everyone “To all of you!” He looked at Tang, who’s bravery seemed to have abandoned him. “And I may not have anything on you, but there are other ways of keeping people quiet.” He threatened.

Pigsy moved closer to Tang on instinct but Azure only smiled. His smile was drenched in insincerity and false cheer as he stepped back to lean against the fireplace mantle once more.

“But fear not. Like I said, this isn’t forever.” He tilted his head as if a king giving his approval. “We just have to play the happy family till I’ve reached my goal. Then I’ll leave all of you alone and never tell a soul about all that I know.” His body relaxed as he explained this and his smile became more welcoming and sincere. “I had wanted us to go through this together on good terms, but you all burned that bridge.” He chided and even wagged a finger as everyone glared at him in rage and resignation.

Chapter End Notes

Let’s not forget Tang grabbing the fake-Mayor in canon. I love this man and I’m sure it shows lol Also Tang with the reverse humble brag, the self-loathing brag XD He’s got nothing and he’s proud!

I so wanted the chapter cut off to be right after Tang decked Azure. A sort of “we’ll be right back” meme cut, but that wouldn’t make the next chapter flow properly so I had to scrap that idea :( I’m still sad about it.

Chapter 18: Cleaning Up

Chapter Notes

Day 4

See the end of the chapter for more notes

“Everything must go. You either throw it in this section to be discarded or you throw it in this section to be mailed to Macaque.” Tripitaka gestured with his arm to the two sections he had marked: the discard area had green tape on the floor indicating its borders, while the mail section had silver tape.

“I can’t believe you’re making me do this.” Wukong groaned in irritation. “Is this even a priority?!”

The manager only stared Wukong down before the monkey gave in; yet he grumbled and grunted the whole way to the doors of the storage lockers. Upon Wukong’s request during the separation and divorce to ‘please just get rid of it all. I can’t…’ Tripitaka had rented these two large locker storage units and had all Macaque’s stuff put in them. Each was the shape and size of a single car garage.

Wukong secured the doors then let out a louder groan of agony as he realized each were filled to the brim. He glanced over to Tripitaka only to boggle and look aghast. The bald man was now sitting in a folding chair between the two marked sections.

“Are you going to just sit there and watch me?!” Wukong screeched as he waved his arms animatedly.

“No.” Tripitaka pulled out a large book. “I’m going to read.”

“YOU-!” Wukong’s face boiled red. He let out a growl then turned on his heel and went towards the first storage unit and began taking stuff out.

The first thing he pulled out was a broom with a wooden handle, the head of the broom looked frayed, as if it was well used. He tilted his head at it and wondered why it was cast away. Just as he was about to put it in the disposal section he spotted it. On the wooden handle at the top was a crudely carved heart. The memory came back and he immediately brought up the broom and looked further down the handle, and there near the broom head was the letter “M”. He then flipped the broom upside down so the broom head was up and the M turned into a “W”.

“A broom?” Tripitaka asked, startling Wukong.

“Yeah, see?” The monkey walked closer to the human. “Macaque carved a “W” here to mark it as my broom. But if you flip it like you’d use it.” He did so and then pointed to the letter. “It looks like an M.” Wukong smiled. “I used to joke that meant he would do the cleaning and I’d put it away.” He chuckled “He got mad at me for that, so I added a heart.” He pointed to the top of the broomstick. He smiled then placed the broom on the floor next to Tripitaka.

“I’m going to keep that one.”

“Why?” Tripitaka asked, immediately. It was such a quick question that Wukong was visibly caught off guard by it.

“Well…Mac probably already has a broom or a cleaning service. I mean this was from way back. So, he won’t need it.”

“Then throw it away.” The human again responded immediately.

“No!” Wukong shot back just as quick.

“Why?”

“Because! Anyway I’ve got more to get through.” Wukong grumbled then fast walked back to the storage unit he grabbed the broom out of.

Looking around Wukong saw a lamp, one he and Macaque had first bought for their apartment. It was their first “indulgent” purchase. It was an ugly, ugly lamp but they decided to blow their money on it because it made them laugh. He grabbed that and took it to the disposal section, lingering only for a moment. Next he pulled out a box that was on top of a worn looking chair. The chair was peach and had a high back and comfy, cushion arms.

“I wondered about the chair.” Tripitaka commented lightly.

“It was our…um,” He blushed and cleared his throat before scratching the back of his head. “Our b-bedroom chair.” His tail twitched back and forth in embarrassment as he quickly hauled it to the disposal section.

“Bedroom chair?” the human tilted his head but Wukong refused to even look in his direction. However he did stop and stare at the lamp for a moment longer before picking it up.

“Actually I’m going to keep this too.”

“Wukong.”

“The kids will love it!” The monkey defended as he brought the ugly lamp to lay next to the broom.

The box that had been on the chair was filled with clothes so Wukong put that in the to be delivered section. Then came various art pieces and decor that Macaque or Macaque and himself had bought over the years. A lot of nature prints and paintings. Wukong was reluctant to part with all of them but put a majority in the deliverables section. The remainder went by Tripitaka in the, unsanctioned, taking home pile.

Hours passed as Wukong went through everything. Wiping his brow from the sweat, Wukong dived back to the first storage unit and grabbed a worn looking duffle bag. Inside was camping equipment and that discovery startled a laugh out of him. “Oh wow!” He said in awe as he dug into the bag some more. “This is…this is so old!”

“What is it?”

“Our camping equipment!” Wukong hauled the duffle up and brought it over to Tripitaka. “This is from way back! Before I even met you! Oh man.” He smiled as he pulled out some of the tent poles. “Macaque and I used to love camping!” He smiled wistfully at the distant memories.

“I don’t believe I’ve ever even heard of you camping besides going to resorts or lodges.” Tripitaka said plainly as he once again put his book in his lap.

“I haven’t in ages!” He whined now longing to go find a desolate spot out in nature and set up a home away from home. “I’m keeping this too!” He declared and his friend boggled at him.

“Wukong, when are you ever going to use it?” Tripitaka then inspected the duffle and noted the holes and various stains on it. “Is it even usable?”

“Probably.” Wukong shrugged. At his friend’s glare he defended. “Macaque lives in the city he won’t want it!”

“Then don’t you think it should be disposed of?”

“No, I want it!” Wukong pouted. “It has memories.”

Strangely Tripitaka dropped the fight after that and let the monkey go back into the storage unit. Next was a wide array of tail rings. He was ready to pick them all up and haul them to the deliverable section when his eye caught on one cuff in particular. It was one that had a sun and moon engraved on it.

“He left it.” Wukong breathed as he hesitantly retrieved the decorated tail cuff. However as he held it and looked it over, anger began to grow in him. He gripped the cuff tightly and was almost ready to chuck it as hard as he could into the wall. He only hesitated as hurt overcame his anger. His grip relaxed and instead he slowly traced the engravings. It had been Wukong’s gift to Macaque for their first wedding anniversary. Macaque had gotten him…wait.

Wukong looked around the almost empty storage unit before standing and going over towards the untouched unit. He scanned it as he held onto the tail cuff. Trying to see if he could spot the gift. Suddenly the hurt was replaced by sadness. Macaque had left his gift and Wukong had hidden his away.

“Is everything alright?” Tripitaka asked as he craned his head to try and see what his friend was holding.

After a short pause Wukong gave Tripitaka a fake-it-till-you-make-it smile over his shoulder. “Yeah, I just have to bring over these tail rings.” Wukong huffed a laugh as he made his way back to the tail rings container. “That prima-donna! He has so many of these! He used to take forever picking out the perfect one to go with his outfit.” He groaned as he put the boxes down. “He would also want us to match and would make sure I wore one if the occasion called for it.” He shook his head as his manager nodded.

“I recall. He did a good job making sure yours matched or were compatible. It was good for photos.” He then pointed to the cuff still in Wukong’s hand. “Is that one yours?”

Wukong looked sadly down at the cuff and twisted it in his hand. “No, it’s Macaque’s but I can’t just…” He bit his lip. “I’ll give this to him in person.”

“We don’t know when that will be.”

“I have to give it in person!” Wukong barked, his tone reeking of his stubbornness so Tripitaka said nothing more. With a huff, Wukong clasped the cuff to his own tail and then went back to work. The other items were various nicknacks, decorations, and a box of coffee mugs. Wukong had moved a majority of the items to the deliverables. And some went to the take home pile, which made Tripitaka shake his head but Wukong ignored it.

Diving into the next storage unit, Wukong had one key mission. He wanted to find his gift that Macaque had gotten for him. After going through some fitness equipment, gardening tools, and even towels and some other linens; Wukong finally spotted his gift.

“Found it!” He cheered to a baffled Tripitaka.

“Found what?”

Wukong grinned and made a dramatic reveal of the painting he had in his arms. It was a standard canvas, it wasn’t even properly framed. The painting itself was unique and clearly self made.

“Well your art style has certainly come a long way.” Tripitaka commented politely.

“What?” Wukong looked at the painting then at his friend and shook his head in the negative. “No! I didn’t make this! Macaque did!” He beamed, his smile filled with pride as he turned the painting around to stare at it again. “It was our first anniversary and we didn’t have a lot of money. I wanted to go big so…” He trailed off but flicked his tail to draw attention to the cuff there. “Macaque’s face when he saw it.” He smiled softly as he was transported back to that time. “He wanted to be mad but couldn’t because he loved it so much.” He chuckled at the memory. “He didn’t even want to give me this afterwards. I literally had to pry it away from him!” He looked back at the painting. The art style was distinctly Macaque’s and it was a painting of Macaque and himself surrounded by trees with a flowery mountain in the background. He gently put the painting down by Tripitaka before diving into all the items he had quickly by-passed.

Finally, well into the evening and at the end of it all, the only item in the disposal section was the chair. The deliverables and take home piles were both huge, rivaling each other in item quantity. Wukong wiped his face with a rag as he congratulated himself on getting through both units in one day. He then felt a tap on his shoulder. Turning he saw Tripitaka behind him, the human had his folding chair in his hands.

“I need you to sit here.” The human gave the folding chair to Wukong and pointed to the space in between the two storage units.

“Uhhh, ok?” Wukong set up the chair and took a seat. His confusion only grew when he noticed Tripitaka had his Manager face on. “Tripitaka?”

“No matter the impulse, do not get up from that seat.” His Manager then turned and grabbed the broom he had first used to create the take home pile. Tripitaka showed no emotion as he held it forward. “You have a maid service. Macaque has a maid service.” Wukong went to say something but the human only narrowed his eyes and Wukong held his tongue. “I know you do have cleaning supplies, yet I also know Pigsy is the only one who uses them.” He tilted the broom so it pointed towards the disposal section. “There’s no logical reason you should have it, this belongs in disposal.”

“I don’t want to throw it away!” Wukong said through clenched teeth.

Tripitaka then used the brom to point towards the camping equipment duffle bag. “If those camping essentials are from before you met me then those are very old and outdated.”

“Yeah well-”

The human glared at Wukong and on instinct the golden monkey backed down again. “I was not done talking.” He said then lifted his glare and put on a more friendly expression. “You had said you want to keep it because it has memories.” Wukong held Tripitaka’s stare and gave a small nod. “It is the same for this broom.” He then waved an arm at all the items in the take home pile. “It’s the same for all these items! The lamp, the towels, the monkey decorations, the art, the mugs and cups, all of it! You don’t want the items, you want the memories attached to them!”

Wukong’s leg bounced as he looked away from Tripitaka’s heavy gaze. The bald man clasped the broom in both hands and drummed his fingers on it.

“I…am not good at social aspects.” Wukong snorted as if to say that was an understatement and the human took the jab with a smile. “But even I can see what you are still denying.”

Wukong barked a laugh. “What?! I’m not denying anything!” He threw his hands up and almost pushed himself up and out of the chair but stern and reminding look from Tripitaka kept his ass planted. “Ok, fine! Yes I want to keep these things for the memories. So what?!”

“You never properly dealt with your divorce.” Tripitaka stated as if it was a fact, and that cowed Wukong rather quickly. “I do have fault in that as well. I knew you were not processing it. You buried yourself in MK and work and I helped you with that. I thought that was for the best and even encouraged it….I was wrong.”

Wukong shook his head as his leg began to bounce again. He bit his lip and huffed out a frustrated breath. “I dealt with the divorce!”

“No you did not.” Tripitaka countered, his tone emotionless. Wukong glared at him anyway as if the man had spat the sentence at him. “You hid from it.”

“Why are you on about this?!” Wukong threw his hands up. “With all this! The storage unit, me wanting to keep some shit! Why are you so focused on this?!”

“Why did it hurt you so much that Macaque said “are” instead of “were”?”

Wukong froze, his anger dropping to his stomach like a stone. Finally he managed to make himself say “Because I didn’t say are.” in a voice that sounded feeble even to his own ears.

However Tripitaka only looked at him, his gaze unwavering. Finally after a full minute of silence Tripitaka pointed at all the items in the take home pile.

“Why can’t you part with these things Wukong?”

“Because I miss him!” Wukong finally admitted and suddenly a wall Wukong was not aware he had up crumbled, and like a broken dam everything rushed out. “...I missed him!” He yelled and grabbed his face, yanking on his hair. “I didn’t want to miss him! Everything was such a mess and he left! And I didn’t want to miss him! I didn’t want to think about him! It hurt! He was gone! But then he came back! And it was- we were! And now he’s gone again! And if these are all I have of him…If I’ll only have memories then, I need them! ……I miss him!” His tone became watery so he quickly tried to reign himself in.

“I think he missed you too.” Tripitaka said as he allowed Wukong a moment to try and calm himself. “I did wonder if I’d get a call about the items he left behind. Yet he never reached out to me, the lawyers, or Pigsy about them.” Tripitaka waited for Wukong to look at him. “Looking at all this I wonder if he was planning on coming back.”

And suddenly it clicked in Wukong’s mind causing him to freeze in place as the memory came back to him.

“So I left, and you never came for me. You never even called.”

Almost horrified, Wukong looked at the piles of items. His eyes then scanned the art, the jewelry, the clothes. The clothes! Macaque had left clothes behind! Slowly, almost hesitantly, his eyes danced over the glimpses of what he could see in each pile and a pit in his stomach opened up and grew larger the more he took it all in.

Macaque had left so much. He had left so much! And it was because he had thought he’d be coming back. He had been waiting for Wukong.

Wukong swallowed hard at the realization.

Sensing Wukong’s change in demeanor Tripitaka quietly put the broom down and quickly went to crouch near his friend.

“Wukong,” He gently grabbed his friend's hand. “Why is it so hurtful that Macaque said “are” instead of “were”?” He asked again.

Wukong’s hand immediately tightened around Tripitaka as his traitorous eyes tried to fill with water. “...we can’t be over…Trip I need him! I’ve missed him this whole time, I just didn’t…!”

“Let yourself.” Tripitaka finished as he patted the monkey's hand. He then made a sound of shock as Wukong leapt at the man and cuddled into him for comfort. Tripitaka balked for only a moment before wrapping his arms around his friend. He hummed a soothing mantra as he waited for Wukong to calm down.

Eventually Wukong let the man go and gave Tripitaka some time as he knew the man was not overly fond of physical affection. After Tripitaka paced for a bit and did some exercises with his hands the human came back. Standing Wukong put his hands on his hips and let out a long sigh.

“I need to talk with Azure. This isn’t fair to him.”

“I often hear the phrase, you can’t control your heart.”

“Yeah.” Wukong sighed, “I still feel like a dick. I mean I do like him, he’s nice but…” The golden monkey looked over all the stuff before him. “But I need to clear things up with Macaque. I don’t want us to be over.”

Tripitaka looked from Wukong to all the items and bit his lip as he saw how his friend's face was falling into distress again. Slowly the human said. “That may be difficult with the divorce, b-”

He was cut off by Wukong bursting into laughter. The golden monkey wrapped an arm around his stomach as he laughed and ignored his friend fretting around him.

Tripitaka tried to quickly finish his sentence. “B-but that isn’t to say-!”

“No, no Trip!” Wukong assured, as he held up a hand, giggles still escaping his mouth. “You made a joke without meaning to, let’s leave it at that.”

At Wukong’s genuine smile Tripitaka nodded. He then told Wukong he would help the monkey load all the items back into the two storage units. One would be filled with the to be delivered items and the other would house all the items he wished to take home eventually. They packed everything back in, with the exception of the cuff still on Wukong’s tail and the painting Macaque had created.

By the time they finished Wukong had begun to whine about how he was starving. It was dinner time and Wukong had worked through lunch; however that wasn’t an uncommon occurrence. Triptitaka and Wukong would have to skip meals sometimes, depending on the work day and job they were doing. However, Wukong asked Tripitaka to take him through a drive thru before taking him home.

“Emotional breakdowns really take a lot out of a man.” The monkey demon complained.

Arriving home, however, Wukong grumbled and complained around a mouthful of french fries. “Aw man, everyones over! I bet Pigsy cooked up a feast and here I’m wasting my stomach space on a dingy burger!” Wukong glared at the now empty fast food bag.

“I’m sure you will find room.” The car phone Tripitaka had began to ring so he waved Wukong to go on without him saying he would join in once he was done.

Wukong carried in the painting and went to open the door only to jerk as he found the door locked. “What the?!” Wukong tried to open the door again, but it still refused to budge. “Who locked the door?” He raised his free hand to bang on the door but stopped himself. Azure’s car was here and he didn’t want the man to find him with a painting of himself and Macaque in his arms.

He walked around to the side of the main entrance to climb and enter through the patio connected to the kitchen. He was taken aback at seeing the kitchen through the sliding glass doors and windows to be empty and with minimal signs of Pigsy cooking. Upon entering the house he heard Azure in the main sitting room so with a sigh he gently put the painting down on the kitchen counter and made his way over.

He rehearsed one last time how he would break up with Azure as he walked. He would mention how they had fun and how he did like Azure but he couldn’t control his heart; and his heart belongs to Macaque. It always has, even when Wukong didn’t want it to. Which is probably why he was never able to fully go for the idea of having another partner. He had tried with Azure, and he was grateful for their time but having Macaque around again, as infuriating as the monkey is, Wukong didn’t want a life devoid of him.

‘Well I can’t tell him all that!’ Wukong thought as he grimaced. ‘I’ll just keep it short. That our time together was great but I can’t do it anymore.’

Lost in his thoughts and planning, the golden monkey had tuned out the rest of the world. So he didn't hear what Azure had been saying, nor was he really paying attention to what was in front of him. It was only once he finished his internal monologue did he blink back into the present.

“Woah!” Wukong jumped as he took in everyone, and he meant everyone, staring wide eyed at him.

Glancing about, the golden monkey realized he had walked fully into the main sitting room and was standing next to the fireplace that Azure was currently leaning against. However, Azure, Pigsy, Sandy, everyone actually were all just looking at him. Their eyes wide and body’s stiff.

“Uh hey guys…?” Wukong waved awkwardly. “Sorry, I was in my own head, did I miss something?”

Sandy and Pigsy shared a glance before looking to MK and Bai He. Red Son and Mei glared at Azure for some reason, while Tang also looked back towards his kids. Glancing at Azure he saw Al looking directly at MK and Bai He with a look that Wukong did not like. However then Azure blinked and it was replaced with a smile as he turned to Wukong.

“Nothing important. We were just having a debate.” He moved to Wukong and leaned close to the golden monkey, making a move to give him a kiss. To stop that Wukong held up a hand and then turned back towards his kids who looked frustrated.

“Is everything ok?” He questioned and from the way both kids eyebrows drew together he knew something was bothering them. “What were you guys debating?” He looked back to Azure.

“Oh nothing too serious,” The lion demon waved a hand. “Just which of your video games is better.” Azure explained in a blasé tone.

Wukong squinted his eyes in suspicion at that. Sure Mei and MK had some video game debates and arguments with each other, but Bai He didn’t seem to even play video games. But without a glance at his kids he let it go with a shake of his head, he had a more important conversation to have.

“OK.” He shrugged. “Listen, Al can we talk?”

Azure tilted his head to the side and his smile became stiff. “Talk about what?”

“Oh, just…” Wukong awkwardly rolled his hands, “...can we talk?” He finally asked again in a huff.

“Of course.” Azure agreed but didn’t make a move from his spot.

“In private?” Wukong thumbed behind him.

Azure’s stare was becoming icy and Wukong wasn’t sure why. Though maybe it’s because he could sense what was about to happen. From Wukong’s glance at the others he could see Mei seemed to be picking up on what was happening; so perhaps Azure was figuring it out too and wanted to delay the inevitable.

“Why in pri-”

“Oh my God! Are you going to dump him?!” Mei burst out with the biggest grin on her face.

Wukong really should have expected that. With a heavy sigh he let his head fall back and his shoulders fell. He knew Mei long enough, of course she’d just call this out. Righting himself he tried to give Al a sympathetic and apologetic look. Meanwhile Azure had froze and his face fell into a look of utter disbelief.

“Are ya?” Pigsy asked, looking for the world like he had just been through the ringer.

Wukong waved his hands around and tried to speak while saying nothing really as he looked everyone over. When he looked back at Azure, he finally dropped his arms and his tail wrapped around his leg in a show of shame. He didn’t want to do this so publicly, it felt almost cruel. Plus Bai He and Azure had gotten so close, he didn’t want her to witness this.

“Can we talk over here?” He stepped back, hoping to coax the lion demon to follow him.

“Are you breaking up with me?” Azure questioned, his tone not one of hurt or confusion but anger.

“I..” Wukong glanced over towards his kids and both looked oddly excited about this. Wukong raised an eyebrow at that.

“Wukong!” Azure barked as he moved into the golden monkeys' space. “Are you trying to break up with me?!”

With a sigh Wukong gave up on the idea of doing this in private. “Yes, I’m sorry!”

Azure snarled and the rest of what Wukong was going to say was drowned out by MK jumping up with Bai He in his arms. Both kids yelled out a “yes!” as if in victory. And Wukong boggled at them. Just the other day Bai He ditched him to hang out with Azure and now she was cheering for them breaking up?! Plus MK and Azure always seemed to get along, yet MK was smiling as if he had just beat Mei in Monkey Fighter. In fact everyone seemed to be beaming as if in some sort of victory.

“What is going on?” Wukong asked aloud as he took in Sandy’s relieved face and Tang clutching his chest as if a huge weight was lifted. Pigsy had even gone over to Tang and wrapped an arm around the man’s shoulders as if congratulating a team member on a job well done.

“We’re not breaking up!” Azure growled, loud enough to bring everyone's attention back to the pissed off lion. “I have worked too hard for it to all fall apart here!”

“Azure-” Wukong tried to start up his break up speech but was silenced by Azure slicing a paw through the air.

“No! It’s not ending here!” The lion demon huffed. He then glanced at everyone and their smug faces only triggered him more. With a growl he turned back to Wukong and glared. “We’re not over till I get my due!”

“Your due?!” Wukong scoffed.

“I’ve put up with all your bullshit and issues, I was the perfect boyfriend!”

“And I’m grateful for that!” Wukong interjected. “But I don’t love you! I can’t!” The monkey's face fell as he tried to comfort Al but was rebuffed.

“I don’t care about that!” Azure spat. “I have put so much work into this, I’m not walking away with nothing!”

“It’s over Azure.” MK spoke up, “You’ve lost.”

“Lost?” Wukong whispered in confusion as he looked between Azure and his son.

“Oh you think so?!” Azure spun towards MK and practically screamed “Have you forgotten all I have on you?! What I could do to you all?!”

MK flinched back and Wukong saw red at the veiled threat towards his family.

“What did you just say.” Wukong growled with such anger and hidden fury that it hushed the whole house.

However Azure recovered and turned to the angry father with a smug smile. “Only reminding your son” He stressed the word “that if we don’t get this back to the way it should be, you’re going to find it impossible to hide his, yours, Macaque’s and all your friends here dirty little secrets from the media.”

“What are you talking about?” Wukong squinted in anger as he let Azure’s words fully sink in. “Are you…blackmailing me?” Azure inclined his head, his smug grin never leaving; even as Wukong scoffed in disbelief. “Azure we have a non-disclosure agreement you really think-”

“As if that would do anything against me!” The lion demon boasted and flapped a hand at Wukong as if waving off a fly. “I could always have my brothers go for me.”

“That would still constitute a violation of your NDA.” A new voice declared. Everyone turned to the newcomer and saw Tripitaka walk fully into the room. The bald man had an unimpressed expression on his face as he continued. “Seeing as the only way Yellow Tusk and Peng could have gotten any of the information is through your relationship to Wukong. So them reaching out would constitute acting as your agents or acting on your behalf.” Azure went to say something but the Manager only walked closer till he was directly in front of the lion demon.

“Also you speak as if your credibility and ability to get any story you wish published is absolute.” He folded his hands in front of himself. “It is not.” Tripitaka then inclined his head towards Mei. “The Long family has very close ties, if not owns or is partial owner in most media. If you go to any of their businesses you will be rejected I can assume almost instantly. The same will happen with any reputable media organization as they will look up if you have a non-disclosure agreement with the legal archives and if they find you do. Which,” Tripitaka leaned closer “you do.” He reminded, making Azure growl in response. “They will refuse to publish your story for fear of being caught in any litigation, especially involving one of my clients. I’m very litigious.”

“You're lying!” Azure accused, his body taking on a more agitated appearance.

“I have no reason to lie.” The bald man said in a blaise tone. “And of the two of us, I have more credibility as I have never been involved in racketeering.”

Azure and everyone looked stunned. Azure glanced back at the fireplace and the notes were indeed burnt up so how did Tripitaka find this out?! Unbeknownst to him, everyone else was pondering the same thing.

“H-how did you…?” Azure tried to ask but the bald man’s utter emotionless face was throwing him.

Tripitaka did not have any expression on his face. Nothing the lion demon could gleam, to hint at how he should play this. What he should do to try and salvage this situation. The manager only looked as if he was reporting the weather or explaining stocks; not dismantling Azure’s plans and any back up plans he could try and think to use.

“If you believed you would be able to run this industry similar to how you and your brothers ran your gang in Oklahoma, you are mistaken.”

Wukong looked flabbergasted behind his friend. “You’re a gang leader?!” The monkey then moved forward and put a hand on Tripitaka’s shoulder. “You knew and didn’t think to tell me that?!”

“I will explain later.” The Managers said in his no nonsense tone. Wukong’s mouth twisted but he let go only to move to stand next to the bald man.

“Back to the matter at hand.” Tripitaka glanced around at everyone then turned his attention back to Azure. “You hold no power.” Azure growled like a cornered animal, “And if you try to move against Wukong, Macaque, or anyone else here. I will ensure you and your brothers not only never work in this industry, but I will be reporting you all for extortion, violation of an NDA, and harassment.” The lion paled. “Extortion alone is a three year felony and I can prove you’ve already committed that.”

“I haven’t done anything!” Azure tried to sound confident but it came out weak.

“Yes you have.” The bald man nodded in affirmation.

“And!” Pigsy spoke up glaring at the lion demon. “You broke in here and threatened us!”

“What!”

Azure gasped and fell back as a claw grazed his whiskers. Startled, he focused on Wukong and flinched back in fear at the rage written across the monkey’s face. Tripitaka was bodily holding a furious Wukong back. In fact, if Tripitaka had not reacted as fast as he did, Azure was sure Wukong would have been able to reach him.

“Let go!” Wukong barked at Tripitaka before turning back to Azure and pushing to move closer to him, making the human slide back with his strength. “You threatened my kids?!”

“Yeah, he tried to blackmail us!” Bai He encouraged pointing an accusing finger at the blue lion.

“I-” Azure tried but was cut off.

“It would be best to apologize.” Sandy said, folding his arms. “Or stay silent.”

“I-I was the one that was attacked!” Azure gestured towards his face where Tang had punched him. “If anything I could call for assault!”

“You broke in and wouldn’t leave!” Pigsy rebutted. “We told you to get and you didn’t!”

“Plus you really want everyone to know you,” Mei pointed at Azure “lost a fight to him?” Mei thumbed Tang with an expression that screamed incredulity. Tang looked insulted at Mei’s tone and spluttered a “rude” in his own defense.

“Pathetic.” Red Son spat just to rub salt in the wound.

Azure looked as caged as he felt. His mind buzzed with half thought out plans but kept hitting a wall. He was only broken out of his mind by Wukong’s angry growl.

“Get out!”

Azure tried to say something, anything! But Wukong’s expression was pure anger, and the vice-like grip his manager had on him seemed to be the only thing truly stopping the golden monkey from moving forward and doing something rash against the blue lion.

“Be advised.” Tripitaka spoke up, as he worked to pull Wukong further back so that Azure had a clear exit. “I will be watching you and your brothers very closely going forward and will not hesitate to take action if I sense anything immoral.”

Azure hung his head in defeat, the fight leaving him as he had no choice but to face his defeat. Wukong and Tripitaka followed the lion to the door in order to make sure he had left. Standing on the porch the two watched his car drive off.

Tripitaka nodded at the sight, “I’ll hire security detail to patrol the perimeter for a time just to be safe.”

He turned to Wukong however the golden monkey did not look pleased, reassured, or anything other than angry. Abruptly the golden monkey did an about face and briskly walked back inside. Leaving the bald man to follow after him.

Inside the sitting room, after Azure had been escorted out the adults reached for the kids. The teenagers and 11 year old were bombarded by “are you ok” questions from all three adults in their various ways. That was quickly followed with the adults cursing Azure and saying how they couldn’t believe him and how they were all happy to be rid of him.

“Kids!” Wukong breathed, making a beeline to MK and Bai He who also ran to meet their Dad. Wukong immediately crushed them into his embrace.

“Are you ok Dad?” MK asked, pulling away only for Wukong to give the teen an incredulous look.

“Am I ok?! I’m more worried about you two!” He ruffled MK’s hair with his tail.

“You two will have security follow you for a time, just to be on the safe side.” Tripitaka said as he rejoined the group.

“Good idea.” Pigsy muttered.

Wukong only gave a glare over his shoulder at his friend. “You!” He growled, and Tripitaka only raised an eyebrow at the anger directed at him. “How could you not tell me I was dating a gang leader?!” The manager's face bloomed into one of understanding as Wukong continued. “How could you let me have him around my kids?!”

The others in the room muttered agreements while folding their arms and giving the bald man affronted and curious looks. The manager only held up his arms and bowed his head a bit.

“Because I did not know until recently.”

“How recent?” Pigsy clipped gruffly.

Tripitaka looked at Wukong who had turned fully to face him. “Remember I had a call come in on my car phone when we arrived?” Wukong gave a nod. “It was Macaque.”

Wukong boggled, even taking a step back in shock. Behind him Pigsy also looked floored while MK and Bai He smiled at the news.

“Macaque, called you?” Wukong stressed as if this was an inconceivable event, and truthfully it was. Macaque still did not like Tripitaka, that had been established, yet he called him.

“I was surprised as well.” He laughed and scratched the back of his neck.

“I can’t believe he called you.” Wukong put his hands on his hips. “I’m like in shock!”

“Well I advise you to prepare yourself, because he called me in order to ask I do whatever it takes to protect you,” Tripitaka stressed with a meaningful look that went over the others heads but not Wukong, whose heart did a small jump. “And your children.” The bald man then scanned the room, “Macaque explained everything to me and asked me to step in.” He then tilted his head. “I was planning to regardless after being given all the new information but he was very worried when I mentioned Azure was here and that the front door appeared to be locked.” At this Tripitaka zeroed back in on Wukong. “Wukong, why do you not have keys to your front door? I have keys to your house. You should carry copies of your keys.”

However Wukong couldn’t respond. His brain was stuck on the fact that Macaque had called Tripitaka for him. Macaque had asked Tripitaka to step into their lives, something he abhorred, for him. His tail wagged as his thoughts swirled around these revelations.

Meanwhile MK and Bai He had just finished explaining that they had locked the front door but forgot to lock any of the other doors. Pigsy and Tripitaka then lectured how going forward the doors will need to be locked just until some time has passed and their security was assured.

“Also,” Tripitaka turned to Red Son. “Your mother is on her way here as well.”

“What?!” Red Son erupted.

“Macaque called her in order to get on a flight and informed her of everything as well.” Red Son groaned as the bald man explained. “So Macaque seemed to think she was flying over here as well.” He turned to Wukong, who had seemed to finally blink back into reality. “You have enough rooms to accommodate everyone.”

At the nod from Wukong everyone lapsed into a moment of silence, which was promptly broken by Mei bursting into laughter.

“I can’t believe you dumped him!” She laughed, holding her stomach as she recalled everything. “All that bullshit from him and the research we did! Just for you to walk in and dump his ass!”

MK joined in on her laughter, “Yeah! Guess we should have talked with Dad.” He joked but it immediately fell flat when Father and son met eyes. “Dad…”

“...I’ll just admit it.” Wukong said, stepping back from both Bai He and MK. “I am still really confused about this whole thing.” He threw his arms up. “Bai He you ditched me to hang out with Azure a few days ago and MK I thought you liked him! But there was research?” He looked over at everyone and upon seeing them all with their own tinges of guilt, a rock settled in his stomach. “You all knew about this?”

“Not at first!” MK rushed. “But when we found out we didn’t want to hurt you.” MK reached for his Dad and grabbed his hand, in a gesture he hoped was reassuring.

“Found out what?”

“That Azure’s a liar!” Bai He added grabbing her Dad’s other hand. Her face then shuttered as she gipped his hand. “I felt so stupid for not noticing.”

“Oh Honey.” Wukong pulled the young girl closer into a half hug and gestured for MK to join them for another group hug. “I’m sorry! You guys should never have been in anything close to this situation!”

“To be fair,” Pigsy broke in. “You told Azure to stay away but he pushed in. So you did try.”

Wukong gave a halfhearted smile at the reassurance. However he still didn’t fully understand everything that had occurred so he couldn’t wash himself from the guilt that he brought this upon his family. However his curiosity and understanding of everything was secondary. Reassuring his children came first.

“I will admit I am jealous that only you got to punch him.” Red Son admitted looking over at Tang.

“How did it feel?!” Mei jumped in. “I wish I got to deck him! It would have been so satisfying.”

Tripitaka came up to Wukong while the rest of the group talked. “I’m going to make some calls. I’m going to assign Erlang to be Azure, Peng, and Yellow Tusks manager. I’m also signing Azure up for anger management as it appears to be sorely needed.”

“Can you do that?” Sandy asked.

“You kidding?” Wukong threw a grin at the water demon. “Tripitaka is the only reason I got this far. He has like an insane amount of power, he’s one of the top executives in the entertainment industry worldwide! Everyone wants a piece of Tripitaka!” Wukong held his arms up to show a large quantity before turning back to his friend. “Erlang will have his hands full.”

“I’ll make sure he oversees every aspect of those three’s lives. He’ll be able to handle them.” He reassured.

“Oh I don’t doubt it.” Wukong chuckled as he reminisced on his previous meetings with Erlang. Saying the man ran a tight ship was an understatement, and his regime for his clients was nothing to scoff at. He would keep the three in line.

Chapter End Notes

Let’s not forget Tripitaka is the protagonist in jttw so of course he comes in and saves the day! In both ways! He helped Wukong recognize his feelings and took out the trash!

Also anyone get the metaphor with the first anniversary gifts??

It also irked me that the only reason for the breakup in the Parent Trap was because of the ultimatum, I get it helps with the the movie and such, but like dude you should have broken up with her when you realized you still were into your ex.

So here’s the bad news, the next chapters are all the emotional ones which I won’t lie I am struggling with. I don’t want to rush things but I also do haha so I’ve written and re-written it like 4 times already cause it wasn’t flowing. So please be patient folks next few chapters will be a bit.

Chapter 19: Drunk Reunion

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Wukong was sitting outside on the patio attached to the kitchen, at his feet was an opened bottle of baijiu. It was pitch black except for the porch lights and the moon. Then he saw the car lights of a taxi pull up towards the house. He let out a heavy huff of breath and with a groan lifted himself up and walked towards the balcony near the front door.

The taxi pulled up close to the door and behind all the cars and Mei’s motorcycle. Macaque leapt out of the taxi with his carryon in tow. He looked to have literally thrown money at the poor driver who couldn’t get a word out to save their life. Wukong chuckled at the sight and that visibly startled the dark monkey.

“Wukong?” Macaque’s tone sounded worried and hesitant but in Wukong’s current state he ignored that and gave a cheery wave.

“Yup!” He popped the P with a laugh. “The doors are locked so you’ll have to climb up like I did.”

“Can’t you just unlock the front door?”

“But that would mean walking all the way over there!” The golden monkey whined like a child.

“Really?” Macaque moved to be beneath the golden monkey.

“Oh what?” Wukong grinned and meant to lean on the banister but missed and fumbled for a bit before righting himself. “What?” He asked again once he was finally leaning on the banister properly, “Can Macaque no longer climb?” He teased, oblivious to Macaque’s growing smile as he observed the clearly drunk monkey. “All that city life make you soft?”

“Soft?”

“Yeah!” Wukong continued. “Bet you can’t climb! An-And I did it with my hands full too so you can’t use that excu-” He hiccuped but continued on as if he hadn’t “excuse!”

“Wukong, you need to move.” Macaque said with clear amusement in his tone.

Blinking the golden monkey realized that the ebony monkey was much closer than before. He seemed to be hanging on the balcony's railings. Wukong blinked hard then looked at Macaque then at the ground.

“Oh.”

Wukong stumbled back to his chair and flopped back into it. Looking up he saw Macaque lift himself over the railing and fully onto the patio. Scoffing at the ease in Macaque’s movements he used his tail to grab the baijiu at his feet. Macaque meanwhile glanced inside the dark house then back to the golden monkey. Wukong had taken a swig of the bottle and let his head fall back with a content ‘Ah’. Macaque opened his mouth to say something but was cut off by Wukong rolling his head to look at Macaque.

“So you can still climb.” He teased.

“Tch, if you can, so can I.” He gestured a dark arm out “Plus your argument made no sense, you also live in a city.”

Wukong blew a raspberry and took another drink. “Well you,” He pointed a swaying finger at Macaque as the dark monkey cautiously approached and took a seat in one of the available patio chairs. “You missed the party!”

“Party?” The ebony monkey's head tilted then looked at the bottle Wukong was holding. “...How much have you had to drink?”

“Yeah!” Wukong gestured back towards the mansion, ignoring Macaque’s other question. “Iron Fan showed up with DBK, got a lecture from him about who I asso-so-so…ass-so-ci…..who I date!” He declared with a frustrated face at not being able to properly get his words out. Macaque blinked at the golden monkey then glanced from the mansion back to Wukong. “And then their family drama blew up so we had a whole thing!” He emphasized with exaggerated movements.

“What happened with them?” Curiosity colored Macaque’s tone as he wondered at the issues he missed. When he had spoken with Iron Fan it didn’t seem anything was wrong, though they did not talk for long and it’s been years since he actually spoke with either of them.

“You already know.” Wukong waved a hand.

“No, I don’t!”

“Yes you do!”

“How would I know?!” Macaque said in exasperation and frustration.

“Cause it’s from like a bajillion years ago!” The golden monkey fired back, sounding just as exasperated and frustrated. Macaque tilted his head in confusion so Wukong rolled his hands and began to explain. “Remember when Red Son was a baby? Iron Fan and DBK went through that rough patch.”

“Oh!” Realization came over Macaque as he did recall that now. “Right.” He nodded, “Why did that pop back up?”

“Red Son found out about it.”

An ache of empathetic dread coiled in Macaque’s gut. A secret best left in the past falling into your childrens lap. It was a thought he bodily shuddered at, as it was too close to his own situation. Though how close, he wasn’t certain. He couldn’t tell if Azure had revealed what he knew on Macaque or not.

“That’s….” Macaque didn’t know what to say, that had to be terrible for everyone involved.

“It was not fun.” Wukong chimed and took another swig. He then held the bottle over to Macaque who took it.

The ebony monkey gawked at the lack of weight in the bottle before taking a chug of his own. He had already figured out Wukong was drunk; but if he drank all this, he was surprised the monkey was able to speak as coherently as he was!

“This whole night hasn’t been fun.” Wukong mumbled, a pout on his face. He then groaned and rubbed his hands down his face before having them flop sideways. “What a night.” He growled, his pout replaced with frustration and…hurt? It seemed to be there but then in a blink it was gone.

“What happened?”

“You already know.” Wukong said with a side glare aimed at the more sober monkey.

Macaque bit his lip. He did remember the manager saying he was with Wukong when they spoke on the phone. “...you talked with Tripitaka?”

“I can’t believe you talked with Tripitaka!” The super star stressed with an exaggerated look of disbelief.

“What did he tell you?” Macaque was not in the mood for this game of tit-for-tat, he needed to know how much of what he said to Tripitaka was told to Wukong and possibly the others.

“What did you tell him?” Wukong countered, with a gleam in his eye. One that begged for Macaque to fight or challenge him.

“Wukong!” Macaque almost slammed the bottle of baijiu on the ground “Just tell me what he said to you!”

“Why don’t you tell me what you told him?!” Wukong immediately fired back, sitting up straight and glaring at Macaque. “Or are you trying to keep more secrets from me?! Does anyone want to talk with me?! Fill me in on shit?!” He ranted. “Why am I the one left in the dark?! My friends, my kids, my kids friends, and you, all know all this shit! But not one of you, thought to talk to me?!” He pointed towards himself and let out an offended scoff. “So no Mac, you tell me what you told Trip!”

“...Not tonight.”

“WHY?!” Wukong bit back, his hands curling into fists as his frustration and anger rose.

“Because you’re drunk and won’t remember this! And I can only handle saying it once, ok?!” Macaque snapped, anger clear in his own tone as he glared at Wukong hard enough for the golden monkey to flinch. “Not everything is about you! This is about me! And me not being…not…” Macaque ‘tsked’ then folded his arms and seemed to curl into himself more. The walls around the ebony monkey visibly rising.

Silence fell over both monkeys as each needed a minute to get ahold of themselves. Until finally Wukong broke it.

“....that’s fair. I am drunk.”

This was followed by a huff of a laugh from Wukong and a weary sigh from Macaque. The golden monkey then rearranged his sitting position and cracked his knuckles before beginning to tell his tale.

“Well I was in the middle, actually no!” He corrected, “I had just broken up with Azure-”

“What?!”

“Shhhh! Let me finish!” The golden monkey swatted in the direction Macaque was sitting. “I broke up with Azure and he was trying to blackmail me into staying with him?! I don’t know! It was weird! Like how did he think that would actually work?” Wukong gave an incredulous look to Macaque but the ebony monkey was glaring at the air. “But he’s saying how he’s going to use everything he has on,” Wukong waved his arms around to showcase an all encompassing circle. “Everyone! If we don’t keep dating. But then Trip comes in and is all that’s stupid, you're stupid and you’re -OH!” Wukong bodily jolted and turned his entire body to fully face Macaque. “You knew he was a gang leader?!” He bit out angrily. “Why didn’t you tell me that?! You nagged me about horses but thought to keep that to yourself?!” He glared but was met with a matching glare from the dark monkey.

“I only found out today!” Macaque then glanced around at the night sky; knowing his plane didn’t land till after midnight he corrected. “Technically yesterday.”

Wukong processed that then deflated like a balloon and slumped into his chair. He took a few calming breaths, his anger not leaving him but settled into a simmer. He took more breaths as he tried to banish the emotion, afterall he had things to do, he couldn't be angry. That could happen later or never, but definitely not now. Yet nothing worked, he was still angry. Not at Macaque specifically like he was earlier, but at…well a lot of things.

“So yesterday was the day of revelations it seems. Shame I missed it.” Macaque gave a forced chuckle that fell flat when Wukong didn’t so much as move. “Bud?”

Wukong grimaced and shook his head no, it was slow at first but then sped up as Wukong’s anger and frustration boiled inside him. He was trying, he was trying so hard to keep it in, but this was just too much. Day of revelations?! Revelations indeed!

“You have no idea. No idea! What happened today!” He screamed looking directly at Macaque but the other monkey didn’t so much as flinch he merely gave Wukong his full attention as the golden monkey continued to rage. “But how could you? You left!” He harshly pointed at the bag by Macaque’s feet. “And I! I! I’m-!” Wukong began to pace as he finally exploded all the anger that had been festering. “I’m so mad! I’m pissed at you!” He viciously pointed at the dark monkey. “I’m pissed at Azure!” He then pointed out towards the driveway. “I’m mad at my friends! And the kids!” He thew both arms towards the mansion. “And me! I’m so mad at me! I did this!” He brought his hands towards his own chest and pounded at it, as if jabbing at himself. “This is my fault!” He screamed, screwing his eyes shut. “I brought him! I caused this!”

Wukong had to blink rapidly as tears poured from his eyes. He wasn’t aware he had been tearing up during his tirade, he had thought he was just angry but that wasn’t just it. He was hurt! Hurt that he brought this into his family. Hurt that his family left him out of everything. He was hurt!

Familiar arms wrapped around him and Wukong bodily flung himself further into them. He was hiccupping as a sob wracked his body. Slowly both monkeys crumbled to the floor, till they were on their knees crouching on the floor. Macaque squeezed Wukong tighter and tucked the man’s face into the juncture between his neck and shoulder, holding him there.

“I messed up so badly Mac.” Wukong cried with hiccups and stutters.

“Shhhh.” Macaque used his nails to claw through Wukong’s mane in comfort as he rubbed another hand up and down the monkey’s back.

He should say it wasn’t Wukong’s fault, however two things held him back. One was that Wukong was beyond drunk at this point and reason would not stick. This was a venting session and Macaque recognized that anything he said would go in and out. He doubted the golden monkey will retain anything at all from this night. And two…Macaque knew this was not Wukong’s fault. He couldn’t have known how much of an asshole and danger Azure was. Yet, Macaque recalled when he left a few days ago. Him wishing Wukong would have recognized that it was not something Macaque would do. And even just now he threw Macaque leaving back at his face. It was perhaps petty. But Macaque wished Wukong had noticed or said something then.

Macaque came out of his own mind when he felt Wukong’s full weight being shifted onto him. “Wukong?” Macaque prodded gently, but with no response he rolled his shoulder so Wukongs face would be visible for a second. That glimpse was all it took to confirm that Wukong had passed out.

Macaque let out a weary sigh as he looked Wukong over, assessing the state the sleeping monkey was in. When he got to Wukong’s tail he gasped at what he saw. On his tail was his tail cuff, the one Wukong had gotten him for their first anniversary.

-

Pigsy woke up and began cooking an enormous breakfast. Afterall it wasn’t just the usual crew but Sandy, Tripitaka, Mei and Red Son’s family that had come over last night and had stayed the night. Therefore the pig demon wanted to make sure he fed everyone, it felt like the one thing he could actually control or help with in this mess.

Yesterday, he had been floored when the kids had all come home and told him everything. Then how everything happened with Azure…the pig demon shook his head at the memories. He did feel vindicated though! He had never trusted Azure and his intuition was once again proven true.

Yet…he let out a sigh as he added some milk to his pancake batter. His thoughts strayed back to Tang and his face screwed up. He felt like he had been played for a fool. Sure, Tang was a good guy and they had been having fun this summer! But Pigsy wasn’t stupid or blind! Tang was good looking, smart, albeit a bit lazy, but overall Tang was a solid 8. He had to deduct points since the man could not cook, and kept a very meek hairstyle.

He still remembers going into shock when the other butler had come out of the pool, sweeping his hair back with an excited grin on his face for the finger food Pigsy had just brought down. Which Pigsy had dropped due to his shock at Tangs modelesque looks. Tang, meanwhile, had whined when the chef wouldn’t let him eat the food from the ground.

“The five second rule is not a thing! Put that down!”

“But it looks so delicious!”

Tang could be a 9 and have anyone in this town if he put half the effort Wukong or Macaque do into his appearance. And Pigsy, well he’s had his past relationships but he knew he wasn’t the best looking or the easiest to get along with. He’s heard Sandy encouragements and talks about self esteem, but Pigsy had a high self esteem in a lot of things. He was confident in his cooking, his work ethic, his morals, and (as proven) his intuition. His looks however…he knew where he stood.

And sure Tang had maybe seemed overly friendly with Pigsy during the summer, wanting to hang out and go with him places. Throwing praise at him and his cooking. But that didn’t mean Tang wanted a relationship with him! That sudden confession had caught him so off guard, but he could smell something wrong with it. And then when Tang explained it, it all made sense.

Tang and him dating was a convenient way to get Tang to stay. And of course the guy felt bad. He knew the man well enough to know he obviously didn’t want to use people. Yet the fact remains, he had lied. His confession was a lie. So all the talks about him trying to say his feelings are genuine, Pigsy couldn’t believe it. Plus, if Pigsy hadn’t followed his gut and kept poking and prodding Tang about the confession, which then led to the actual truth of the fact, the human would have just gone along with this lie. Would have led Pigsy on! Taking him out on dates and acting like he wanted to be together when really he just needed to be here.

When he had confronted Tang about that, the human dodged the answer! Saying he did tell Pigsy so what does that matter. What does it matter?!

Pigsy huffed and angrily whipped the egg mixture of the scrambled eggs. ‘It matters cause we would have been dating based on a lie!’ He thought. And if he could live with a lie like that, then he could lie about anything! Like these so-called genuine feelings.

Yet his gut was telling him something different. During Tang and his forced date, he had to fight to keep the scowl on his face, and to not entertain Tang’s attempts at flattery and flirtation. During all their talks, where the handsome human tried over and over to explain himself, he had to firmly grip his anger and offense, lest it slip away from him. Sandy had given him such a disappointed look, but he had managed to dodge talking with his friend. No doubt the big blue man would lecture him about being too stubborn and-

“Quite the spread my friend!”

Pigsy screamed and on reflex he threw the spatula he had been holding at the voice. However it was easily caught by the blue water demon, who then turned it back around and offered it back to Pigsy.

“I uh…” Sandy looked at all the dishes currently being cooked or already done but not yet plated. “I take it you have some things on your mind?”

Pigsy snorted, “Who wouldn’t after that mess.” Then he finally zoned back into the present to actually look at all the items he had prepared in his stress cooking. There were a few pancakes already piled up, with more sizzling on the flat top. Two different omelets were in the middle of being made, and there was already a bowl of scrambled eggs done. He had rice cooking and glancing at the blender, he would guess he has some crepe batter cooling in the fridge.

“Want me to lend a hand?”

“No, I’ve got this.” The pig demon waved his friend towards one of the island chairs. “Plus I know you’re just waiting for an opening to talk to me about everything.”

“Would this be that opening?” Sandy tilted his head to the side and the chef only huffed at him. “We could always talk after my morning run!” Sandy offered cheerfully.

“Just…get it over with.” Pigsys relented while he continued to cook, missing the perturbed look Sandy gave his back. “Before the whole house wakes up.” He grumbled, clearly grumpy about being cornered in his own kitchen and forced to confront the very feelings he was ruminating about.

“Well I’m still in shock over all that happened yesterday!” Sandy confessed as he leaned bodily on the counter. “That Azure is dangerous! I don’t think we should celebrate so soon. Tripitaka does have a lot of power and pull but I’m still worried.”

“Agreed.”

“That’s why I’m going to have him closely monitored.” Tripitaka said, in lieu of greeting.

Pigsy and Sandy turned to see the bald man looking ready for the day. He was in the same clothes from last night which he seemed displeased by.

“Morning Tripitaka!” Sandy greeted with a smile and Pigsy gave his own greeting, to which the bald man nodded at each of them. He then took a seat next to Sandy at the island.

‘Great, again Trip gets a front row seat to my love life.’ the pig demon groaned as he moved to fetch the crepe batter from the fridge. He was just waiting for the rug to be pulled out from under him and for Sandy to change topics to him and Tang.

“Desperate and dangerous people should not be underestimated.” Tripitaka intoned to which Sandy nodded. “I’ve already screened a few security teams and will make a selection today. Sandy it may be best to stay close in case they target you since you do not live here.”
