“Thanks but I think I’ll be alright. I’d hate to intrude.” Sandy rubbed the back of his head. “They all have so much going on. Last thing they need is me just wandering around.” He laughed good naturedly and Tripitaka smiled at the blue giant.

“On the contrary, your assistance and assurance of your safety may be just what they need at this time, but I will leave that decision to you.” Tripitaka bowed his head, then turned his attention to the bustling chef. “How are you faring Pigsy?”

“‘M good.” He muttered “I want to wring Azure’s neck for what he put the kiddos through. They’ll have nightmares for sure.” He growled and behind him Sandy nodded sadly in agreement.

“So other than that,” Sandy prodded “Are you feeling different about uh anything?” Pigsy threw the blue water demon a glare over his shoulder. Next to him Tripitaka raised an eyebrow and glanced between the two in confusion.

“Knew you’d come round to it at some point.” Pigsy muttered bitterly as he platted more food. “And no it changes nothing!”

“Apologies, I’m unsure what you both are talking about.” The bald man said, looking between the two.

Sandy was pouting at Pigsy who was glaring back at him with a hand on his hip and the other waving his spatula at the man. “This guy won’t leave well enough alone.” He accuses.

Sandy only pouted harder but leaned forward in earnest. “I think you two would be good for each other if you gave him a shot.” The blue water demon then got a mischievous glint in his eyes and teased, “‘Sides I saw you going to protect him from Azure yesterday, and I saw your face when Tang punched him.”

Pigsy spluttered as his face heated up. “You! You saw nothing!”

Sandy let out a teasing “oh-ho!” and held his fingertips up to his mouth.

“Tang? Macaque’s butler?” Tripitaka questioned “I was not aware you two were in a relationship.”

“We’re not!” Pigsy immediately growled.

Tripitaka seemed to be doing mental math in his head from the look of concentration on his face. Till eventually he shook his head and rubbed the space between his eyebrows.

“Look!” Pigsy turned back and began ensuring everything was platted and began moving the dishes and kitchen equipment towards the sink. “The guy needed an excuse to stay and faked liking me to get that.” Sandy went to say something but was silenced by a fierce glare from Pigsy. “Don’t even start on the whole, he does like me bullshit.” He spat. “You don’t start a relationship on a lie if you really like them.”

“But he explained his reasoning!” Sandy tried to argue for the glasses wearing man.

However before Pigsy could fire a response back Tripitaka spoke up. “Well I imagine it won’t be an issue for much longer.” Sandy and Pigsy immediately gave the man their full attention.

“Meaning?” Pigsy barked, not liking the implication of those words.

“I do not know what the future holds for sure.” The human disclaimed with a glance back towards the kitchens entrance, “But Tang, Macaque, and Bai He do live in New York. I imagine they will all move back there.”

“You said Macaque was on his way over here yesterday!” Pigsy said in an accusatory tone.

The human nodded in agreement. “Yes and I assume he’s here now. However, that doesn’t change the fact their home is in New York.”

“Oh.” Sandy’s body deflated.

Pigsy blinked and rocked back on his feet. He bit his lip then scowled. “I guess that’s correct. They’ll have to head home…eventually.”

“Indeed.” Tripitaka agreed.

“Guess you won’t have to deal with Tang for much longer then.” Pigsy looked towards Sandy and visibly startled at the serious and weighted look the man gave him. “You can cease invalidating his feelings because of your own insecurities and past.” The pig demon scrowled and went to retort but Sandy stood up. “I know what you’re about to say Pigsy, you’ve said it to me before. I don’t agree. MK, Wukong, and myself all saw what you refuse to see. Even when the poor guy has bared himself to you.” Sandy shook his head in disappointment, a heavy silence fell over the trio before the water demon left citing “I’m going to go on my morning run before everyone wakes up.”

The silence remained heavy even after the water demon’s departure. Tripitaka looked at Pigsy who was scowling at the floor and bit his lip in a show of awkwardness and feeling out of place.

“I’m going to call the security companies.” The human said, moving to get up. However when Pigsy didn’t so much as budge he cleared his throat. “Perhaps,” Pigsy looked over to the man. “Before he leaves, you should clear the air? Lack of proper communication was an issue that other time.” He stressed and then gave an awkward smile. “I don’t want you to make a similar mistake due to the same issue.”

Chapter End Notes

Chapter 20: A Nice Bonus

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

“Please tell me you have coffee ready.” Tang practically begged as he came into the kitchen. The man then boggled at the full spread of food. “Oh...uh nevermind.” His eyes danced at the various arrays of breakfast items. “I see you were busy.”

“I got coffee.” Pigsy said gruffly and held up a steaming mug.

After Tang took the mug Pigsy went back to wiping down the counter and putting away the various ingredients he no longer needed. Tang, meanwhile took a seat on the kitchen’s island and let his eyes dance around all the options.

“Don’t tell me I have to wait for everyone to be up. MK, Mei, and Red Son didn’t go down till so late!” He bemoaned. “They’ll sleep till noon.”

Pigsy huffed but gave a go ahead gesture while continuing to put away items in the pantry. Tang’s eyes lit up as he grabbed various items, excited at all the delicious food choices. As he took bites of each item he kept sending words of praise to the chef. Saying how it was so delicious, it melted in my mouth, the flavors compliment each other so well. Finally Pigsy couldn’t take it and snapped.

“Alright enough already!” He barked, visibly startling the glasses wearing man. Tang jolted and the forkful or omelet flew to the floor. “You can stop with all the compliments and flattery! Shows over! Azure’s gone!”

“...What?” Tang said slowly with an undertone of offense.

“The compliments and over the top…” The pig demon waved his hand. “You can stop.”

Tang’s face took on a hard edge as he set his silverware down. He stared Pigsy down then grabbed the coffee mug and slowly raised it. “I’m going to drink my coffee and then we’re having a discussion.”

And Pigsy just grumbled and gave a huff of annoyance. But Tang did not look away. The whole time he drank from his mug he stared the pig demon down. It unnerved the chef enough to make him fidget in place, much as he tried to hide it.

True to his word, once he set the mug down Tang clasped his hands and leaned forward. “I am not faking anything. The only lie I told you, was a lie of omission on why I needed to stay here at that moment.” Pigsy scoffed and Tang twitched but only took a calming breath before continuing. “You cook the best food I’ve ever tasted and I’m genuinely impressed and enjoy it. If you don’t like me complimenting it, then just tune me out! I advocate for historic monuments, I'm used to being tuned out and ignored.” That got a surprised laugh from the pig demon but Tang kept his serious face. “Look…” Here Tang deflated and took his glasses off to wipe them with his shirt.

“I got the message. You aren’t interested in me. That’s fine.” He put his glasses back on but avoided looking at the chef.

“It’s…” Pigsy started then let out a groan as he dragged a hand down his face. “Ok look! We both know a guy like you and a guy like me are just…not a thing!”

“You…” Tang looked over to the pig demon that was glaring off to the side. “You have self esteem issues?” He couldn't help but ask rather untactfully.

“It’s not self-!” Pigsy began to rant, throwing his arms up in the air. “Look it’s just-!” However upon looking at Tang, and how confused but earnest the human looked he paused. “Aw Hell.” He groaned. “You already got a preview thanks to that asshole Azure.”

Tang mentally circled back to what Azure said he had on Pigsy. Something about eloping with a cooking star. He bantered around how that could correlate to them now; perhaps a bad break up that shattered Pigsy’s confidence? Or was it so good that no one could replace that person? While he was thinking of different scenarios Pigsy moved to take a seat next to the human. Tang only focused back in on him after a weighted sigh escaped the man.

“See cooking stars usually buy or credit other chefs' creations and feature them on their show. They can’t come up with new creations everyday. That’d be a full time job in itself. Nevermind the filming and prepping.” Pigsy explained, “Chang'e” He sighed the name, and Tang’s heart throbbed at the wistful tone the pig demon used. “She’s so talented. She actually did come up with a lot of the dishes she showed. But other than that she had chefs in the background.” The pig demon gave a half smile at the memory. “I was one of those background chefs.” The pig then nodded his head towards the hallway. “Tripitaka actually got me in. It was great, I got to cook and come up with all these different dishes! And I could experiment to my hearts content! It was one of the best jobs I’ve ever had.”

“Sounds like it.” Tang said, trying to sound kind but he knew it came out jilted sounding.

“Yeah well, I was the longest lasting background chef. Chang'e would cook with me when she could. Or she’d improve something I didn’t even think of!” He complimented and Tang swallowed hard as the scenario of this Chang'e being too good to replace was becoming more and more solid. “We’d hang out after hours too.” Pigsy began to sound, not bitter but no longer melancholy. “Used to do karaoke all the time after a hard day.” Pigsy’s mouth set into a line. “Then I…messed up.” He said as if it pained him to admit.

Tang quirked an eyebrow, “You messed up a dish?”

Pigsy grumbled and gave a look of offense at the mere notion of that. “No!” He then waved a hand into the air. “I misinterpreted what Chang'e and I were. I had thought…I stupidly assumed…” The pig demon took in a breath to gather courage, and Tang put a hand on his shoulder in support. Pigsy glanced at the hand then Tang’s face and seemed to shrink inwards again.

“Did she break up with you?” Tang asked gently.

Pigsy deflated and dropped his face into one hand. “To break up we’d need to be in a relationship.”

The human blinked at the pig in confusion and mentally went over everything Pigsy had told him. “What do you mean?”

“Ugh!” Pigsy groaned. “I was an idiot and thought we were dating! I never even asked, just assumed since we hung out and everything. I was such an ass!” He berated himself with a groan.

“You were never dating?” Tang couldn't help but blurt out. He immediately wished he could take it back as Pigsy deflated even more.

“I assumed we were. But I never brought it up with her! We never kissed, but her husband had died only a bit ago so I thought she was just being guarded!” He groaned in embarrassment. “I made a complete ass out of myself with a whole stupid proposal I did while we were filming.”

“You proposed?!” Tang gawked.

“Luckily Tripitaka was there that day.” He sounded so low and defeated as he explained. “He helped cover the whole thing up. Made sure my shame never got out and got me hired here shortly after.” Tang put a hand on the pig demon’s back as the man buried his face in his hands. “They way Chang'e looked at me and what everyone said….” He trailed off sounding defeated.

“What did they say?”

“Nothing that wasn’t true.” The pig demon grumbled, making Tang narrow his eyes. “Chang'e and you are so far out of my league. People like you guys don’t belong with a gruff and scruffy pig demon like myself-”

“That’s bullshit!” Tang practically screamed. He then grabbed Pigsys face and practically threw the man’s neck out with the force he used to bodily turn the chef to face him. “You may have messed up with this Chang'e but that’s because you made assumptions and didn’t talk with her!”

“Thanks for that.” Pigsy grumbled as he tried to right himself so he didn’t get kink in his neck.

“If this is why you want to keep doubting what I’ve been telling you then you need to stop listening to ghosts of the past and listen to me instead!” He yanked the pig closer until their faces were very close. “I like you alot! I want to date you!”

Pigsy wrestled himself free with a grumble and Tang’s stern face melted into a more open and nervous one. When Pigsy righted himself and turned to him, he paused in his movements seeing Tang twirl his hands together in a show of nerves.

“I know I wasn’t fully upfront about everything in the beginning. So if you really can’t get past that then…” Tang’s shoulders slumped. “Then that’s ok. I’ll get over you, I just need a straight answer if this,” He gestured between them “has any potential or not.”

Pigsy looked at Tang and bit his lip, looking nervous and agitated at the same time. “I’m not great at conversation.” He muttered and Tang’s nerves lessened as he gave an encouraging smile.

“We didn’t have any issues at the meat market or any of the other times we’ve been alone.” He reassured. “You know before all this madness really picked up.”

“Didn’t know you had those interests in me then.” The pig demon muttered.

“Guess you have something in common with this Chang'e then.” Tang joked. It fell flat as Pigsy slowly turned to look fully at Tang to give the human a flat and unimpressed stare. Tang let out a nervous laugh. “Eh…bad joke?”

“A bit tasteless.” Pigsy teased but gave a small smile.

“So does this mean…?” Tang asked, extremely hopeful.

The pig waved a hand at the human and turned to go back to putting the ingredients away and cleaning. “Let’s start small, I don’t want to be too far from the kids.”

Tang beamed and practically bounced back to his seat “Agreed! Plus then you can cook us food! That’ll be better than going out anywhere.”

—-

‘Damn my internal clock!’ Wukong thought in frustration as his body woke up, much to his minds frustration. He resisted the urge to stretch and tried to will himself to fall back asleep but to no avail. Though truthfully his mouth felt so slimy he did have a strong urge to get water to at least rinse it.

However upon cracking his eyes open the monkey winced then immediately squeezed his eyes shut and turned to curl into himself. Even though the room was dark, the small amount of light illuminating the room from under the door frame and through the cracks in the window shades was enough to make Wukong’s eyes sting.

“How much did I drink last night?” He muttered then groaned and finally gave into his urge to stretch. Bowing his back and pushing both arms on either side of himself. Only to violently flinch and then wince again when his one hand hit someone (he distinctly felt his fingertips brush some hair).

“Who the-?!” Wukong ripped the blankets down to the end of the bed in his haste to figure out who had invaded his bed.

He was met with a bleary eyed ebony monkey, who was forcing himself to sit up using his elbows. “What happened? What happened?” Macaque slurred as he furiously tried to blink the sleep from his eyes. He was able to come back into focus when he heard Wukong give a retching sound.

“Oh! SHIT!” Macaque cursed, then quickly grabbed the bowl he had placed on the night stand and quick as a flash, shoved it at Wukong. The golden monkey eagerly grabbed the bowl and put his face in it to catch all his vomit. “You good bud?”

Wukong only retched some more and the sounds of more throw up followed making Macaque’s face fall into one of disgust. “Yuck.”

Wukong lifted his face and glared at the dark monkey. “Shut up.” He mumbled.

“Give it here.” Macaque then took the bowl to the on-suite bathroom to dump and rinse it.

Meanwhile Wukong laid himself back on the bed and moaned in pain. “I moved too quickly. Ugh the room is spinning!”

“I take it you don’t remember challenging me to climb up on the terrace?” The ebony monkey called from the bathroom.

Wukong gripped his head in pain and threw a glare towards the bathroom. “I don’t even remember seeing you yesterday.” He then noticed a tall glass of water on the nightstand and quickly downed the drink.

“Figured.” Macaque replied as he came back and put the bowl close to Wukong. “Since that was the first thing you said to me when I got here.”

“...what?” Wukong looked so confused but Macaque just waved a hand at him. The golden monkey’s eyes cautiously followed Macaque as the dark monkey went back to his side of the bed and sat against the headboard. “What else did I say yesterday?”

“Not a lot.” He shrugged. “You somewhat told me about you breaking up with Azure and how he tried to blackmail everyone…?” He ended on a question but was met with only Wukong scowling at the memory. “And that DBK and Iron Fan had a rough time because of old issues.”

“Red Son had a rough time.” Wukong corrected.

“I’m sure they didn’t have a picnic either.” Macaque muttered, the sting of old issues being aired to one's own children, hitting too close for his comfort.

“Fair.” Wukong muttered and then let out a groan. “My head is killing me.”

“After how much you drank.” Macaque chuckled. “I’m surprised you’re not still drunk.”

“I’m definitely not since my head is trying to split in two!” He moaned. “It’s too bright.” He threw a glare at the light peeking in from the crack in the curtains. “And loud.” His glare slid over to Macaque who rolled his eyes.

“You’re so whiny.” The ebony monkey teased, though his voice did drop in volume. “Better?”

“Yeah.” Wukong let out a sigh and allowed himself to just lay there, trying to get his bearings. He noted he was still in his clothes from yesterday and let out a small groan at the realization. He glanced over to Macaque who seemed to just be staring into space, probably thinking over whatever Wukong revealed to him yesterday.

“I can’t believe you called Tripitaka.” He muttered, causing Macaque to turn and look at him.

“Yeah well…” Macaque reached up and rubbed the back of his neck. “I wasn’t here and soon as I found out what the kids had uncovered, I knew I needed someone to protect you and the kids.”

“Still…for you that’s….” Wukong rocked his head.

“Don’t get me wrong.” Macaque crossed his arms. “I’m not crazy about him. But I know if anyone can take on a gang leader it’d be him.”

“I mean, to be fair,” Wukong added in an almost petulant tone. “I’m the one that dumped a mob boss.”

“Is he with the mob?!” Macaque asked, loud and alarmed.

Wukong winced at the volume. “He was in a gang right?”

“The mob and some ragtag gang are not the same thing!” Macaque lectured then let out a weighted sigh. “Giving me a heart attack.” He muttered to himself as he ran a hand through his mane.

“I feel like I had several so it’s only fair.” Wukong muttered playfully.

He then thought back to Tripitaka’s words yesterday and reached over to grab Macaque’s hand. Just laying his on top of the darker monkey’s, the tips of his fingers curling slightly over the fingers edge. Macaque zeroed in on the hand on top of his own, he seemed to even hold his breath at it.

“You must have been…” Wukong paused, then squeezed the hand under his own. “So scared when you found out he was here yesterday.”

Macaque let out a rush of air as he thought back to that moment. He had gripped the airport phone so tightly, fear and dread halting his breath but not his mind as he quickly caught Tripitaka up on everything. “When Tripitaka said the front door appeared locked. That’s what really scared me.” He turned his hand and in tandem the two monkeys fingers interlocked and they each gave a squeeze of reassurance. “My mind kept going to the worst case scenarios and I…I swear I was so close to just running back here if the plane took a second longer to begin boarding.” The golden monkey let out a huff of a laugh as he offered a lopsided smile. To which the corner of Macaque’s mouth twitched up in a small half smile. “How about you?” His tone turned to one of teasing. “Breaking up with a gang leader? That had to be a story.”

Wukong groaned and flopped onto his back, his hand releasing Macaque’s as he instead pressed the heel of his hands into his eyes. He willed his hangover to lessen so the instant headache that blossomed at that memory wouldn’t hurt so much.

“It was something!” He growled.

Macaque waited but when nothing else came his face dropped to one of impatience. “What, no fun unless you were actually there?”

“Fun?!”

“Give me details Wukong!” Macaque poked the golden monkey's face with his tail.

“I can’t tell you everything if I tried! I was left out of most of the important stuff apparently!” He said, not without bitterness. Yet hidden behind that bitterness was the hurt he mentioned yesterday; Macaque only recognized it now that he knew to look for it and his face softened in sympathy.

“Wukong.” He said evenly, gaining Wukong’s focus. Holding it, Macaque said patiently “I wasn’t here. I worried the entire plane ride and drive here.” Wukong’s eyes softened and he gently pet the ebony tail still near his head. “I need you to tell me what happened. What did Azure say? Did he do anything to the kids?”

Letting out a loud sigh Wukong sat up and situated himself to sit against the headboard of the bed. Macaque joined him and sat next to the golden monkey, anxiously waiting to hear what had all transpired.

“I had just had a uh…” He glanced at Macaque who was giving him his full undivided attention, Wukong coughed and turned to try and hide a blush that rose up on his cheeks. “A rather enlightening day with Tripitaka.”

“Enlightening?” Macaque mouthed silently in question.

“So I was planning on breaking up with Azure privately but when I was thinking of what to say I ran into him and everyone in the sitting room.”

“Which one?”

“The one with the massive fireplace on the main floor.” Macaque nodded so Wukong continued. “I tried to talk with him privately but he was being really stubborn about leaving and then Mei figured out I was trying to talk to him to break up and asked that in front of everyone. And then to my utter confusion everyone started to cheer!” He rubbed his forehead at the memory. “Bai He had just ditched me for Azure but her and everyone were celebrating and saying how Azure had lost….I still don’t fully get how everything fits.” He said in exasperation. “Then Azure starts saying how we’re not breaking up till he gets his due?! And then he tries to blackmail me into staying with him? I don’t get how he even thought that would work!

“He was desperate.” Macaque growled.

“Yeah well he threatened to sell stories about all of us and then Tripitaka came in and shut that idea down. But he also dropped the bomb that Azure was a gang leader! Which only I didn’t know about! Apparently everyone else knew!” He jolted slightly when he felt Macaque’s tail wind around his waist. Sharing a look Wukong gave a grateful smile then took a calming breath.

“I assume then Tripitaka kicked him out?”

“Actually Trip had to physically hold me back from assaulting Azure.”

“What? What happened?!”

“Found out he had threatened the kids so..” He shrugged and Macaque only nodded in agreement. Wukong then barked a laugh and nudged Mac with his elbow. “You’ll love this.” The ebony monkey quirked an eyebrow as the golden monkey grinned, “Guess who did punch Azure?”

“...Pigsy?” a negative headshake. “MK?” Another negative. “Red Son?”

“Nope!”

Macaque rolled his eyes and jostled Wukong with the tail still around his waist. “Who?”

“Tang!” The golden monkey grinned.

Macaque’s mouth dropped open in shock, “What?! Tang?!”

“Yeah! In the face too! I guess he punched him before I got there.”

“I wish I got to see that!” Macaque grinned.

They lapped into silence for a time before Wukong sniffed himself and physically recoiled. With a grimace he shuffled out of bed.

“I hate to say this but I need a shower.” He grabbed his head as a headache made itself known. “Or a bath”

“Yeah you do.” Macaque teased, making a face of exaggerated disgust. “You reek!”

Wukong shot him an affronted look. “Ok mister cuddle next to me all night. I can’t be that bad!”

Macaque’s face burst into an embarrassed red as he shot back “I did not! We weren’t-!”

Confident in having the upper hand Wukong gave a smug look and made a grand gesture to the bed Macaque was still in. Macaque’s face went further scarlet as he made stilted and jerky movements in order to remove himself.

“We were - I was just sleeping in our bed!” Macaque shouted as he straightened now standing next to Wukong who blinked owlishly at him. The darker monkey was confused till he went over what he said again and now he was blushing for another reason.

“Our bed?” Wukong parroted in a small almost hopeful voice, a smile slowly growing on his face as Macaque tried to right himself.

“Your bed! I meant, your bed!”

“But it is our bed-”

“We don’t have a bed!” Macaque cut in his tone firm and he slashed a firm hand in the air between the two monkeys.

Wukong took a step forward, “We cou-”

“Wukong!” The dark monkey interrupted, his tone so firm and final it jolted the golden monkey. Macaque’s eyes flashed with an emotion too quick for Wukong to properly catalog, but it may have been regret or hope. Either way, the dark monkey quickly averted his gaze. “You have been through a lot. We both have but you…” he let out a heavy breath and folded his arms. “In the past 24 hours I think we both have aged 50 years.” He let out a stilted chuckle, still avoiding looking in Wukong’s direction. “Let’s…”

“What? Not talk about it?” Wukong confronted, causing Macaque to flinch inwards. “I’ve tried that and it didn’t help at all. In fact I’m positive I made things worse from that!”

“Wukong you-”

“I need to talk about us.” The golden monkey shot back, firm in his conviction.

“There isn’t an us!” Macaque fired back, turning to try and put space between them.

Wukong followed Macaque, only a step or two behind as the dark monkey moved. “That’s what I want to talk about!”

“You're hurt, and were lied to by Azure and our kids! You’re-”

“That doesn’t have anything to do with this!” The dark monkey flinched his arm away from where Wukong had tried to grab him.

“Yes it does! I’m not going to be your rebound!”

“You are not a rebound!” Wukong screamed in exasperation. “You are the only relationship I’ve ever wanted! I mean of course I slept around after our divorce but-”

“Oh of course.” Macaque mocked with an unimpressed scoff.

“See! That!” Wukong jumped in front of Macaque, who still refused to look at him. “You getting jealous just from that proves-”

“What do you want from me?!” The ebony monkey blurted out, his eyes finally meeting Wukong's again. “I’m trying to be the better person here Wukong! I’m trying to be better!” He slapped his chest. “I know we both screwed up our marriage, and I know we both messed up this time! But you just went through hell, we have the kids to think about, and I know you are not ready for a relationship!” He finished, poking the golden monkey hard in the chest.

Wukong’s face immediately morphed into one of pure annoyance and aggravation. “Oh! That’s right! You all know me so well!” He backed up and made a show of throwing his hands up and down, gesturing to all of himself “You all know me well enough that none of you! None of you!” He emphasized pointing at Macaque and then the door. “Feel the need to actually talk with me. Or get my side! No! It’s nice actually! Super!” He bit out, with a mean smile. “I’m so glad you know me more than I know myself!” He yelled in exaggerated and fake thankfulness. “I’m ecstatic that you know, I’m not ready to move on from a relationship I ended because I’m still in love with you!”

“...what?”

“What’s wrong?! I thought you knew me?! I thought you knew everything!” Wukong jeered, stepping into Macaque’s space. The dark monkey took a step back but his face was one of confusion as his mouth opened and closed like a fish out of water.

“Yesterday Tripitaka made me realize I’ve had my head up my own ass about our divorce. I went through all your stuff! All of it!” He stressed, once again invading Macaque’s space. “The lamp, the camping equipment, the damn tablecloths you like but served no purpose! Everything!” He poked the dark monkey in the chest, “And I realized how much I miss you! I’ve missed you! But I worked so hard on erasing you that I…” The steam of his angry and annoyed rant teetered off as his next words came out, emotional and raw.

“… I forgot you.” He took a step back and continued in a more honest and open tone “Forgot all the things we did, the memories, the feelings you made me feel, including mad.” He threw a teasing look to the dark monkey who instinctively returned it with a smirk. “And then you came back and I tried to put you in the box of just tolerate, like a shitty co-worker. But then…we’d have those moments, and I…I think I shoved you away. Then you left again and….” He trailed off but Macaque looked unable to speak, his ears splayed out on the side of his head, clearly eager to hear every word the golden monkey spoke.

Squaring his shoulders Wukong took a breath and grabbed the dark monkey's hands, his grip firm as he felt the ebony monkey's hands shake with small tremors. “Macaque, we aren’t perfect. We fight over stupid shit, we both suck at communication, but…I don’t want perfect. I just don't want to lose you. I can’t! If you leave one more time….”

“Wukong….I…” Macaque truly wasn’t sure what to say. He had a thousand thoughts running through his head, so he tried his best to process them and think of a proper response.

The golden monkey waited as Macaque seemed to be in his own head; and he knew he should give the man some time. He just threw a lot at him, when they had already been through a lot yesterday. But then his eyes caught on the tail cuff on the night stand. Perking up he went to let go of one of Macaque’s hands and felt a flower of hope burst awake in his chest when the dark monkey immediately grabbed his hands, refusing to let go.

“I just wanted to grab that.” He explained quickly, as he inclined his head toward the night stand where the tail cuff lay. Macaque glanced over towards the table in question. “It’s yours and” Wukong tilted his head to the side and gave the ebony monkey a smile. “I want to give it to you. I actually hung your painting up again last night. It’s in-”

“I love you.”

Macaque snorted a laugh at the dumbstruck look on the golden monkey's face. “You’re an idiot. We have shit timing. We’re not good at this.” He used their joined hands to gesture between them. “But I love you…I never stopped loving you.”

“I’m sorry I didn’t call.” Wukong said sincerely as he moved to close the space between them.

“We both messed up.” Macaque released their hands and instead moved his hands to be around Wukong’s neck. The golden monkey reciprocated by winding his arms around the dark monkey's waist. However when he went to lean in for a kiss Macaque leaned back with a genuine look of disgust.

“Absolutely not, your breath does reek and you recently threw up.”

“Are…are you serious?”

“You physically recoiled from your own smell earlier Wukong.” The two monkeys leaned back and the golden monkey threw an arm up in the air.

“I was exaggerating!”

“Smell your breath right now and tell me you’d want to make out with that.”

“I’d always want to make out with you.” Wukong tried to flirt but the dark monkey only playfully shoved him away.

“Yeah, yeah” he broke their embrace and stepped away. “I’ve been here awhile and you haven’t jumped me. You can live through a shower and teeth cleaning.” He teased.

—---

“Morning Bai He.” Tang greeted with a smile as the young girl blinked beerily at him. She still looked half asleep with her hair a mess and face heavy in sleepiness. Sluggishly she took a seat next to her butler, her eyes widening as she took in all the food.

“Kid, you know you can go back to bed.” Pigsy urged but the young girl was too busy looking over all the food.

“Good Morning!” Sandy interrupted as he and the towering figure that was the Demon Bull King made themselves known. Each had a towel wrapped around their shoulders and a good sheen of sweat on their bare chests. “Mr Bull and myself just got done running some laps around the perimeter. Makes you hungry for a good meal!” Sandy smiled as he moved to take a plate.

DBK let out a gruff and short greeting before walking past the group “I’ll be in the pool. If my family wakes up let them know I am out there.”

“Will do.” Sandy smiled as he grabbed a bowl of rice.

“This is like a buffet.” Bai He muttered to herself, still in awe of the full spread in front of her.

“Ah, ah!” Tang corrects. “This is better than a buffet.” He beams at the pig demon who rolls his eyes but takes the compliment.

“What?” The young girl asks but shakes her head in dismissal as she instead grabs a crepe to begin her breakfast.

“Is Baba here? Did you see him?”

Tang glanced around as if looking for a sign of his boss, as Pigsy shook his head in the negative.

“He’s probably sleeping in one of the bedrooms!” Sandy answered kindly.

“Yeah,” Tang agreed. “He wouldn’t have arrived till late yesterday.”

“I know, I tried staying up till he got here but fell asleep.” She muttered as she loaded some chocolate chips onto her crepe. “Can I eat outside?”

Unbeknownst to Bai He, Tang and Pigsy both shared a look with Sandy who nodded at them. Tang then made a look of relief and smiled at the young girl, brushing her hair into some semblance of order.

“Just stay where DBK can see you and that will be fine.”

Bai He looked confused before her face fell in realization at the new normal she’d have to get used to. “Oh, right.”

Shortly after Bai He went downstairs to go eat by the pool, Tripitaka walked back into the kitchen. He rolled his neck as he went to grab himself a cup of tea. He glanced at Pigsy and Tang, noting their relaxed and more gentle atmosphere. He then looked to, hoping he would fill in the blanks but the blue giant only smiled at him. Tripitaka opened his mouth, looking ready to speak before shutting it and shaking his head.

“I hired security detail, they will be here in 2 hours.” All three adults gave the manager their undivided attention as he explained. “They will do a sweep of the property. Suggest camera placement, install a security system in the house, add window locks, and will replace all the door locks. Along with other details that they will go over further in person.”

“Aw.” Sandy pouted as he fished out his personalized key with a picture of kittens on it. “Goodbye my friend.” He said mournfully.

“Don’t worry Sandy.” Tang soothed. “We can personalize our new keys!” He turned to Pigsy “I’m thinking a cicada topped key. What do you think?”

Pigsy looked upset at what Tang assumed was the same reason Bai He looked put out. At the whole situation and the new normal they’d have to get used to. However the pig demon did quirk an eyebrow at the very odd animal choice.

“I like that.” Tripitaka chimed in after taking a pull of his tea.

“I think I’ll do a cat again.” Sandy said in a thoughtful tone, as if this was something he had to think over carefully. However everyone just gave him an unimpressed look.

Pigsy broke the silence with a sarcastic. “What a shock.”

—------

“No way Wukong!” Macaque growled and lifted a bottle of soap as if to throw it. “I will throw this at you!” He warned from his place in the jacuzzi bath tub.

“It’s not like we’re naked naked!” Wukong tried to inch closer but flinched back and held up his hands in surrender when Macaque hefted the bottle for a throw.

“We are naked for monkey demons!” The ebony monkey shot back. “And we are in no way ready to jump to bathing together right now.”

“Then why did you agree to stay if you won’t let me join you.” The golden monkey whined.

“Because you asked me to stay and,” The dark monkey turned and practically melted into the water, only his head floating at the surface. “I have missed this tub.” He practically purred.

“Yeah but I wanted a bath.” The golden monkey mumbled as he kicked the floor.

“You can use the shower,” He pointed towards the spacious stand alone shower in the bathroom “or you can wait till I’m done.” He shrugged.

Wukong rolled his eyes but prepared the shower. As he waited for the water temperature to adjust he grabbed his tooth brush and quickly brushed his teeth. As he did that he watched Macaque continue to melt into the bath. Snorting at the sight.

“You going to fall asleep in there?” He asked around his tooth brush.

“I just might.” Macaque muttered. “I didn’t go to bed till late and someone.” He shot a look at Wukong. “Woke me up early this morning.”

Some time passed as both washed themselves and soon enough both monkeys were finished of their respective bath and shower. Wrapping themselves in bathrobes Macaque went to tease Wukong but stopped. The golden monkey was staring hard at his own reflection, appearing lost in his own head.

“Wukong?” Macaque prodded the monkey demon with a hand on his arm. Wukong blinked and seemed to come back to himself, flashing a reflexive smile before dropping it.

“Sorry, ah.” He went for another smile. “Got distracted. Let’s-” Macaque grabbed his arm more firmly and gently moved him away from the counter so he could put himself between Wukong and the mirror.

“Something’s wrong.” He stated. “What is it?”

“Nothi-” Macaque glared and Wukong dropped the falsities with a groan. “Ok.” he let out a weighted sigh. “Once we go out there, once we leave this room, we have to go into full parent mode. Figure out what all happened with the kids' research, and what Azure did or said to them, and all that fallout.” Macaque could hear the anger and guilt in Wukong’s tone and bit his lip as he thought that over.

“Yeah.”

“Yeah?!” Wukong barked incredulously. “I mean there’s so much! And-”

“There is a lot but I know of…some of it. So it’s not as overwhelming for me.” Wukong turned and glared to the side at this.

Macaque couldn't help but get defensive, crossing his arms and leaning back. Seeing this Wukong waved his hands and tried to placate the ebony monkey.

“I just…I wish… I don’t get-” He tried but couldn’t seem to find the words. Till finally he admitted in a voice aching with pain. “Why didn’t any of you talk with me?!” He pointed towards himself. “Am I that bad? Do you all think I wouldn’t listen?”

“It’s not like that.” Macaque said in a stilted tone; not defensive but still closed off as they neared the subject he did not want to discuss.

Wukong bit his lip but kept eye contact as he spoke. “Azure said he had information on everyone, including you…” He trailed off but Macaque did not nod or give any affirmation other than tightening the grip he had on his own arms. Which Wukong took to mean as confirmation. “That’s why you left.” He stated plainly, like the fact that it was. “You kept saying you had to go, and it was because of him.”

Macaque let out a sigh that instead of relieving him, seemed to only make the dark monkey more stressed and closed off. “Yes.” He clipped out, as he averted his gaze from the golden monkey.

“So he threatened to sell your secret to the press in order to get you to leave.”

“No!” Macaque began pacing as he tucked his head down. “He threatened to tell you and the kids about it!”

“What?” Wukong sounded genuinely confused now. “What could he possibly know about you that I don’t?”

Still pacing Macaque kept looking down as he explained. “It was after the divorce. Azure found out about it and told me he’d keep it a secret, but only if I left for New York that day. No kids.”

Wukong felt like he’d been slapped. He knew it, logically last night he put that together but to have it spelled out so plainly. It was a fresh sting of pain and regret. “I’m sorry!” He said with meaning “I should have-”

“No.” Macaque turned and grabbed Wukong’s hand, slotting their fingers together he gave a reassuring squeeze. “No…I- I could have handled it differently. I should have given a hint or just told you about it but…I couldn’t think! So much happened all at once!”

“I know that feeling.” Wukong joked trying to lighten the mood. It worked as Macaque huffed a laugh and gave the golden monkey a light shove.

“I didn’t want to leave.” Macaque confessed and Wukong’s mouth thinned with guilt. “Wukong-” He tried but the golden monkey backed away.

“I should have known or guessed at something being wrong.” He berated himself, and tried to shake off the dark hand now reaching to grab his elbow.

“And I should have just told you! Or called you afterwards! Or something!” Macaque barked back, stunning the golden monkey. “It’s not always your fault Wukong!”

Wukong could only blink, visibly startled by Macaque's vehement denial of him taking all the blame. So Macaque used Wukong’s shock to his advantage and bodily pushed the towel clad monkey back to the bedroom.

“Just most of the time it is.” He teased with a smirk which broke Wukong out of his stupor to lightly shove Macaque as they fully came out of the bathroom.

“Trust me, all that shit kept going through my mind on the airplane and in New York.” He groaned. “I was a mess.”

“I would have been too.” Wukong then looked pensive and hesitantly asked. “What was it that Azure found out?”

Macaque froze midstep, his entire body seeming to seize up so Wukong immediately back peddled. “You don’t have to tell me!” He rushed.

“No it’s…” He trailed off but couldn't seem to bring himself to look directly at Wukong “I mentioned yesterday that I’d tell you but…”

“Mac.” Wukong now was the one to gently grab the other's hand and intertwine their fingers. He gave a squeeze, “I don’t even remember seeing you last night.” A laugh busted out of Macaque at that.

“I knew you wouldn’t.” Macaque said with a trialing chuckle but the humor left quickly as he continued “I will tell you…eventually.” He deflated and turned so he and Wukong were face to face again. “Now’s just…not the right time.” Wukong gave a small smile of reassurance and reached out to grab the ebony monkey's other hand. Macaque squeezed it and gave a tiny smile back.

Wukong was carrying enough weight and guilt. The ebony monkey didn’t miss when earlier Wukong said he made things worse. And Macaque knew that if he told Wukong everything, the golden monkey would blame himself. And with everything else they had to deal with the moment they left the sanctity that was their room, this was something that could be addressed later.

“Come on, time to face the masses.” Macaque urged as he used their joined hands to pull Wukong towards the bedroom door. The golden monkey continued to drag his feet, literally, but Macaque had an ace up his sleeve.

“Oh by the way,” He remarked as he continued to pull the reluctant monkey along. “We have to make sure the kids follow the one to a bed rule for sleepovers.”

“One to a bed?” Wukong questioned. “Oh! Did you check on the kids last night” The dark monkey nodded. “And let me guess, you saw Mei and MK cuddling?” He made an unimpressed sound and freed one hand to wave it about in a lackadaisy manner. “Don’t worry, they do that all the time.”

“Oh no,” Macaque tutted. “Not MK and Mei.” He stressed the girl's name.

“MK and….?” Wukong stretched the sentence with a confused lilt to his tone. “Red Son?” He asked, then rambled. “I mean Red Son isn’t a cuddler that I know of. Most times he sleeps in a guest room. Maybe yesterday was just too ruff and he needed it…?” But the look on the dark monkey's face was a patronizing one, as if he was humoring Wukong by letting him ramble on. “What?” He finally asked, refusing to move another step till Macaque elaborated.

“Well, you may be right about one thing with your whole speech there.” He waved his tail in Wukong’s face. “They both evidently needed something last night.”

You could pinpoint the moment Wukong caught on to what Macaque was implying. “What.”

“Our son and Red Son were sharing a bed, and from what I could see when I checked in on them, they were not clothed.” The ebony monkey further explained.

“And you left them like that?!” Wukong exploded, now bolting for the door. However Macaque reacted quickly and tried to yank Wukong back. The golden monkey grabbed the doorframe and pulled at it as he was now the one dragging Macaque along, while the dark monkey tried everything to keep him from storming into MK’s room.

“They were passed out! It was almost morning. What would waking them do?!” Macaque defended.

“It makes it easier to yell at them when they’re awake!” Wukong shot back. “And why are you so calm about this?! Our baby boy-!”

“Is a 17 year old teenager!” Macaque growled. “I mean come on Wukong! Think back to when we were that young.”

The golden monkey did pause at that but before the ebony monkey could take a relieved breath they both stiffened and locked eyes as they recalled exactly what they were like at 17.

“We need to buy condoms.” The dark monkey said, tone deadly serious.

“Condoms?!” Wukong looked apoplectic. “Are you serious?!”

“Absolutely! Safe sex-”

“No! What we need is a chastity belt and to kick that Red Son out of our house!” Wukong took off towards MK’s room again, Macaque grabbing at him once more.

—-------

“Between yesterday and this morning this has been,” Mei beamed with unbridled joy. “Such a great summer.” Next to her Bai He giggled, however MK and Red Son glared at her. Both boys still had a blush stained on their cheeks from what they would both agree was the worst and most embarrassing moment in their lives.

Wukong had burst into MK’s room and began to scream a lecture at the two very startled and naked teens. Which drew the attention of the whole house. So then everyone swarmed MK’s room to see what the commotion was. This resulted in Red Son’s parents joining Wukong in his lecture to the boys. Thankfully Macaque and Tripitaka stepped in; a team up no one saw coming. Seriously, even both adults looked perturbed when they found they were working together. They had managed to get everyone to agree to let the boys at least dress before the lectures and discipline went any further.

Everyone had then converged in the main dining room; Sandy, Tang and Pigsy laid all the food out so everyone could eat as they talked. It was the most embarrassing hour of their young lives.

“Right.” Tripitaka spoke, bringing everyone's attention towards himself. “I already told Pigsy, Sandy, and Tang but I hired a security detail. They should be here,” He checked his watch. “In roughly an hour, maybe less. They will do a security run, replace and install locks on the doors and windows. Then they will discuss personal protection details.”

“Oh! Are you guys going to have, like, Daddy DBK or Sandy looking guys follow you everywhere?!” Mei asked in excitement.

“Do not call my father that!” Red Son screeched as the man in question only shook his head at the girl's antics.

“I bet they’ll wear suits all the time like the guys that are there when we’re at events!” MK added, encouraging Mei.

“And sunglasses that they never take off!” Bai He chimed in.

“Glad they’re having fun with this.” Wukong said with a rueful shake of his head as the kids went on and on about the imaginary bodyguards.

“So uh…” Pigsy trailed off in a grumble and put-out tone. “Are you gonna ask Tripitaka to hire security for your place?” He looked at Macaque who quirked an eyebrow but then scowled at the thought.

“I’m not inclined to make this,” He gestured between himself and the bald man. “A regular thing.”

“Besides we’re doing a sweep here right?” Tang added easily. “No need to do more.”

“Well what about…” The pig demon frowned and after 10 seconds of silence grunted out “when you all go back.”

The air around the adults stilled, Tang shared a pained look with Pigsy as Macaque and Wukong glanced at each other. The kids noticed the shift in the air and their conversation ground to a halt. Wukong then cleared his throat, failing to see the ebony monkey’s look that practically begged him to stay quiet.

“Well that’s easy, they’re not going back.” He beamed, looking at Macaque only to wince when he saw the dark monkey glaring at him.

“We aren’t?!”
“You aren’t?!”

Bai He and MK practically shouted with glee as they looked between their parents. Macaque gave another glare of annoyance to Wukong before turning back towards the whole table.

“Well we haven’t discussed that yet.” He tried only for Tripitaka to interrupt him. The bald man slammed his silverware down and glared at the golden monkey.

“You haven’t talked with him-”

“No, we did talk!” Wukong defended, he gestured towards Macaque and himself. “We’re back together!”

“Wukong!” Macaque growled but that was lost in the sea that was everyone else's reactions.

“YOU ARE?!” Everyone shouted, quickly followed by an avalanche of questions.

Wukong leaned back in his chair and turned to give a sheepish look to Macaque, “I’m guessing this is why you were glaring at me.”

“You think?” He fired back and they both snorted and laughed at the craziness.

—--------

“You hired Nezha?!”

“I hired Li Jing and Lady Yin’s security company.” Tripitaka clarified as he continued to review the paperwork Ao Bing (Nezha’s partner in work and life) had handed him.

“Yeah.” The brunette stuck his tongue out at the golden monkey. “So maybe take a chill pill.”

Wukong glared at the tattooed young man, who was currently standing in one of their many sitting rooms. Ao Bing was sitting between Tripitaka and Macaque; both of whom were going over the same paper work. Macaque didn’t want Tripitaka just deciding things so he had his own copy to review, it was his condition he had given before agreeing to hire them on fully. So the blue haired man was going over all their services in detail with the two as Nezha and Wukong antagonized each other.

“You do remember this is the same brat that embarrassed me on the red carpet right?”

“Which time?” Nezha said, stoking the fire of Wukong’s annoyance.

“You little-” He growled.

“If I may,” Ao Bing interrupted politely. “Nezha and myself were assigned since we already have a relationship with MK and yourself. We’re hoping that makes it an easier transition for any privacy they feel they are losing. I know MK from meeting him with my cousin Mei and both of you from when we’d all attend the annual Long Family reunion.”

“Yeah plus MK and I hang out already so now I’ll just be getting paid to do it. So,” He rolled his shoulders, “It won’t be a big adjustment for him.”

“Wha- when do you two hang out?” Wukong questioned, truly confused.

“When he wants to get away from the paparazzi.” The tattooed brunette shrugged. “I helped him out a few times and we bonded. We’re friends.” He stated easily, like it was a common fact.

“You threw paint on me but are friends with my son?” Wukong asked incredulously.

“You were wearing fur, you insensitive ass!” Nezha immediately shot back.

“Enough.” Tripitaka intoned with a voice of one berating children. “The logic is sound. As fun as the children had at breakfast I think they’d find more traditional security guards stifling, wouldn’t you agree?”

Wukong puffed up his cheeks in a pout and instead of answering turned to Macaque and poked him with his tail. “Macaque?”

“Do not make me agree with him twice in one day.” The dark monkey answered in a pinched tone.

“I’ll make a friend out of you yet.” The bald man smiled, immediately earning Macaque’s ire.

“That is not our relationship.” He growled.

“Well I’m gonna do a perimeter sweep.” Nezha drawled.

“Wait,” The dark monkey reached out as if that would stop the man “we haven’t signed off on every-”

“But you will!” The brunette sing-songed back already leaving.

“...ok yeah, he will get on my nerves.” Macaque acquiesced, sharing a look with Wukong.

“But he is right.” The golden monkey growled in a reluctant voice. “So where do we start?”

—----

“Dad and Baba are back together. Tang and Pigsy are together.” Bai He counted off on her fingers before pointing at her brother and Red Son “And you two are together.” She paused for a moment as if processing all that. “That seems like an insane amount of relationships and stuff happening just over this summer break.”

“Quick Bai He, if you have a crush call them. You’re family’s on a roll!” Mei encouraged with a grin.

“You're hilarious.” Mk drawled sarcastically. “Tang and Pigsy I feel like we all knew that was coming.”

“Same with your parents.” Red Son quipped but MK and Bai He looked surprised at him.

“No, that was definitely a surprise.” MK argued and Bai He nodded in agreement with her brother.

“You two need to watch more rom-coms.” Mei said in way of explanation. “Take note Red Boy.” She teased and grinned a shit eating grin at the immediate glare that earned her. “I mean isn’t this what you two planned all along?”

“No!” The siblings denied.

“But it is a nice bonus.” MK added with a smile as he pulled Bai He into a half hug, both beaming with pride at what they accomplished.

Chapter End Notes

Can you tell this was supposed to be broken into multiple chapters?? Lol but I just want to give the people the ending you’ve all patiently waited for! So excuse the jumping around. I had wanted to go into more detail with a few parts but decided against it. Like with Ao Bing and Nezha, meeting the whole gang and yatta yatta. So sorry boys you’re only in for a bit but you made it in! I also had some other things I wanted to add on like Iron Fan and Mac’s reunion, DBK and Wukong both doing the whole “your son corrupted my son!” bit, and Azure being a bitter bitch and telling SWK what he knows about Mac (just to be an ass). But that would drag this on unnecessarily and I feel we’ve reached the end. If you do want to know more feel free to ask me here or on tumblr :)

Also earlier this year I had written a finale and epilogue that was so Hallmark movie cringy I could see the anime pastels and flowers floating in the background of each scene. It was not good haha. So glad I re-wrote it and went with this. I love it! Shadowpeach and Freenoodles relationships still feel very much alive. They’re not perfect, it’s not all sunshine and rainbows and happily ever after, they feel realistic :D at least to me and I’m my main target audience lol

I hope you guys liked it and thought it was worth the wait :D

Chapter 21: Fanart, Memes, and Thanks!

Chapter Summary

I hope this fic made you smile!

So I need to do a special shout out to a few of you who truly, and I mean really truly, are the reason I even kept going with this fic. I was low a few times in my life while this fic was in progress. But Chapter 12 was going to be my last full chapter of this fic and then I was going to post the skeleton plot I had at that point for the remaining story (which doesn’t even match the actual story now so bullet dodged there). I just had no faith in my writing and thought the fic was shit and my work was bad. I mean I know I’m not the best writer but I was just in a mindset of “what’s the point, who even cares about this trash?” And then I remembered these guys, and I thought:
Hey they care. They care enough to comment on practically every chapter so I owe it to them to post Chap 12 and then I’ll apologize when I post the skeleton.
Then chapter 12 freaking blew up with all of you and I just got SUCH a confidence boost! So the full fic being written out was back on.

So, again, from the bottom of my heart I want to thank all of you, thank you so much for reading this fic, commenting, and giving kudos! I genuinely appreciate it!


The rockstars that saved this fic are:

And everyone who joined part way in or chimed in more after Chapter 12 I really really appreciate all of you! You guys continued to give me the confidence to write this story out and post it.
